spectrum at `harmonic=k` plus the frequency-aware phase term): k = 1 carries ~99.9 % of the power, so
Σ_k repeats k = 1 — 0.514 (3 f_amp) / 0.476 (10 f_amp) at a = 0.30.  **The asymmetric k = 1 over-statement is
a real amplitude, not power placed on the wrong harmonic.**

*Which side is right away from k = 1?*  The same Monte Carlo (64 × 4000 periods, recorded to 25 f₀), bands
k·f₀ ± 8–12 f_amp:

| a | k | MC/pnoise | MC/(library orb + phase) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.00 | 0 | 1.47 (floor, ~1e-5 of k=1) | **0.0074** |
| 0.00 | 1 | 1.0028 | 1.0015 |
| 0.00 | 2 | 1.0066 | **3.21** |
| 0.00 | 3 | 1.0312 | **3.70** |
| 0.30 | 0 | 1.10 | 0.0096 |
| 0.30 | 1 | 0.9986 | 0.309 |
| 0.30 | 2 | 1.0099 | 0.396 |
| 0.30 | 3 | 1.0274 | 0.953 |

**pnoise is right at every harmonic; the line-shape model is wrong away from k = 1 even on the SYMMETRIC
orbit** (where k = 1 is exact).  Ruled out: FFT leakage (`floquet_modes` p/q periodic to 1e-15).  Partly
explains it, measured: (i) `oscillator_spectrum(harmonic=k)` keeps only the h = k phase line, and the
h = ±1 line's TAIL at 2f₀ is large — summing all h takes k = 2 from 3.21 to 1.60; (ii) `orbital_spectrum`
keeps `Re(row·C_lhj·row)`, dropping eq (98)'s dispersive `Im c` term, which cancels in the ± pair near a
line (so k = 1 is untouched) but not far from it — restoring it takes k = 2 to 1.39, k = 3 to 1.50.
Neither touches k = 0 (~150× over: the tank inductor shorts node v at DC, which Lorentzian tails do not
know).  **Reading: these are LINE-SHAPE models, valid near the orbit's own harmonic lines; far from them
(DC at a shorted node, a harmonic with |X_k| ≈ 0) a sum of Lorentzian tails is not the broadband response.**
⚠ The hazard it leaves is a plausible wrong answer: `oscillator_spectrum(harmonic=2)` on a symmetric
oscillator returns ~0 where the true density is 5.6e-6 V²/Hz.  **Proposed, not built:** refuse or warn when
the harmonic carries no carrier (|X_k| below a fraction of the signal), as `am_pm` already refuses, and
point at `pnoise`.


## `oscillator_spectrum` is frequency-aware by default (Andreas: "Build it", 2026-09-14 night)

**The item.** E6's phase half: the Lorentzian from `c` uses the DC PPV at every offset, and where part of the
phase response goes through a slow mode (the amplitude mode under AM–PM coupling, or a slow node in the
source's path) it over-states above that mode's corner.

**Prototype first** (`orb/faproto.py`, no source edits), four gates named before running, all passed:
the complex-safe equation-row map of `frequency_aware_ppv(0)` equals `ppv()`'s `samples_eq` to 7e-14 and is
linear to round-off; hostile (C = 4, Q = 8, a = 0.30) pm/4S_v,fa = 1.003 / 0.998 / 0.986 / 0.980 at
0.3 / 1 / 3 / 10 f_amp (closed form 0.942 / 0.647 / 0.366 / 0.302); A2 slow node 1.004 / 1.004 at 1e-3 /
1e-2 f₀ (closed form 0.729 / 0.027); symmetric control ≤ 1e-3; 0.25–0.67 s per offset.
⚠ **`c_fa(0)/c` first read 0.9963 on the asymmetric fixtures and 1.0000 on the symmetric one with IDENTICAL
samples** — `frequency_aware_ppv`'s `times` is truncated to the sample count (398 against 399), so a
quadrature over it drops the last step, which differs from the cycle mean only on an asymmetric orbit.
Measured, not read: over `ppv()`'s full grid it is 1.000000000000.

**Built.** `frequency_aware_ppv` returns `samples_eq` (real and imaginary parts through `_equation_row_ppv`,
which casts to float) and `period`, with the one-short `times` documented; `PAC.frequency_aware_diffusion
(pss, offset)` = `c(f)` by `diffusion_constant`'s own quadrature over the full grid (`c(0) = c` exactly);
`oscillator_spectrum(..., frequency_aware=True)` uses `c(f)` per offset, cached on `|offset|`, and
`frequency_aware=False` is the closed form.  **Default ON**, per the recorded rule that the default is chosen
for accuracy over cost; it moves the answer only above a slow corner.  Gate:
`test_oscillator_spectrum_is_frequency_aware_above_the_slow_corner` (hostile within 3 % and the closed form
still at 0.647 / 0.302, symmetric control 2e-3, A2 within 2 % and the closed form still 0.729 / 0.027,
DC limit 1e-6).

⚠ **Two tests pinned the CLOSED FORM and failed on the default change, correctly**
(`test_a_source_behind_a_slow_node_rolls_off_...`, `test_the_frequency_aware_ppv_is_the_ppv_at_dc_...`,
plus the asymmetric-sum test) — each now passes `frequency_aware=False`, with the reason at the call.
⚠ **And one defect of my own, caught by those same runs:** the amplitude-pole warning's new sentence put a
literal `~2 %)` inside a %-formatted string, so every call above f_amp RAISED `unsupported format character`
instead of warning — four unrelated-looking failures, one cause.  Fixed (`%%`).


## PSP gate resistor thermal noise, added (peer report; built in a worktree subagent, 2026-09-14 night)

**The defect.** `PspMosLongChannel`'s `br_rg` carried the conductance and a collapse but no noise; PSP's
`CollapsableR` (`PSP103_module.include:1719`) contributes `white_noise(nt0·ggate)` (`:1684`).  Reproduced:
`CY[g,g] = CY[gi,gi] = CY[g,gi] = 0.0` on the IHP lv nmos card at 1/0.13 µm (rg 100.19 Ω) and 10/1 µm
(1.3025 Ω), where 4kT/rg = 1.6544e-22 / 1.2726e-20 A²/Hz; pmos the same.
**Fix.** `white_noise(mult·4·phit0·PSP_QELE/rg, 'rgate')` on `br_rg` — the PLAIN thermal voltage the validated
channel noise is handed (`psp_kernel.noise(..., phit0, ...)`), i.e. PSP's `nt0 = 4·KBOL·TKD`, no CT, no FNT;
the collapse kept.  **Measured after:** `CY[g,g]` = 4kT·mult/rg with relative error 0.0 (nmos, pmos, both
geometries, mult 1 and 2); drain/source/bulk/noi entries unchanged to 1e-12; a common-source stage's output
noise rises by 3.2256188e-19 against 4kT·rg·|A|² = 3.2256192e-19 (0.99999988).
`TestTheGateResistorNoise` (12 cases), each shown FAILING on the unfixed code first.

⚠⚠ **What the subagent found on the way, NOT fixed (owner's call):** several existing PSP noise comparisons
in `test_psp_gap.py` CANNOT FAIL — `pytest.approx`'s default absolute tolerance is 1e-12, and the recorded
quantities are `sig` 8e-42..1e-35, `sid` 9e-26..6e-23, `sfl` ≤ 6.5e-15, `C` ~1e-13 F.  Affected:
`test_both_densities_match_psp`, `test_the_gate_density_matches_psp`, `test_the_coupling_capacitance_matches_psp`,
the `rel=1e-12` check in `test_the_drain_density_is_untouched_by_the_split`, the plateau check in
`test_it_rises_as_f_squared_and_then_flattens`.  Its own first CY test passed on the broken element for this
reason; the new class uses `abs=0.0`.  ⚠ The fix raised `sig` through an ideal gate source by up to 1828×
and `test_the_gate_density_matches_psp` still passed — the vacuous tolerance again; that test now builds with
`rg = 0` (PSP's `sig` is the intrinsic density) and agrees with the record to 3.3e-6, which also says the
0.4 % / 8.4 % figures in those docstrings are stale.  Three tests that expected zero noise from a card-built
device now pass `rg = 0.0` with the reason recorded.

**The orbital flat factor: the WEIGHT, not the shape (item 3 of "Do all three", 2026-09-15).**  `orb/amint.py`.
The orbital line weight at `j f₀` is `W_j = Σ_{l,h} Re(row·C_lhj·row)`; Σ_j W_j equals the Monte-Carlo-validated
`row·R·row` exactly.  Integrating pnoise's fundamental AM over the line (37 log-spaced offsets, 0 to 0.45 f₀)
against `W_{+1}`:

| a | ∫AM/4 / W₊₁ | ∫S_orb / W₊₁ (grid check) | am/4S_orb at 0 … 19 f_amp |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.00 | **1.003** | 1.004 | 0.9995 … 0.9950 |
| 0.30 | **0.318** | 1.004 | 0.3166 flat … 0.3154 |

So the orbital line has the right SHAPE and 3.16× the WEIGHT the physical fundamental AM carries — two thirds of
the decomposition's orbital variance is not fundamental AM at the output.  Not at other harmonics (W₊₂ =
1.3e-8 against W₊₁ = 1.29e-6), not in PM (pm/4S_ph,fa 0.98–1.00).  **Hypothesis, NOT tested:** Traversa &
Bonani keep only the DC harmonic `V_{1,0}` of the phase–orbital correlation because averaging over the phase
diffusion kills j ≠ 0 — valid for times ≫ 1/(ω₀²c), i.e. within the linewidth.  Far above it (the LPTV regime
pnoise lives in) the full harmonic correlation between `v₁ᵀB` and `v_lᵀB` survives and would CANCEL part of the
orbital line (the sign needed).  Building that cross-spectrum is the next step if wanted; open.

**The harmonic guard, BUILT (same request).**  `oscillator_spectrum` refuses `harmonic = 0` and any harmonic
where the output has no carrier (|X_k| ≤ 1e-9 of the signal); `orbital_spectrum` refuses where its line weight
`W_k` ≤ 1e-9 of the total — both returned a plausible ~0 / tails-only value against a Monte-Carlo-confirmed
5.6e-6 V²/Hz at 2 f₀ on a symmetric orbit.  Deliberately NOT caught, recorded in both docstrings: DC with a
small real line (~100× over) and 2 f₀ on an asymmetric orbit (0.40).  Every existing caller passes
`harmonic=1` (checked, keyword and positional).  Test
`test_the_line_shape_spectra_refuse_a_harmonic_that_has_no_line` (refuses on the symmetric orbit, runs on the
asymmetric one where a line exists).


## The coloured-source guard judged colour against ONE global scale at ONE state (peer report, 2026-09-15)

`PAC._refuse_coloured` compared `CY(w0)` with `CY(10 w0)` at `x_last` against `1e-9·max|CY|` over the whole
matrix.  Since `d0e7e4e` a low-rg PSP device puts a white 4kT/rg of 1.27e-20 in `CY`, and a drain's flicker
colour (2.5e-30 on its own 1.3e-27 at that state) passed the global threshold 1.27e-29 — `covariance` folded 1/f
at w0 on a sample-and-hold (IHP lv nmos 10/1 µm, 100 kHz, clock phase 270 so the switch is OFF at t = 0) and
said nothing; the peer measured the held variance 4.5 % high.  Reproduced with controls: rg = 0 at 270 →
refused; rg on at 90 (x_last conducting) → refused; rg on without flicker → runs, correctly.
**Fix:** per-entry `|c1 − c2| > 1e-9·max(|c1|, |c2|)`, at `x_last` and 8 REDUCED states spread over the orbit.
Test `test_a_coloured_source_is_refused_whatever_else_is_in_CY` (PDK-gated), shown failing on the old guard
("DID NOT RAISE") first.  ⚠ **An instrument slip of mine on the way:** my orbit scan passed FULL waveform
columns to `_cy_at`, which takes a REDUCED state (it keeps `xr[:n−1]`) — the states were misaligned and the
per-entry colours it printed (0.889 / 1.9e-3) are not quoted anywhere as results; the fix deletes the
reference row.


## The PSP comparisons that could not fail, tightened (Andreas: "Do all three", item 1; worktree subagent, 2026-09-15)

`pytest.approx`'s default absolute tolerance (1e-12) decided every PSP comparison of a quantity below it —
noise 1e-42..6.5e-15, capacitances ~1e-13 F, leakage ~1e-14 A.  An instrumented run over the five PSP test
files listed every call site where `abs ≥ rel·|x|`; a mutation run (model value ×1.05 inside each comparison)
showed ALL 16 old sites in `test_psp_gap.py` still PASSED under the perturbation.  Now `abs = 0` (or a scaled
abs) at a tolerance set from the measured worst point, and every tightened comparison fails under ×1.05;
505 tests pass across `test_psp_gap.py`, `test_psp_current.py`, `test_hdl_psp_surface.py`, `test_psp_kernel.py`.

⚠⚠ **Four assertions had been checking the WRONG THING, all hidden by the vacuous tolerance:**
1. the long-device gate capacitance against PSP's TOTAL (with overlap) on a device built without overlap —
   8.5–15.2 % low at every point; against the intrinsic `cgg` it agrees to 3.7e-6;
2. two tests read `C[1,1]`, the TERMINAL gate row — exactly 0.0 once the gate resistor made the gate an
   internal node; a 100 % error that passed.  With the internal gate index: 3.7e-6;
3. `cgs` as `−C[gi,s]` — the induced-gate-noise network puts `−CGeff` on the `noi` column and `+CGeff` on
   the source column, so it read 0.56–2.0× with the wrong sign; `−(C[gi,s] + C[gi,noi])` agrees to 8.7e-7;
4. junction leakage against `+ijd` — PSP counts bulk-to-drain, our entry is into the drain; the model gives
   `−ijd` to 3.4e-5.

**Real residuals now PINNED at measured tolerances, not explained:** the drain density is NOT "identically"
unchanged by the induced-gate-noise split (−2.09e-7 at Vg 0.4 / Vd 0.05, flat over 1 Hz..1 GHz with flicker
off — not cancellation); the p-channel overlap/fringe capacitance is 1.99e-3 off on the short device
(3.3e-4 long; n-channel 3e-6 / 7e-7). **Stale figures corrected:** the "2 % unexplained" short-device residual
measures 3.64e-6; the noise docstrings' 0.4 / 8.4 / 3.2 / 0.55 / 0.1 % all measure 1e-6..2e-5.
**Vacuous, outside PSP, NOT fixed:** `test_spicecard.py` lines 49, 53, 64, 284; `test_chained_first_class.py`
line 371 (and probably 368–369).

⚠⚠ **CORRECTION (same session, an hour later): the pinned "p-channel overlap residual" is NOT a model residual.**
`test_the_total_gate_capacitance_matches` compares the model's `C[gi,gi]` with `cgg + cgsol + cgdol` from the
reference file, which does not record PSP's gate–bulk overlap `cgbol`; the model's `C[gi,gi]` includes it
(`q_gb = cgbov·vgb`, compact.py:645).  `cgbovl` is ~1e-28 F on the NMOS card and not negligible on the PMOS card
— which is exactly the n/p asymmetry.  Measured per point (9 points each), subtracting the model's own `cgbov`:

| device | worst got/tot − 1 | worst (got − cgbov)/tot − 1 |
|---|---|---|
| nmos long | 3.02e-6 | 3.02e-6 |
| nmos short | 6.75e-7 | 6.75e-7 |
| pmos long | 3.31e-4 | **3.37e-6** |
| pmos short | 1.99e-3 | **8.77e-7** |

A fifth wrong-reference assertion, found because a peer session reported its external PMOS reduced gate
capacitance agreeing to 6e-7 "once cgbol is included".  The test still carries the 0.3 % tolerance set on the
omission; to be corrected (subtract `cgbov`, tolerance from the measured 3.4e-6) with the rg-geometry batch.


## NPortS: `.Z` dropped Im S, both `.Z` and `.Y` fail on a sweep, and conversions renormalise to 50 Ω (peer report + audit, 2026-09-15)

**Reported (peer), reproduced:** `NPortS.Z` does `np.asarray(self.S).astype(float)` — the only float cast in
`nport.py`.  Complex S: `max|Z/Z_true − 1|` = 1.027 at z0 = 1 and 50 with a ComplexWarning; the real-S control
is exact.  ⚠ **Worse on an ordinary circuit:** the R = 50 Ω / C = 3.18 pF two-port from `TwoPortAnalysis`
(z0 = 1) gives `.Z` off by **5103×** at 1e7 Hz, 51× at 1e9, 1.12× at 1e11 — `E − S` is nearly singular near DC,
so an Im S of only 3.8e-4 dominates the inverse.
**Found beyond the report:** on a swept `solve_s(freqs=array)` result (Waveform-valued S) `.Z` raises
("setting an array element with a sequence") AND `.Y` raises (`_inv` sends object arrays to sympy:
"cannot sympify ... Waveform") — neither conversion works on a sweep.
**Fix prototyped (scratch), not yet applied:** `Z` keeps S's dtype and mirrors `Y`; a swept S is converted per
frequency (stack the Waveform entries' `get_y()` into (nf, n, n), convert, rebuild Waveforms on the source's own
`get_x()` axis and labels) — measured Z·Y = I to 2.9e-14 / 5.3e-16 / 4.6e-16 and Z = z0(E+S)(E−S)⁻¹ exactly on
the R–C fixture.  Queued with the rg-geometry batch.

⚠⚠ **A related hazard, reported NOT fixed (design, owner's call):** `NPortY.S`, `NPortZ.S`, `NPortA.S` and all
three `CS` properties take `z0 = 50.0` as an ARGUMENT OF A PROPERTY — unreachable — and those classes store no
`z0`; only `NPortS` carries `self.z0`.  So any S → Y/Z/A → S chain comes back referenced to 50 Ω whatever the
source's reference.  Measured (real S): `NPortS(S, z0).Y → NPortY(Y).S` and the Z route are exact at z0 = 50
(8e-17 / 2e-16) and wrong by **1.138** at z0 = 1 — which is `TwoPortAnalysis`'s own reference.  A dead knob
that reads as configurable.


## The batch after `2946ac4`: rg on W_f·L_f, the cgbol reference, NPortS.Z/Y (2026-09-15)

Each regression test was run against the UNFIXED code first.

**`to_long_channel`'s `rg` (peer report).**  `geometry()` now returns `Lf = max(l + delLPS, 1e-9)` and
`Wf = max(w + delWOD, 1e-9)` (PSP :46/:48) and `rg` divides by `Wf·Lf`.  Test
`test_the_gate_resistance_uses_psps_effective_width_and_length` rebuilds the expected value from `LE + 2·LAP` and
`WE + 2·WOT` (not from the new keys, so it cannot agree with itself), pins the n-channel 1.3025 / 100.192 Ω, and
asserts a card with an offset was seen.  Before: FAILED on PMOS 10/1 µm, 0.1 against 0.0914397.  After: 457 pass
across `test_psp_gap.py` and `test_psp_current.py`.  Audit of `psp_scaling`: no other drawn-W/L site (`iL`/`iW`
normalise by the drawn dimensions as PSP does; `u0_eff`'s `(WE/w)(l/LE)` cancels the element's drawn `w/l`).

**`test_the_total_gate_capacitance_matches`.**  Subtracts the model's own `cgbov` (the reference omits PSP's
`cgbol`) at rel 2e-5 — 4 cases pass; the 0.3 % tolerance set on the omission is gone.

**`NPortS.Z` / `.Y`.**  `Z` keeps S's dtype and is written as `Y` is (`_inv`, the toolkit's `sqrt`); both route a
Waveform-valued (swept) S through `_per_frequency`, which converts each frequency and rebuilds Waveforms on the
source's own axis.  `Y`'s docstring said "Return Z-parameter matrix"; corrected.  Test
`test_nports_z_keeps_the_imaginary_part_and_works_on_a_sweep` (complex S at z0 = 1 and 50 to 1e-12 with the round
trip; the R–C two-port's scalar `.Z` at 1e7/1e9/1e11 Hz; swept `.Z` against per-frequency values and Z·Y = I).
Before: FAILED at 1.0272.  After: all 12 NPort tests pass.

⚠ **Four `nport.py` doctests FAIL, and they failed identically at HEAD before this change** (checked in a
detached scratch worktree): `NPort.__floordiv__` and `NPort.series` raise at `sympy.symbols('abcd')` (now one
symbol; needs `'a b c d'`), and `NPortA.S` / `NPortS.A` differ only in NumPy's array repr (spacing,
`np.float64(...)`), the values right.  The suite does not collect doctests, which is why they went stale unseen.
Recorded, not fixed.


## E5 decided: the ~2 % residual is a CONSTANT — no excursion dependence at any order the data can see (Andreas: "Do 1 and 2", item 2, 2026-09-15)

**Run.** 60 more restart-free Monte Carlo runs on the slow fixture (a = 0, 10 000 periods each; PSD × ¼ / 1 / 4 with
seeds 141–160 / 121–140 / 161–180), bringing every level to 36 seeds.  Same worker (`mc_worker5.py`) and reducer
logic as the recorded joint fit; the new full-level files are tagged `psd1`.  **Instrument control:** the new
full-level seeds match the old ones (demod 1.0088 ± 0.0129, crossing 1.0054 ± 0.0132 on the first 10), and the
reducer restricted to the old seeds reproduces the recorded fit exactly (+0.0118 ± 0.0101 / +0.0060 ± 0.0091).

**Named before the run:** 36 seeds/level → σ(c₁) ≈ 0.0067; c₁ ≥ 3σ from 0 ⇒ linear-in-amplitude; c₁ consistent
with 0 and 0.02 excluded at 3σ ⇒ constant; otherwise undetermined.

| estimator | level ratios P(s)/(s·P(1)), s = ¼, 4 | linear c₁ | quadratic c₂ |
|---|---|---|---|
| demodulated phase | 0.9964, 0.9964 | **−0.0002 ± 0.0064** (χ² 0.60/1) | −0.0006 ± 0.0025 |
| zero-crossing phase | 1.0034, 1.0004 | **−0.0011 ± 0.0064** (χ² 0.21/1) | −0.0001 ± 0.0023 |

**Outcome: constant.**  A linear law carrying the whole 2 % (c₁ = 0.02) is excluded at 3.2σ (demod) / 3.3σ
(crossing); the quadratic law it would need (c₂ = 0.0094) at ~4σ.  The 1.2σ "live possibility" (c₁ = +0.012 on 16
seeds) was noise.  So the ~2 % offset between the noisy estimator and the deterministic tone route is flat in `a`,
in the grid, in the band AND in the noise excursion; what it is keyed on remains the one screen already recorded —
something whose slow/core difference is large (the noise's modal composition at the two source locations).  E5
stays OPEN as to cause; its order question is closed.  Machine time: ~5 h of Monte Carlo at 5 workers.


## E5, what the two routes do NOT share — steps 1 and 2a (Andreas: "Do as you suggest", 2026-09-15 night)

The residual to explain is a slow/core BAND double ratio, MC against the deterministic forward-tone route at a = 0:
demod 1.036 vs 1.010 (+2.6 %), crossing 1.129 vs 1.112 (+1.5 %).  The tone route already shares the fixture, the
fixed-step gear integrator and grid, and the demod/crossing estimators with the MC (`mc/tone.py`), so those cannot
carry it.  Scripts: session scratch `e5/estbias.py`, `e5/estcheck.py`, `e5/tone_trunc.py`; old scratch `mc/`.

**Step 1 — the MC reducer's spectrum estimator: CLEAN.**  Synthetic Gaussian phase records with KNOWN spectra
(pnoise's PM shape for the slow and the core source; synthesized 8× longer and cropped, so trends and sub-resolution
power are present), through the reducer's exact pipeline (whole-record polyfit detrend, Welch nperseg 5000 with
linear detrend, mean of P·f² over 0.08–0.15), 400 records per shape:

| shape | B = estimate / truth |
|---|---|
| flat (calibration) | 1.0001 ± 0.0022 |
| slow PM (in-band S·r² +3.4 %) | 1.0004 ± 0.0022 |
| core PM (in-band S·r² −1.1 %) | 1.0004 ± 0.0022 |
| pure 1/f² | 0.9976 ± 0.0021 |

B_slow/B_core = 1.0000 ± 0.0031 against the 2.6 % needed.  ⚠ Slow and core agreeing to four digits with identical
error bars was flagged as too good; an instrument check (paired and independent draws, and ±30 % tilted in-band
shapes that must move B) is running and is recorded when in.

**Step 2a — the tone route's sideband truncation (|m| ≤ 4): RULED OUT.**  32 more tones (|m| = 5…12, both fixtures):

| reading | slow/core at |m| ≤ 12 relative to |m| ≤ 4 | tail fraction core / slow |
|---|---|---|
| PM₁ | 0.9997 | 3.1e-4 / 1.3e-5 |
| demod | 0.9997 | 2.9e-4 / 1.2e-5 |
| crossing | 0.9955 | 4.6e-3 / 1.2e-4 |

Opposite in sign to the residual and ≪ it (the core's tail is 25–40× the slow node's, as its RC predicts).
⚠ **A convergence claim in this log was partly wrong:** "the m = ±4 terms are 1e-3 of m = ±1, so the sum is
converged" holds for EVEN m only (≤ 1e-3 on this half-wave-symmetric orbit); ODD m = ±3 carry 8.9 % / 7.7 % (core)
of m = +1 on the demod reading, and the crossing tail decays slowly (2e-4 at m = ±12).  The sum was nonetheless
converged to the level that matters here, as the table shows.

**Step 2b — launched: simultaneous random-phase excitation.**  `mc/mtone_worker.py` is the MC worker with the white
per-step noise replaced by a flat-amplitude random-phase multi-tone synthesized on the step grid: arm A on the tone
route's coverage (|m ± r|, |m| ≤ 4, r ∈ 0.08–0.15; 6300 tones), arm B on every bin to the step Nyquist (white density,
deterministic amplitudes; rms 4.2381e-3 against the MC's 4.2389e-3).  Core/slow × seeds 901/902, 10 000 periods.
Named: A ≈ tone & B ≈ MC → content outside the sideband bands; A ≈ B ≈ MC → simultaneity; A ≈ B ≈ tone → the per-step
Gaussian amplitude statistics.

**Step 1's instrument check (in): the estimator is clean to ~1e-4, and one of my predictions about the check was wrong.**

| arm | result |
|---|---|
| PAIRED draws (identical per record) | B_slow = B_core = 0.9941; per-record slow/core **1.00001 ± 0.00003** |
| independent seeds | ratio 0.9919 ± 0.0031 (the earlier independent run: 1.0000 ± 0.0031) |
| in-band tilt +30 %, paired | B = 0.9942 ± 0.0022 |
| in-band tilt −30 %, paired | B = 0.9940 ± 0.0022 |

The paired design is the decisive one: on identical realizations the reducer's shape-dependent bias between the slow and
core spectra is ≤ 3e-5, against 2.6 % to explain.  ⚠ **Named before the run and REFUTED:** "±30 % tilted in-band
shapes will move B visibly" — they move it by 1e-4.  The detrend + Welch + band mean is unbiased for smooth in-band
shapes, so a tilt cannot bias it; that is also why slow and core agreed to four digits in the first run (B is set by
the draw realization, not the shape).  ⚠ The first run's "pure 1/f² 0.9976" was draw scatter, not a shape response,
and is not evidence that the test can see a shape.  The two independent-seed ratios (1.0000, 0.9919, each ± 0.0031)
scatter as independent 400-record means do, one at 2.6σ.  **An estimator-bias explanation of E5 is excluded at ~1e-4.**

**Step 2b — the multi-tone arms: INCONCLUSIVE, and a reading I gave was withdrawn the same hour.**  Welch readout
(the MC's own estimator), relative to the white MC's slow/core: arm A (the tone route's coverage, all at once)
0.9985 ± 0.0174 demod / 1.0118 ± 0.0158 crossing; arm B (every bin, flat amplitude) 1.0381 ± 0.0249 / 1.0265 ±
0.0169; the tone route sits at 0.975 / 0.985 of the MC.  ±1.7 % cannot separate a 2.5 % effect.  ⚠ An "exact-bin"
readout then gave arm A / tone = 1.0248 / 1.0291 with the two seeds agreeing to 0.09 %, reported as pointing at
simultaneity — WITHDRAWN: narrow windows around r = 0.10 put the seeds at 0.958 vs 1.170 (±0.005, 101 bins) and a
shifted 701-bin window at 0.9907 / 1.0004.  The design assumption was wrong: several input tones (different m) land
in the SAME output bin with random relative phases, so a random-phase multi-tone is noise-like per bin, not
deterministic; the 0.09 % was luck.  Arm B's exact-bin readout is invalid outright (seeds 17–19 % apart).

⚠⚠ **And the residual itself was never shown to be significant.**  Recomputed from the files: the MC slow/core
band ratio at a = 0 moves +0.09 % (demod) / −0.05 % (crossing) from 16 to 36 slow seeds; its standard error is
±1.53 % / ±1.32 %, dominated by the CORE fixture's 16 seeds (±1.37 % / ±1.12 % on its band power).  Against that,
the recorded +2.6 % (demod) / +1.5 % (crossing) is ~1.7σ / ~1.1σ.  Every mechanism search in this section assumed a
residual the statistics do not establish.  Deciding it needs core seeds (36 per fixture → ~1.0 % on the double ratio).

## 2026-09-14 — the gear/trap free-period stall near λ₂ → 1: characterized, then DETECTED AND REDIRECTED (not fixed)

**Reported** by a peer session (pycircuit-only runs on its weakly limited LC fixture; no external-simulator numbers
involved): `PSS(method='gear')` on an oscillator with second Floquet multiplier 0.99 returns `converged=False` at 800
points per period and converges at 1600.  Reproduced here, then characterized (scripts in the session scratchpad
`stall/`):

| probe | result |
|---|---|
| methods at 0.99, 800 pts | gear and trap stall; **radau converges in 7–10 evaluations** |
| residual purity | the residual is a pure function of the unknowns (re-evaluation bit-identical) |
| solver strategies from the stall | damped, Armijo (20 halvings), Levenberg–Marquardt: all stop at the SAME floor; tighter inner tolerances change nothing |
| nearby root? | a scan along the Jacobian's two weakest directions finds no lower point — a floor, not slow convergence |
| grid sweep | sharp threshold: converges quadratically at 925…1600, never at ≤ 900; `σ_min(J)` at the root falls LINEARLY toward ~904 |
| weakest direction | ~99 % in the entering-history block `x_{-1}` of the solved-history unknowns `(x_0, x_{-1}, T)` |
| discrete λ₂ | unchanged at 0.990 across the sweep |
| at 0.999 | no convergence even at 6400 points |

**Reading:** the two-step discrete periodic solution of the solved-history system ceases to exist below a grid threshold
that grows as λ₂ → 1; no iteration count, damping or tolerance can reach a root that is not there.  Radau, with no
history and no manufactured opening, does not show it.

**Fix direction chosen (Andreas): detect and redirect.**  `PSS._free_period_solve` now calls
`_diagnose_lmm_free_period_stall` on a failed, non-trivial, non-stage-method solve: one extra residual evaluation, SVD of
the Jacobian at the last iterate, and a warning that names the solved-history stall when the weakest direction is > 50 %
in `x_{-1}` (a generic multistep-floor warning otherwise, e.g. trap), pointing at radau or a finer grid.  The generic
non-convergence warning no longer advises `method='gear'` on an AUTONOMOUS solve (it did, which sends this caller the
wrong way); non-autonomous advice is unchanged.

**Pycircuit-native twin** (`_weakly_limited_lc` in `test_analysis_shooting.py`: L = 100 µH, C = 100 pF, loss 1e-4 S,
cubic negative resistance sized by first-order averaging for λ₂ at 1 V).  ⚠ The first twin had the negative-resistance
SIGN reversed (a positive cubic conductance: gear "converged" to T = −1.26e-6, collapsed to T ≈ −2e-21 at 1000 points)
— the vdP convention `i = μ(u − u³/3)` is the negative resistance.  Corrected, it reproduces the peer's picture:

| twin run | converged | evals | σ_min | weakest vector in `x_{-1}` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| gear 0.99, 800 | no | 51 | 1.1e-4 | 0.992 |
| gear 0.99, 1000 | yes | 10 | 1.4e-3 | 0.988 |
| radau 0.99, 800 | yes | 6 | — | (no history block) |
| trap 0.99, 800 | no | 73 | — | (no history block) → generic warning |
| gear 0.99, 200 (6 its) | no | 19 | 2.4e-6 | 0.999 |
| gear 0.9, 200 (6 its) | yes | 6 | 5.3e-3 | 0.999 |
| radau 0.99, 200 (6 its) | yes | 9 | — | — |

⚠ The weak singular vector sits in `x_{-1}` on a CONVERGED gear solve too (0.988, 0.999), so the history share alone is
not a stall detector; it is read only after the solve has already failed, as the ATTRIBUTION, never as the trigger.

**Pinned:** `test_a_gear_free_period_stall_near_unit_multiplier_is_named_and_redirected` (gear 0.99 @ 200 stalls and
is named with radau advice and no gear advice; radau converges there silently; gear 0.9 converges silently; 7 s).
Proven to FAIL on the pre-change code in a HEAD worktree (`assert 0 == 1` on the stall warning, after the fixture's own
"still stalls" guard passed).

## 2026-09-14 — NPort reference impedance carried through conversions (peer spec, Andreas confirmed)

**The hazard, measured on HEAD** (module loaded from `git show HEAD:`): a complex S at z0 = 1 converted to
`NPortY`/`NPortZ`/`NPortA` and read back with `.S` is off by **1.18** (max entry) — every class renormalised to 50 Ω,
because `.S`/`.CS` were properties with a `z0=50` argument a property can never receive, and conversion constructors
dropped the source's `z0`.  `TwoPortAnalysis.solve_s` returns z0 = 1, so `NPortY(result).S` described a different
reference than `result.S`.

**Built:** `NPort.z0` (class default 50); `NPortY/Z/A(..., z0=None)` take the source port's `z0` when converting and
50 Ω when constructed directly; `.S`/`.CS` evaluate at the stored `z0` through `_s_at(z0)`/`_cs_at(z0)`; `to_s(z0)` on
every class (incl. `NPortS`, which renormalises through Z/Y rather than relabelling); `NPortS(port)` keeps the source's
`z0` (and toolkit) unless one is given, and then renormalises; cascade/parallel/series results keep the left operand's
`z0`; `NPortY/Z.S` convert a swept (Waveform) matrix frequency by frequency.

**Pinned:** `test_a_converted_nport_carries_its_reference_impedance` — complex-S round trip through Y/Z/A at z0 = 1
and 50; direct `NPortY(Yref).S` unchanged at 50 Ω; `to_s(1/50/75)` on all four classes against the textbook
`(Z − z0)(Z + z0)⁻¹` AND Bosma's `CS = kT(E − SSᴴ)` (an independent reference for the noise renormalisation); the
analysis result (z0 = 1) scalar and swept; a cascade keeps z0 = 1.  Fails on HEAD (no `z0`), the 12 existing NPort
tests and the symbolic example tests pass; the 4 stale doctests are the same four as on HEAD.  Scope: a swept `CS`
(plain-array entries, not Waveforms) is not converted per frequency.

20 more core-fixture seeds (121–140, a = 0, full level, 10 000 periods; `mc/run_e5core.sh`), reduced with
`scratchpad/e5/significance.py` (same Welch × f² band mean over 0.08–0.15 per period as `mc_e5fit.py`).  The reducer
reproduces the recorded errors at 16 core seeds (±1.53 % demod, ±1.32 % crossing) before any new number is read.

| reading | core seeds | MC slow/core | shift vs 16 | residual vs tone route | σ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| demod | 16 → 26 → 36 | 2.5187e-6 → — → 2.4812e-6 (±1.04 %) | −0.97 % → −1.49 % | +2.60 % → +1.60 % → **+1.07 %** | **1.0** |
| crossing | 16 → 26 → 36 | 2.7411e-6 → — → 2.6944e-6 (±1.01 %) | −1.17 % → −1.70 % | +1.50 % → +0.31 % → **−0.23 %** | **0.2** |

Named before the run: ~1.0 % on the double ratio — measured 1.04 % / 1.01 %.  The residual shrank monotonically as core
seeds were added; the 16-seed core mean sat ~1.5 % high.  (The residual is moved by the ratio's shift; the slow-seed
16→36 move of +0.09 %/−0.05 % is ignored, ≪ 1σ.)

**Consequences.**  (1) E5 is closed as NO ESTABLISHED RESIDUAL: pnoise/the tone route agree with the MC slow/core
ratio to ~1 %.  (2) The order fit ("a CONSTANT", c₁ consistent with 0) is consistent with a residual of zero and should
not be read as evidence of a constant offset.  (3) ⚠ Every mechanism search in this section — estimator bias (clean to
1e-4), sideband truncation (ruled out), multi-tone simultaneity (inconclusive, a claim withdrawn) — chased a 1.7σ
number.  The lesson is the one already in the measurement-discipline record: compute a residual's significance from
the files BEFORE searching for its mechanism.

## 2026-09-15 — E4 and E6's orbital half: both EXPLAINED by measurement (Andreas: "Start 1 and 2")

### E4 — the +3.0 % is the two knee-crossing solves: a missing t = 0 node, then the clamp edge

Scratch `e4/` (repro, perstep, seedsweep, clamp_trbdf2, nodes, t0node).  Reproduced exactly: gear2 and trap 135 → 139
device evaluations (+3.0 %), worst solve 8 → 14 iterations; every other family saves on the same case (radau −2.2 %,
esdirk43 −13.1 %, trbdf2 −7.1 %, glm3 −13.6 %, glm4 −12.5 %).  Per solve (gear2; trap identical), seed error on node b:

| solve | seed − root, off | on | its off / on | Δ evals |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | −0.236 | −0.236 (declined, 1 node) | 8 / 8 | 0 |
| 3 | −0.040 | **+0.196** | 7 / 13 | +6 |
| 4 | −0.015 | **−0.075** (clamp edge) | 5 / 14 | +9 |
| 5 | −0.009 | −0.019 | 5 / 6 | +1 |
| 6–40 | — | 5–50× smaller | — | −12 |

**Mechanism.**  (1) Newton cost on the exponential is lopsided in the seed error: a seed sweep on the real step
equations reproduces the predictor's counts exactly (7 at −0.040, 13 at +0.196, 14 at −0.075); from above ~1.1 it/VT
plus ~5, from below the first tangent overshoots up the exponential (−0.3 → 28).  (2) Solve 3: only TWO nodes (t₁, t₂ —
the t = 0 state is never a node), so a LINEAR fit lands 7.5 VT past the knee, inside the clamp.  (3) Solve 4: the
quadratic wanted −0.171; the CLAMP (xref − 1.5·motion) set −0.075, below the root where starts are dear.  One-step
families escape because their stages are nodes inside each step (trbdf2's worst knee seed +0.053).

**Discriminators.**  Clamp K = 0.5/1.0/1.5/2.0/3.0 → +0.0/+3.7/+3.0/+5.2/+10.4 % (1.5 stays near the best total; a
tighter clamp gives savings back elsewhere).  Degree 1 everywhere: solve 4 14 → 6 but total +3.7 %.  The t = 0 state as
a node (scratch monkeypatch): solve 3 13 → 10, total **+0.7 %** — it carries 3 of the 4 evaluations.  ⚠ Failed
predictions: "degree 1 ≤ 135" (140); "one iteration per VT" held only loosely.

**Not a defect:** `Transient._pred_reset` documents the transient START as a discontinuity ("a node from before it is a
silently wrong guess") — a source may step at t = 0⁺ or the initial state may be inconsistent.  A node at t = 0 only
when the start is consistent would be a design change: owner's call, unbuilt, measured on this one fixture only.  The
recorded no-carve-out decision stands.

### E6 — the orbital flat factor IS the orbital mode's PM projection at the output

Scratch `orb/quad.py`, `orb/closure.py`, `orb/closure_grid.py`.  The phase mode `p₀ = ẋ` is pure PM, so a
phase–orbital correlation can act only in the PM quadrature.  Split the (single, real-μ) orbital mode's fundamental at
the output against `U₀,₁`:

| a | AM share sin² arg(U_l,1/U_0,1) | recorded am/4S_orb (flat factor) |
|---|---|---|
| 0.00 | 1.0000 | 0.9995 |
| 0.10 | 0.9716 | 0.97 |
| 0.20 | 0.6889 | 0.6988 |
| 0.30 | 0.3069 | 0.3166 |

(Named before running: AM share = the flat factor, ~1 at a = 0; AM share ~1 at 0.30 would kill it — held.)  Closure at
1/3/10 f_amp (tot = (up+lo)/2 against 2(S_ph,fa + AMshare·S_orb)): **1.002–1.005** at a = 0.30, 1.002–1.003 at 0.20,
control 0.998–0.999; am/(4 S_orb·AMshare) 1.014 (0.20) / 1.031 (0.30), flat in offset — and **1.031 → 1.017 on a 2×
grid** (400 → 800 points; total 1.005 → 0.998), halving with h like the known O(h) `floquet_modes` residual: a grid
effect, not physics.  pm/4S_ph,fa stays 0.98–0.985 at a = 0.30 on both grids (open, ~2 %).

**Reading.**  `orbital_spectrum` counts the orbital mode's variance in BOTH quadratures of the output.  Pnoise's AM holds
only the AM share; its PM holds the phase term alone, so the orbital mode's PM projection is cancelled by the
phase–orbital correlation that Traversa & Bonani keep only as its DC harmonic `V_{1,0}` (a symmetry zero at the source
here, hence `S_corr` ~1e-8).  This confirms the recorded hypothesis's SIGN and SIZE by the quadrature split plus the
closure; ⚠ the full-harmonic cross-spectrum itself was NOT built, so its equality to −PMshare·S_orb is inferred from
the closure, not computed independently.  On a symmetric orbit the orbital mode is pure AM (share 1.0000), which is
why every symmetric fixture validated.  Candidate (owner's call, unbuilt): report `orbital_spectrum` as AM share ×
line (the physical AM), or build the full-harmonic correlation.  Peak RSS ~290 MB per script.

### E6 — the FULL phase–orbital correlation, BUILT: `PAC.modal_spectrum` / `PAC.correlation_spectrum` (Andreas: "Build full correlation term")

**Construction.**  Every Floquet mode l — the phase mode (μ = 0) and each orbital mode — carries noise from input
sideband m to the output at ω: `T_m^l(ω) = Σ_j (d·U_{l,j}) V_{l,m−j}ᵀ / (i(ω − jω₀) − μ_l + a_j)`, with U, V the Fourier
coefficients of p_l, q_l (`orbital_correlation`'s conventions) and `a_j = j²ω₀²c/2` the phase-diffusion rate.  With
`T = T⁰ + Σ_l T^l`, `S = Σ_m T_m (CY/2) T_mᴴ`; phase, orbital, correlation are its blocks.  No fitted factor.

**Gates, each named before its run** (scratch `orb/fullcorr.py`, `fullcorr_grid.py`, `nearcarrier.py`, `sidebands.py`):

| check | a = 0 | a = 0.30 |
|---|---|---|
| total/(pnoise/2), +1/+3/+10 f_amp, 400 pts | 1.0006 / 1.0006 / 1.0006 | 1.054 / 1.014 / 1.006 |
| same, 800 pts | 1.00013 (all) | **1.026 / 1.006 / 1.002** (halves: O(h)) |
| H 8→12, sidebands 16→24 | — | 4th digit |
| −3 / −10 f_amp (lower sideband) | 1.0006 | 1.008 / 1.006 |
| harmonic 2, ±3/±10 f_amp | 1.0009–1.0010 | 1.005–1.014 |
| correlation / orbital, +3 / +10 / −10 | +0.013 / +0.047 / −0.052 | **−1.74 / −1.49 / −1.38** |
| phase / library Lorentzian, 0–3 linewidths | 1.00022 | 1.037 (O(h), 1.017 on 800) |
| correlation / phase within the linewidth | ~1e-9 | ~2e-6 |

The modal sum reproduces pnoise's upper/lower sideband ASYMMETRY (correlation −1.74 vs −1.13 of orbital at ±3 f_amp),
which the two-term sum could not.  ⚠ Why the three are computed together rather than the correlation added to
`oscillator_spectrum + orbital_spectrum`: the correlation cancels most of the other two, so a few percent in any part is
amplified in the total, and the line-shape spectra keep only each line's resonant term (modal orbital/library 1.0025 /
1.0074 / 1.024 at 1/3/10 f_amp on the SYMMETRIC orbit).  ⚠ Withdrawn: "the dropped cross term is NOT the cause" (docstring,
warning, two test docstrings) held only for eq (92)'s DC-harmonic form.

**Pinned:** `test_the_modal_spectrum_with_the_full_correlation_closes_on_pnoise` (a = 0: total ≤ 2e-3, |corr/orb| < 0.1,
phase = Lorentzian ≤ 1e-3 at 0 and 1 linewidth, corr/phase < 1e-5; a = 0.30: total ≤ 2 %, corr/orb < −1, old two-term
sum > 2×; `correlation_spectrum` bit-equal; harmonic 0 refused).  Mutation (correlation dropped from the total) KILLED
(a = 0 branch reads 0.994 / 0.978 / 1.027).  `orbital_spectrum`'s asymmetry warning now points at `modal_spectrum`.

## 2026-09-15 — Time-sampled noise (`PAC.sampled_noise` / `sampled_variance`) BUILT, and a coloured-fold ADDITIVITY defect in `pnoise(cyclostationary=True)` FIXED

**Request** (peer session, Andreas's decision): a time-sampled, frequency-resolved periodic noise analysis so a sampler's
held variance can include coloured (1/f) sources.  Priced first; Andreas chose a first cut (steps 1–3, Sepke eq. 33
deferred), gear/one-step period maps only (stage methods refused), and the SERIES-frequency band.

**Construction.**  For a driven circuit the periodic adjoint at `ν = f + n f0` differs from the one at `f` only through
phases (`exp(-jνT)` is shared), so ONE transposed solve per `(t0, f)`, seeded at `t0` through the reverse pass's `inject`,
gives the per-step sensitivity `Sv_j`; every sideband is `Σ_j exp(j2πν t_{j+1}) Sv_j M_j(ν)` with `M` the per-component
square root of `CY(x(t_{j+1}), ν)`.  `S(f; t0) = Σ_n |…|²` is the one-sided PSD of the sample series `y(t0 + kT)` on
`(0, f0/2]`; the variance over `[fmin, fmax]` is its trapezoid on a log grid, nothing added below `fmin` (so `±fmin` is
excluded around EVERY clock harmonic).  Oscillators refused; `fmin` has no default.

**Gates, named before each run** (scratch `snoise/`):

| check | result |
|---|---|
| seeded row at t0 = 0 vs `adjoint_transfer_row` | 1.5e-16 |
| LTI series sum (|n| ≤ 10) vs `Σ pnoise(|f+nf0|)`, white | 1e-10 |
| same with a 1/f source at 0.137 / 0.01 f0 | 1e-10 |
| switched capacitor (gear 400), white held variance vs `covariance` | 0.998721 = 0.998721 (1e-6) |
| tracking instant vs `covariance` | 0.846606 / 0.846745 (1.6e-4, covariance's O(h/τ) floor) |
| poison: CY sampled one step early | held 1.30 (alive; j+1 is right) |
| poison: sidebands cut to N/8 | tracking 0.767 (alive) |
| flicker-only held variance vs fmin, 1e-4 → 1e-1 f0 | +0.0010 kT/C per decade, flat (ratio 0.988) |

⚠ **A prediction that FAILED:** "total variance grows by a constant per decade of fmin" — the increments were 0.020 then 0.18
kT/C because raising fmin also removes the FLAT white series PSD (`fmin/(f0/2)` of it); the ln law holds for the flicker
part alone.

**⚠⚠ The defect.**  The coloured fold took ONE symmetric square root of the SUMMED `CY(x(t), ν)` per band.  Independent
sources whose modulations differ then do NOT add — `sqrt(A(t)+B)` couples them across `(t, t')`.  Measured on the
switched capacitor (switch white `4kTg(t)` + a constant 1/f source at 'out'): shipped `pnoise(cyclostationary=True)`
read both = white + flicker **+7.3 % of the total at 0.013 f0, +2.2 % at 0.137 f0**; the sampled held variance was
+0.85 % kT/C; with white and flicker inside ONE element, joint 1.3279 vs two separate elements 1.2150 kT/C (**+9.3 %**).
The white P-form was never affected (linear in `CY`).  **Fix** (`_cy_components_model`, Andreas: "Fix both, shared
helper"): one root per leaf element × {white part, power-law part}, fitted per element with the existing
thermal-plus-power-law fit (`_colour_fit`, refactored out of `_cy_colour_model`); a uniform exponent scales one root per
band.  After: pnoise both − white − flicker = 1.5e-16 / 8.6e-16 of the total; one element (split) vs two elements
2.3e-11 (sampled).  Guards: if the elements do not sum to the circuit's `CY` (an override), warn and fall back to the
joint model; an element whose colour does not fit is evaluated per band with one root (warned — its in-element split is
lost: 4.2e-4 on the EKV stage).

**Moved pinned number:** `test_mos_pnoise_runs_through_the_cyclostationary_route…` flicker ratio 0.319 → **0.3055** (the
old value was the joint root's; thermal-only 0.376 unchanged).  Its per-band reference now differs from the fitted route
by 4.2e-4 (the in-element split), bounded < 1e-3; a class-attribute restore bug in that test (staticmethod lost) fixed.

**Pinned:** `test_the_sampled_variance_is_the_covariance_at_that_instant_for_white_sources`,
`test_the_sampled_series_psd_is_the_pnoise_fold_on_an_lti_circuit_white_and_one_over_f`,
`test_independent_noise_sources_add_in_the_coloured_folds` (mutation: joint root restored → killed, 2.62e-16 vs sum
2.43e-16), `test_the_sampled_variance_grows_as_ln_fmin_with_flicker_and_refuses_what_it_cannot_do`.  Open: Sepke eq. (33)
special case; stage-method period maps; the integrated time-average identity (validation 2) not pinned separately.
Scope note (Mahmutoglu & Demir 2015): modulated-stationary 1/f over-predicts a switched MOSFET's low-frequency flicker —
agreement with another tool is agreement on the convention.

### Sampled noise under the STAGE methods, and the time-average identity per frequency (Andreas: "do sampled noise for stage methods … then the time-average consistency check")

**Stage methods, native.**  A radau/trbdf2 source enters EVERY stage, so the seeded reverse pass reads the coupling
`h Σ_i A_ik p_i` at each stage abscissa `t_j + c_k h` (`_stage_pass`, the loops of `_sideband_forced_{dirk,full}` with a
seed at `t0`) and `CY` is sampled at the STAGE states (`_stage_states`: one re-traversal of the converged orbit on a
fresh inner transient, the run's own restored, cached per factored period).  GLM maps stay refused.

| sampler, white (400 → 800 points) | radau | trbdf2 |
|---|---|---|
| LTI series sum vs pnoise fold (|n| ≤ 10) | 1e-10 | 1e-10 |
| held, CY at stage states | 1.000000 → 1.000000 | 0.999979 → 0.999997 |
| held, CY at end-of-step state for every stage | 0.8667 → 0.9319 | 0.8636 → 0.9311 |
| tracking (stage states) | 0.9491 → 0.9746 (O(h)) | 0.9162 → 0.9566 (O(h)) |
| `covariance` held / tracking | 0.876 → 0.934 / 1.000 | 0.870 → 0.933 / 0.998 → 0.9994 |

⚠ **My named prediction failed:** "both CY placements agree with covariance to O(h²)".  The stage-method `covariance` is
NOT a reference at a switching edge: its Van Loan injection freezes `C`, `G`, `CY` at the END of each step (the off
conductance across the switch-off edge), reading the held variance 13 % low at 400 points and 6.6 % at 800 — an O(h)
error, halving — while under gear it reads 0.9987.  Its tracking value (exact injection where the operating point is
slow) is the better one there; the sampled route's tracking O(h) matches gear's.  The three methods' orbits are
identical at the same indices (checked), so this is not a time shift.  Recorded, not fixed.

**Time average, per frequency.**  Averaged over the sampling instant the series samples the time-averaged
autocorrelation at lags kT, so `mean_t0 S(f; t0) = Σ_k S_avg(|f + k f0|)` with `S_avg = pnoise(cyclostationary=True)` —
⚠ my earlier statement "only the integrated variance connects" was WRONG; the per-frequency identity holds for the FOLD.
Fixture: the sampler buffered by a unity VCVS into an RC low-pass (pole f0/20), gear.

| named: fold |k| ≤ 10 closes to 1e-5 | ✗ 1.0014 / 1.0070 at 0.137 / 0.37 f0 (400 points) |
| hypothesis: pnoise samples CY one step early | ✗ refuted — CY(j) reads 1.30 / 1.33, CY(j+1) 1.0011 / 1.0058 (200 points) |

| 60 points, full fold | 0.99961 (cut at 10: 1.0025) |

**Pinned:** `test_the_sampled_noise_runs_natively_under_the_stage_methods` (LTI identity both methods; held 1 ± 1e-4 radau
400/800 and trbdf2 400; tracking error halves 400 → 800), `test_the_time_average_of_the_sampled_psd_is_the_fold_of_time_averaged_pnoise`
(60 points: full fold ≤ 1e-3, the |k| ≤ 10 cut > 2e-3 off).  The radau refusal left the refusal test.  GLM refusal untested
(no GLM PSS fixture).

### Peer report on 9b98d69: `pnoise(cyclostationary=True)` with a 1/f source ON a clock harmonic — FIXED, and the coloured ratio stop was dead

**Reproduced** (switched capacitor + `IS(noiseFc=1e6)` at 'out', gear 200 points): 99.9 / **100.0** / 100.1 / **200.0** kHz →
9.16e-15 / **6.28e-2** / 9.15e-15 / **5.32e-3** V²/Hz, no library warning at the harmonics.  Mechanism, measured: `1/T` rounds
(99999.999999999985 Hz for T = 1e-5 s), so the literal 100 kHz puts the folded band 1.455e-11 Hz from DC — finite and
enormous for 1/f — and the harmonic guard (`HARMONIC_GUARD` = 1e-12 f0) refused only a NON-finite CY.  A second form: at
exactly `k/T` the band is at 0 and the colour model's Python float division raised `ZeroDivisionError` from inside the
guard.  **Fix:** on a harmonic, a frequency-DEPENDENT CY at the folded band refuses by name (white stays allowed — the
driven-divider value); the colour models divide in numpy (inf, then the non-finite refusal).

**A second defect found on the way (pre-existing since the colour model, 2026-09-09):** the stop rule evaluates the model at
NEGATIVE band frequencies (`f − l f0 < 0`), and a non-integer power of a negative base is NaN — so the coloured ratio stop
never fired: every coloured cyclostationary call ran to the Nyquist bound and warned "stopped at Nyquist".  Located with
numpy invalid → raise (`_cy_components_model.model`).  Fixed with `|w|`.  The folded totals were unaffected (the fold used
`|f − p f0|` already); the MOS pin (0.3055), identity, additivity, LTI and stage tests pass unchanged.

**Pinned:** `test_pnoise_refuses_a_coloured_source_folded_onto_dc_at_a_clock_harmonic` (f0, 2f0 as `k/T` and as literals
raise naming the harmonic; 1.001 f0 ratio-stops with no invalid-power or Nyquist warning and matches `ratio_tol=0` to 1e-4;
white on f0 still answers).  Fails on HEAD (`ZeroDivisionError`).  Docstring: pass grid instants to `sampled_noise` when
comparing at a round instant (a `timestep` of T/1000 can give 999 steps).

### Sepke et al. 2009 eq. (33), the LTI-plus-reset case: `sampled_noise` VALIDATED, no code change (Andreas: "Do the Sepke")

The closed-form external check the sampled-noise first cut deferred.  When a node is reset before a window of length `Tw`
and is LTI inside it, the one-sided sample-series PSD is `S(f) = Σ_n |H_w(f + n f0)|² CY(|f + n f0|)`, with the
windowed impulse response `H_w(f) = (1 − e^{−(a + j2πf)Tw}) / (C (a + j2πf))`, `a = G/C`.  Fixture: 1 nF, 1 MΩ (thermal
4kTG) and a 1/f `IS` at one node, reset through a NOISELESS switch (gon 10 mS, vs 10 µV) while a 1 kHz sine clock is above
0.95 (10 % of the period); radau; the reference uses the same ±50 sidebands and no fitted factor.  Named prediction before
the run: agreement to ~2e-3 at 1000 points.  Measured (worst over f = 10…500 Hz):

| points | instant | white (4kTG only) | with 1/f |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1000 | end of window (Tw 0.889 T) | 1e-5 | 2e-5 |
| 2000 | end of window | — | 9e-5 |
| 1000 | mid-window (Tw 0.449 T) | — | 6e-5 |
| 400 | end of window | 1.6e-4 | 4.9e-4 |
| 400 | mid-window | — | 1.2e-3 |

Better than predicted, so the controls came first.  The reference with the window ONE STEP longer is 4e-4…1e-2 off,
40–300× the residual.  The never-reset (stationary) RC reference is 0.13×…1.87× off, so the window itself is being
checked, not only the filter.  Not monotone in the grid (2000 points is no closer than 1000): the residual is set by
where the reset edge falls inside a step (sub-step window-start uncertainty), not by the integrator's order.
Test `test_the_sampled_psd_of_a_reset_rc_is_sepke_eq_33_white_and_one_over_f` (400 points, both instants; < 3e-3 and
both controls asserted).  Scope: eq. (33) needs a reset and an LTI window, so it says nothing about a bias-modulated
source (the PSP residual above); that part stays covered by the per-frequency time-average identity.

## 2026-09-16 — B3 (Aprille & Trick) BUILT as per-iterate phase re-selection; the substitution alone is the bordered solve; C3's 09-02 rejection withdrawn (Andreas: "Do B3")

Read A&T 1972 (TCT) §"The Autonomous System" first: unknown `v = [x_01 … T … x_0n]`, Newton `v ← v − B⁻¹H`, and
Step 3 re-selects `k` by `|f_k(x(Tⁱ))| = max` with `w_0k = x_0kⁱ` every iteration; Appendix I proves LOCAL convergence
only. Algebra before measurement: with `x_0k` held, the substitution's `B` is the bordered Jacobian with the phase
row eliminated, so a FROZEN-`k` substitution must reproduce the bordered iterates. Harness: the shipped autonomous
closure captured through an `fsolve` shim, three outer solvers on its top block. Named predictions: (1) frozen
substitution = bordered; (2) re-selection converges on-orbit (against 09-02's record); (3) re-selection rescues 4×,
not 10×/30×.

| van der Pol μ = 1, 6 seeds on a circle | on-orbit | 4× | 10× | 30× |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| bordered, frozen pin (shipped) | 6/6 | 0/6 | 0/6 | 0/6 |
| substitution, `k` frozen | 6/6 (iterates ≤ 1e-9 of bordered) | 0/6 | 0/6 | 0/6 |
| substitution frozen at re-selection's FIRST `k` (control) | 6/6 | 0/6 | 0/6 | 0/6 |
| **A&T re-selection** (trap x_in and x0 frames, radau, gear) | 6/6 | **6/6** | **2/6** | 0/6 |
| re-selection, matrix-free (trap, gear) | 6/6 | 4/6 | 2/6 / 0/6 | — |

(1) held; (2) held; (3) was too pessimistic at 10×. Q = 8 tank, series loss (algebraic node), 4×: 2/6 → 6/6 under
trap, gear (solved-history) and radau. Every frozen success is also a re-selected success. ⚠ At 10× some
re-selected runs "converge" to `T = 0` — the existing refusal catches them. ⚠ Where the two rules' periods differ
(trap, `x0_unknown=True`) it is trap's phase-dependent discrete orbit: on-orbit seeds at three angles give
6.663745 / 6.662827 / 6.663525 under EITHER rule, and a frozen solve re-seeded at a re-selected answer reproduces
its period to ≤ 1e-14.

**Why 09-02 failed (C3's record, withdrawn).** A first guess — the damped Newton's carried trial row going stale
under a re-selecting callback — was half refuted: it does not break the on-orbit seed (converges in 4–8), but it
DOES fail every 4× seed the consistent row solves. What reproduces the recorded on-orbit failure is a pin taken from
the unknown `x_in[k]` compared against the MANUFACTURED `x_0[k]` (40 iterations, not converged; consistent row: 8)
— the frame error later fixed for the frozen pin. So "the row carries no information, the orbit slides" was wrong.

**Built:** `PSS.solve(phase_rule='reselect' | 'frozen')`, **OPT-IN — the default stays `'frozen'`** (see the suite
outcome below, which is what decided it); `_phase_row` inside every autonomous
residual (plain, solved-history, full, DIRK, GLM, both matrix-free builders), a pure function of the iterate — no
callback, so the carried trial evaluation stays consistent. `grid_error` repeats the rule.

**Found by the targeted run, fixed:** `test_B16_…_twin_default_trbdf2` failed `(3, 400) == (3, 399)`. `_solve_twin`
passed `timestep = T/N` and `solve` floors `int(T/(T/N))`: re-selection moved T in its 15th digit (…325 → …316)
and `T/(T/400)` became 399.99999999999994 — the "same grid" twin ran on 399 points. Latent for any T; fixed with half
a step of slack (twin back on 400 points and bit-identical period 6.730691494904).
Test `test_the_phase_pin_is_reselected_every_iterate_and_rescues_a_far_seed`.

⚠⚠ **THE FULL SUITE MOVED THE DECISION: 6 failed / 3222 passed with `'reselect'` as the DEFAULT, and all six pass
with the old rule forced back — so the default is now `'frozen'` and re-selection ships opt-in.** The claim written
into this entry an hour earlier, "every frozen solve that converged also converged reselected", was drawn from two
fixtures and is FALSE. What the six were:

| failing test | what moved |
|---|---|
| `…state_reset_needs_no_saltation_but_grid_alignment_is_a_cliff` | `converged = False`: `Idtmod`, wrap exactly ON a grid point (1200 pts, `ic = 0`) |
| `test_the_stage_predictor_leaves_a_wrapping_state_alone` | the same fixture and the same failure |
| `…frequency_aware_ppv_is_the_ppv_at_dc_and_corners…` | plateau (mode content at 0.1 f0) 1.64e-6 against the pinned 2.45e-6 |
| `…ppv_takes_the_dense_spectrum_when_it_can_afford_it` | its NEUTER guard: the truncated path stopped failing (gap ratio 0.933) |
| `…truncated_lam2_is_gated_on_its_own_ritz_residual` | recorded starved-basis gap ratio −0.031 → −0.064 |
| `…ppv_is_invariant_to_the_newtons_inner_solver` | `lambda_2` dense vs matrix-free no longer bit-equal (1e-13) |

Two mechanisms, both measured rather than reasoned:

  * **The moving pin wanders on a discontinuous period map.** On the grid-aligned `Idtmod` wrap both rules pick the
    SAME `k` at the seed (row 1; the folded row is 3, and `|dphi/dT|` ties at 2.000e3 on rows 1 and 3), so it is not a
    bad first choice — the re-selection itself is what fails there, on the one map documented to have no derivative.
    The off-grid wrap (500 pts, `ic = 0.31`) converges under both.
  * **Re-selection lands on a different PHASE of the same orbit.** Slow-node van der Pol (`tau = 100 T`): the orbits
    agree to the digit — period 6.656833399 both, `v` ∈ [−1.99854, +1.9984], `w` ∈ ±3.2382e-3 — so this is not a
    different solution; but `frequency_aware_ppv`'s mode content at 0.1 f0, a surface this record already flags as
    definition-sensitive at that offset, reads 1.64e-6 against 2.45e-6.  ⚠ AND THE PIN ITSELF IS PHASE-SPECIFIC,
    measured under the FROZEN rule alone: six seeds around the same orbit give 2.4522 / 2.3161 / 1.9423 / 1.8306 /
    2.2461 / 2.4445 e-6, so the test's "2.45e-6 ±10 %" holds for the phase ITS seed lands on and nothing more.
    Re-selection's 1.6437e-6 sits just below that spread, at the phase where the slow node's near-singular
    coordinate is held at its seed value — a latent fragility in the test that a default flip would have exposed
    as a regression.
    ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-17 (Andreas: "Do phase-specific PPV plateau pin …").** Re-measured on a fresh seed sweep
    (1.5 / 1.75 / 2.0 / 2.25 / 2.5, all on the same orbit — period 6.656833 and |μ₂| 0.990059 identical, orbit
    min/max to four digits, `v(0)` drifting 1.9014 → 1.8952): the plateau reads 2.2563 … 2.6410e-6, a **17 % span**,
    while **the T/τ RATIO `plateau_10/plateau_100` reads 10.349 / 10.350 / 10.351 / 10.352 / 10.353 — a 0.0 % spread**,
    because both plateaus are read at the same phase and the phase factor cancels. So the ratio is the phase-invariant
    quantity and it was already the second assert. The absolute pin is now the **interval [1.3e-6, 3.0e-6]**, which
    clears every phase on record (1.6437 … 2.641e-6) by ≥1.13× at both ends and still rejects a wrong POWER of T/τ
    (the τ/T = 10 plateau, 2.3e-5, is 7.7× outside). ⚠ A first rewrite used `2.2e-6 ±25 %` = [1.65, 2.695]e-6, whose
    lower edge sat **0.08 % above the reselect reading 1.6437e-6** — a knife edge, caught before it shipped: that is
    why the pin is an interval and not a centre±percent. ⚠ The ratio tolerance was NOT tightened onto the measured
    10.35: whether that 3.5 % above 10 is physical or a 240-point grid effect is unestablished, and pinning it would
    fix an unexplained number. ⚠ `seed = 3.0` does not converge at either τ/T, so the usable seed range is bounded.
    The two Arnoldi/Ritz pins move for the same
    reason, and the dense-vs-matrix-free `lambda_2` equality breaks because the two paths choose `k` from
    last-ulp-different `dphi/dT`. ⚠ A first control was misread: "frozen re-seeded at reselect's answer" returns to
    FROZEN's own phase, so it never isolated phase — the orbit comparison above is what settles it.

So `'reselect'` is the documented remedy for a far seed (`tstab` is the other, and they compose), not the default.
The `_solve_twin` grid fix stands on its own and is covered by the forced-frozen rerun.

## 2026-09-16 — E1 (GLM PCNR stage path) BUILT; E2's premise checked and found WRONG (Andreas: "Do E1/E2")

**The first measurement made E1 a defect, not a gap.** Before writing anything: a diode rectifier under
`pcnr=True`, counting PCNR solves against `Diode.limit` calls — gear2 4872 / 1, radau 16596 / 49789 (its documented
per-stage sync), **glm3 3 / 39987 with `pcnr_status == 'used'`**. So a GLM run did device limiting and reported that
it had used PCNR. The 3 solves are the startup's Radau substeps through `_rk_step_coupled`; the forced-failure
control below reproduces exactly 3, which is what confirms that reading rather than leaving it a guess.

**Built:** a GLM stage IS `_rk_stage_pcnr`'s form — `q(Y_i) − target − h a_ii K_i = 0` over `h a_ii` gives
`i(Y) + iq_eff + u = 0` — and every shipped tableau is DIRK-like with one non-zero diagonal (0.25 / 0.25 / 0.258,
all stages equal), so unlike an ESDIRK there is no explicit stage lacking an `h a_ii`. `_solve_timestep_glm` routes
each stage through it, with the DIRK path's per-stage fallback to `self._newton` (PCNR carries no continuation
ladder; the limiting solve does). After: one solve per stage per step, `limit()` down to the one sync per solve,
**device evaluations 0.34–0.38×** of the limiting run. Forced-failure control: run completes, 16196 fallbacks,
`status='partial'`, waveform bit-identical to device limiting.

⚠⚠ **MY CORRECTNESS GATE WAS REFUTED, AND THE REFUTATION IS THE USEFUL PART.** I predicted "matches device limiting
to near machine precision on a fixed grid", from the architecture doc's 2.8e-17 / 7.5e-17. On a 1001-step fixed grid
(identical step sequences, verified) at reltol 1e-9:

| path | PCNR vs device limiting |
|---|---|
| glm3 | 6.2e-9 |
| **esdirk43** (shipped, documented) | **1.6e-8** |
| trbdf2 | 8.4e-11 |
| radau (COUPLED) | 0.0 |

So the gap is not a GLM property: every SEQUENTIAL stage path shows it, and the doc's figure came from a different
comparison (a 160-step grid at reltol 1e-10, where it does hold). Two hypotheses were named before measuring —
(H1) the two solvers stop at different points of the same equation, (H2) my wiring solves a different system. **H1,
decisively:** the glm3 gap tracks `reltol` — 1.2e-6 / 6.2e-9 / 7.9e-16 at 1e-6 / 1e-9 / 1e-12.

⚠ **GLM4 was the one outlier and the control exonerated the solver.** It stalled at 1.0e-8 even at reltol 1e-12.
With PCNR nowhere in sight, glm4's own floor is 8.2e-9 (stage predictor on vs off) and 1.3e-8 (reltol 1e-12 vs
1e-13), against ~1.4e-15 for GLM2/GLM3 — a fresh number for the recorded "badly scaled tableau" (B ~2500, two
abscissae outside [0,1]). The test therefore asserts agreement on GLM2/GLM3 and records glm4's floor instead of
pinning it.

Test `test_pcnr_is_the_glm_stage_limiting_too_and_says_so_truthfully` (passes here, fails on unchanged HEAD).
Architecture doc scoreboard 3-for-4 → 4-for-4.

**E2: the roadmap entry's premise is false.** "GLM is the one integrator class not on this branch's own backend" —
`jaxtransient.py` mentions radau/trbdf2/esdirk **zero** times and refuses anything but `'gear'`/`'euler'`/`'trap'`
in three places, and the parity ledger's P6 row lists the same three. No stage or multivalue method is on that
backend, so GLM would be the FIRST, and the Nordsieck `r·m` question is the second problem rather than the first.
⚠ `JAXTransient`'s docstring still says "CPU-only: nothing, as of 2026-09-01", which cannot be squared with those
refusals — one of the two records is wrong and this one was checked. Left as a decision: the backend's purpose is
`solve_batched`, a sequential multi-stage step with a per-stage Newton is the shape the `lax.cond` lesson punished,
and GLM4 is already 2.4–4.7× slower than radau on the CPU at equal grid.

### An unimprovable Newton step was committed in SILENCE — peer report, two retractions, and a diagnosis fix (Andreas: "Fix it" / "Do as you suggest")

**The report, and why its headline is withdrawn.** A peer session reported "the free-period shooting Jacobian for
`method='trap'` is not the derivative of its residual — the step is an ASCENT direction and the solve wanders". The
mismatch is real and I reproduced it (phase row 0 against 7.86e-3 by central differences; state block off ~1.9e3 at
weak damping; period column correct to 1e-4; `x0_unknown=True` agrees at 5.1e-05). But it is **not a defect**:
lines 1784–1811 of `shooting.py` have recorded since 2026-09-02 that `J_code` is not an approximation to
`dF/dx_in` at all — that derivative is SINGULAR on every circuit measured (σ_min exactly 0, rank 1/3, 2/4, 1/3) —
so an exact Newton for this formulation does not exist and `I − dx_end/dx_0` approximates a *different*,
well-posed derivative. That is why the plain path is a contraction (ratio ~0.076), not a Newton. **The peer
withdrew the headline** once shown the passage.

**My own two retractions, both caught by measurement.** (1) I first read gear's Jacobian as disagreeing (0.34) — it
was my finite-difference step, `1e-7·|z_j|` with no floor, collapsing on a near-zero `x_{-1}` component; with a
floor gear agrees at 8.7e-06. The peer had hit the identical trap in their own first attempt. (2) I reported an
ascent-direction reproduction from fixtures with **loss ≥ gain**: (μ=1e-3, rs=2e-3) settles to pk-pk 0.85 after
400 periods and is still shrinking — no limit cycle, so both frames "failed" because there was nothing to converge
to. Then my first rebuild of the peer's own fixture had the negative resistance backwards (`−a u + b u³` instead of
`a u − b u³`), damping the tank to the origin. An envelope gate in front of every fixture now catches both.

**What survives, reproduced independently on the peer's λ₂ = 0.99 tank** (their circuit, rebuilt pycircuit-only;
their switch sits at a fixed control voltage so it is the gl = 1e-4 S loss, written as a resistor; fixture
validated first at amplitude 1.0 flat to 1e-5 over 85 periods):

| frame | budget | status | ‖F‖ | worst J err | direction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| default (x_in) | 25 | noconv | 6.765e-07 | 1.000 | +3.9e-14 **UP** |
| default (x_in) | 200 | noconv | 9.011e-07 | 1.000 | +1.5e-14 **UP** |
| `x0_unknown=True` | 25 | noconv | 3.069e-05 | 6.8e-07 | −9.4e-10 |
| `x0_unknown=True` | 200 | noconv | 3.069e-05 | 5.9e-07 | −9.4e-10 |

The peer's drift-up reproduces to three digits (6.77e-07 → 9.01e-07 theirs). The step is uphill against the true
derivative at both budgets, and four halvings cannot cure an uphill direction. ⚠ **The x₀ frame is not a fix**: it
makes the Jacobian the actual derivative and the step a descent direction, yet ‖F‖ plateaus — identical at 25 and
200 — and the solve still fails. That answers the row the peer could not capture. ⚠ `tstab` (50 periods) does not
rescue it either, and neither does refining **in** that frame: x₀ frame at 1600 points with a 200-iteration budget
is still non-converged (T +1.4e-06 rel, amplitude 1.99999). **`radau` does**: 25 iterations, period
6.2831863119e-07, amplitude to five digits. So the remedy list on that fixture is x0_unknown ✗, tstab ✗, finer x₀
grid ✗, radau ✓ — which is exactly what the message steers to, and why it steers there rather than to a knob.

**So the defect is the SILENCE, and that is what was fixed.** `analysis.fsolve` commits a step the line search
could not improve — there is no other candidate — and then scores convergence against `F` at the *previous* point,
so a climbing solve left no trace: no counter, no warning. It now counts those iterations and reports them in the
`infodict` it already returned and every caller discarded (`ls_unimproved`); `_free_period_solve` passes it to
`_diagnose_lmm_free_period_stall`, which names the count. **Strictly observational — no trajectory changes**, and
deliberately so: the peer measured a case that climbs for ~90 iterations and then converges by 200, so refusing the
step would turn a slow success into a failure.

⚠ **And the message's own over-claim is narrowed.** It asserted that raising iterations, damping or tolerances
"does not help" — measured for GEAR's solved-history stall, then written as though it held for every multistep
free-period solve. It does not: the peer's trap case at λ₂ = 0.9 converges with a 200-iteration budget, and the van
der Pol here converges at the default 25. The line-search count is now the discriminator the message names.

Test `test_an_unimprovable_step_is_counted_and_named_instead_of_committed_in_silence` (passes here, fails on
unchanged HEAD in 1.0 s — there is no `ls_unimproved` key there). Measured on the tank: **24 of 25** steps
unimprovable.

⚠ **THE PREMISE WAS DISPUTED AND THEN CONFIRMED, which is why it is worth stating twice.** The peer first recorded
"`conv_f` scores `F` at the previous point, not the trial" as REFUTED — reasoning that the cached trial
(analysis.py:380) is consumed at the top of the next iteration (351–352), so the test must score the accepted
point. That answers a different question. Traced variable by variable in `a5e192b`: `F` is assigned only at
350/352, `Ft` is never assigned into `F`, `conv_f` at 390 tests `abs(F)`, and `x0 = x` happens afterwards at 397 —
so within an iteration the test scores the residual at the point being LEFT, and the returned `x` is one step
beyond the point whose residual met the tolerance. They withdrew the refutation. Both of their framing errors in
this thread had the same mechanism (reading the code plausibly instead of tracing the variable), and both of mine
had another (a fixture that could not carry the claim). Worth keeping as a pair.

### A8 opened: the additive edge-jitter number, measured on the DRIVEN buffer chain (Andreas: "start with A8 as you suggest")

**Reconnaissance only — no source touched.** Three findings, then the number.

⚠ **MY OWN "CHEAP FIRST STEP" WAS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN.** I proposed evaluating `sampled_noise` at the
oscillator's threshold crossings. `_sampled_series` refuses an autonomous PSS by design: *"an OSCILLATOR has no
sampling instant fixed to its own phase — its phase diffuses, so there is no cyclostationary sample series."*

✅ **THE OSCILLATOR SIDE IS ALREADY COVERED, AND THE n-SCALING PROVES IT INDEPENDENTLY.** `K(t₀+nT) = K_orb +
n·d·u·uᵀ` contracted on the output row and divided by slew² gives `σ_t²(n) = [A + nB]/slew²`. Measured on the
suite's own Q=8 fixture: slope per period 3.928306e-07 s² → `c_equiv = 6.251420e-08` s against the shipped
`c = 6.248352e-08` s, **ratio 1.0005** — a second route to `c`. The intercept is bounded (A = 2.943820e-07 s²)
and the ratio to `√(c·n·T)` falls 1.3230 → 1.0006 as the walk takes over.

✅ **THE ADDITIVE NUMBER IS NOT IN THE OSCILLATOR'S COVARIANCE AT ALL** — A8's entry says so in its own table
(*"the buffer chain's own thermal noise; the LDO modulating each inverter's delay"*). Given the waveform, the
chain is a DRIVEN LPTV system, and `sampled_noise` works on driven circuits. Three tanh buffers into RC loads,
`gm·R = 0.5`, 4e-21 A²/Hz per stage, f₀ = 1 MHz → **σ_t = 8.27e-11 s** at the last crossing, band [1 kHz, 500 kHz].
Gated in the suite as `test_the_additive_edge_jitter_is_method_independent_and_nothing_manufactures_its_floor`.

⚠⚠ **THREE CLAIMS FROM THE FIRST PASS OF THIS SECTION ARE WITHDRAWN.** All three were measured, all three were
wrong, and each survived because the check that should have caught it was built in a way that could only agree.
They come before the surviving numbers because the pattern is the lesson.

**(1) The headline 5.712532e-11 s was GRID-BOUND, not an answer.** The sample series folds every sideband up to
the GRID's Nyquist. With `R = 1e3, C = 2e-12` the RC pole sits at 80 f₀, far above the fold, so the time grid —
not the circuit — decided how much of the noise tail was counted. Evidence: at npts 1600 the folded variance is
3.38e-07 V² at `maxsidebands = 10` against 1.83e-06 at 400, so a ten-sideband read captures **18 %** of the
answer; and σ_t climbed +14.66 / +8.71 / +4.89 % per grid doubling over npts 200 → 1600, leaving the quoted
figure about 18 % under its own extrapolated limit. **A number that moves with the grid is a discretisation
artefact.** The fix is a fixture whose own pole lies below the fold: `C = 2e-10` (pole 0.80 f₀) settles to
+0.24 %, then +0.14 % per doubling.

**(2) The "242× sources-OFF floor" was RESISTOR THERMAL NOISE — not the method's floor, and not the Biggio
control I claimed to have run.** Setting the injected `IS` PSDs to zero leaves the resistors noisy: `R.CY` is
`4kT/r`, which at 1 kΩ is 1.657607e-23 A²/Hz against the injected 4e-21. I switched off one set of sources, left
another on, and reported the remainder as an instrument floor. The ratio is exactly predicted as physics —
`1 + PSD/(4kT/R)` = 1 + 241.312 = **242.312** against 242.311695 measured. ✅ The real control is *better* than
the claim it replaces: `R` takes `noisy=False`, and with every source silent the variance is **exactly 0.0 V²**.
The method manufactures no floor at all, and the contributions are additive to 2.2e-16.

**(3) "σ_t·slew constant to 0.9 %" was VACUOUS.** σ_t is *defined* here as `√var/|slew|`, so `σ_t·|slew| ≡ √var`
identically — residual measured at 1.4e-20 and 0.0. The check never tested `σ_t ∝ 1/slew`; it asked only whether
the output variance moved when the drive was turned down, and on the saturating first fixture (stages clipped at
~0.87 pk-pk) it did not. On a fixture where the drive genuinely moves the operating point, the same quantity
drifts 24 %. ⚠ By the same token `PSD×4 → 1.99690` is **arithmetic, not evidence**: given exact linearity in the
source PSDs, `var(4·PSD) = 4·var_inj + var_rest` is an identity, and 1.996902 follows from the resistor share
alone. Both are decent consistency checks; neither could ever have failed.

✅ **WHAT REPLACES THEM: A CROSS-FAMILY GATE, WHICH TWO GRIDS OF ONE METHOD CANNOT GIVE.** gear-2 declares this
fixture unresolved (LTE 5.11e+06 × tolerance at 400 points, 1.29e+06 at 800) — and radau, which reports nothing
at any grid tried, lands on the same number anyway:

| method | npts | σ_t [s] |
|---|---|---|
| radau | 100 / 200 / 400 | 8.249493e-11 / 8.266431e-11 / 8.274739e-11 |
| gear | 400 / 800 | 8.263152e-11 / 8.275126e-11 |
| trap | 400 | 8.268268e-11 |

That is 0.17 % across three families and a 4× grid range, with radau 400 and gear 800 agreeing to 0.005 %. The
gear warning is itself mostly the `reltol` chosen: LTE/tol runs 5.11e6 → 5.98e4 → 600 as reltol goes 1e-11 →
1e-9 → 1e-7, while σ_t is unchanged to seven digits.

⚠ **WHAT IS STILL NOT VALIDATED, and it is the honest limit of this result:** converting a variance to a time by
the local slew is a **definition** here, not a measured jitter. Nothing in this section checks that the crossing
instants of a noisy transient actually scatter by σ_t. That needs Monte Carlo over injected time-domain noise —
machinery that does not exist (`_vdp_injected` is injection LOCKING, a deterministic `ISin`) — and it is now
A8's named next step rather than something this number is allowed to imply.

**SCOPE, stated so the next reader does not overclaim this:** the route covers the buffers' own WHITE noise under
an ideal periodic drive. It does **not** cover the LDO modulating each inverter's delay — bias-dependent and
non-stationary, outside the modulated-stationary support — and the oscillator's share is `c`, already shipped.
Those two now live in **A11**, carved out so that A8 does not quietly become them.

⚠⚠ **EIGHT CONVENTION ERRORS, ALL MINE, AGAINST INTERFACES THAT DOCUMENT THEMSELVES** — recorded because the
pattern matters more than any one of them: state-indexed vector contracted against pair-space matrices; `u` and
`v` taken from two different calls with different normalisations (`v·u` = −0.1687 vs `pair_inner` = +0.6661, a
uniform −3.949 rescaling); a rank-one 4×4 growth matrix read as a vector via `ravel()[:n]`; the docstring's
*superseded* prescription (`P − (t/T)G`) followed from prose when the test's executable route C says the opposite;
`BSource` terminal pairs reversed (`terminals = ('inp','inn','outp','outn')`, so each stage was controlled by its
own output and drove backwards — every node 0.0000 pk-pk); and full-versus-reduced output indexing **twice**
(`waveform` rows are the full MNA vector; `_output_waveform_row` takes a REDUCED index and lifts it itself, so a
full index addressed the source's branch current and returned 0.0 for sources ON *and* OFF — an all-zeros result
that is a mis-addressed query, not a floor). The library was right every time. Two procedural fixes now stand in
every A8 harness: a **late-vs-later envelope gate** in front of each fixture, and an **assertion that the resolved
output row is the node I think it is** — the second caught the last silent zero and turned it into a refusal.

### A8's anchor: a linear stage with a CLOSED FORM, after the peer named what the cross-family gate could not do

The cross-family gate above says the number belongs to the circuit rather than to an integrator. It cannot say
the number is **right** — three families can agree on a wrong value, and the three-tanh chain has no analytic
value at any grid to check against.

⚠ **THE GAP WAS NAMED BY THE PEER SESSION** (`test-pycircuit-spectre-e2`), auditing their own sampled `kT/C`
against the grid-bandwidth warning: *"our number has a CLOSED FORM to sit against… a quantity 1.4 % from its own
closed form cannot be drifting by tens of percent with the grid without that showing up as disagreement with the
physics, not just with the other tool. Your σ_t had no such anchor, which is exactly why grid refinement was the
only thing that could have caught it."* Correct, and it generalises past this item.

✅ **SO A8 GETS ONE.** A single LINEAR stage — `i = gm·v_in` into `R‖C`, no tanh — has an analytic everything.
White current sources `S = S_inj + 4kT/R` into `R‖C` give `var = S·R/(4C)` over all frequencies, of which the
resistor's own share is exactly `kT/C`; the flat series PSD puts `1 − fmin/(f0/2)` of it inside the band; and the
fold reaches only the **grid's** Nyquist, so `captured = (2/π)·arctan(f_N/f_c)`.

⚠ **THE DEFICIT WAS NAMED BEFORE IT WAS MEASURED** (the pre-commitment rule, and the same mechanism that
produced the withdrawn headline — here in its benign, quantified form): `(2/π)·f_c/f_N` predicts **2.533e-03 at
npts 400 and 1.266e-03 at 800**, halving per doubling.

| quantity | measured | closed form | agreement |
|---|---|---|---|
| variance, npts 400 | 4.999763312e-09 V² | 4.997986519e-09 V² | **1.0003555** |
| variance, npts 800 | 5.006181237e-09 V² | 5.004332559e-09 V² | **1.0003694** |
| untruncated deficit | 2.178e-03 → 8.976e-04 | 2.533e-03 → 1.266e-03 | ratio **0.412**, halving as predicted |
| slew | 1.956176e+06 V/s | `2πf₀A_out` = 1.956197e+06 | 1.1e-05 |
| σ_t | 3.614654e-11 s | 3.618558e-11 s | 0.11 % (itself the truncation) |

So the variance is right in ABSOLUTE terms to 3.7e-04, and the residual that remains is a mechanism named in
advance rather than a tolerance chosen afterwards. Test
`test_the_edge_jitter_of_a_linear_stage_matches_its_closed_form_and_the_grid_truncation_it_names`, which also
asserts the truncation term is **doing work** — dropping it puts the coarse grid outside the tolerance the
corrected form passes, so the correction cannot quietly become decoration.

⚠ **WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO:** it anchors the VARIANCE and the SLEW, not the claim that crossings scatter by σ_t.
That gap is closed in the next section.

### A8's last gap: σ_t STOPS BEING A DEFINITION — the crossings really do scatter by it (Andreas: "continue on a8")

Everything above computes `σ_t := √var/|slew|`, and both the roadmap and the tests said plainly that this is a
**definition**, not a measured jitter. This is the measurement.

✅ **POOLED OVER 9 SEEDS × 3 GRIDS (npts 200/400/800) AND 3528 CROSSINGS: `σ_t(MC)/closed form = 1.01099 ±
0.01156`, consistent with 1.000 at 0.95σ.** A factor of 2 in the variance is excluded at 26–35σ, a factor of 4 at
44–86σ. ⚠ The error model checks itself: the seed spread (0.03467) matches `1/√(2N)` for N = 392 crossings
(0.03571) to 3 %, so the scatter is sampling noise rather than a systematic. Gated in the suite as
`test_the_edge_jitter_is_the_scatter_of_a_noisy_transients_crossings_not_just_a_variance` (one seed, 192
crossings, 5.1 % sampling error — it gates the FACTOR at 5.3σ, not the 1 %).

⚠⚠ **PHASE 1 DELIBERATELY MEASURED NO JITTER AT ALL, because of the 17th shape in the record.** An MC that
injects noise with the same one-sided/two-sided convention the analysis assumes cannot validate that analysis —
that pair once agreed to **0.9965 while both were 2× wrong**. So phase 1 measured `kT/C` on an RC, whose value
neither route can influence. Backward Euler gives a closed form to predict first, `Var = (kT/C)/(1 + h/2τ)`:
predicted 0.993789, measured **1.005742 ± 0.014891**, with the rival `σ_i² = S/h` reading (1.987578) rejected at
~66σ. A zero-amplitude control returned exactly 0.0, since a dead source and a correct answer are otherwise
indistinguishable. ⚠ The O(h/τ) term (0.6 %) is BELOW that run's 1.5 % precision and comparable to `np.var`'s low
bias on a correlated series — so only the factor of 2 is claimed, not the correction.

⚠⚠ **TWO FAILURES OF MINE ON THE WAY, both instructive, both now gated.**

**(1) A HARNESS WHOSE CONTROLS FAILED THEIR OWN CRITERIA — and one of them fed the measurement.** Run from
