`x0 = 0` with no burn-in: "noiseless crossing spacing = T" came out 473 ppm off (read literally, a drift of
**13× σ_t per period**, which would have swamped the result); "noiseless scatter ≪ σ_t" came out **41× σ_t**;
pk-pk read +5.4 % of analytic. The third was not cosmetic — that record's MIDPOINT was the crossing threshold for
the noisy runs, and an offset threshold moves the crossing off the steepest point, showing up as a slew 0.9948 of
analytic: about half of the deficit I would otherwise have reported. The fix was also a simplification: the
output is a filtered sine with no DC path, so the threshold is **exactly 0.0**, analytic rather than measured.
After moving every control past burn-in: spacing = T to **2.15e-13**, floor **7.9e-19 s = 0.0000 of σ_t**, drift
~2e-14 s/period.

**(2) A REFINEMENT TEST TOO COARSE TO SEE WHAT IT LOOKED FOR, and the "1.2 % deficit" it was chasing is
WITHDRAWN.** The first corrected run gave 0.9881 and I quoted it as `± 0.8 %`, taking the sem from three seeds
that happened to agree; the true per-seed error is `1/√(2·392)` = **3.6 %**. I then predicted the deficit would
fall as 2.8/1.4/0.7 % across npts 200/400/800 — grids whose own sampling error is 2.1 % each. The result was
non-monotone (1.00207 / 0.98602 / 1.04491, the FINEST grid furthest from 1), which is not discretisation but
noise. Pooled properly it is 0.95σ from unity. ⚠ **This is "a null measured on a grid coarser than the effect",
in the time domain: I set up a trend test whose error bars were the size of the trend.** What *is* clean is the
deterministic control beside it — slew/analytic runs 0.992398 → 0.996171 → 0.998086, halving per refinement, a
textbook O(h) confirmation showing the harness resolves h-effects and it is the σ statistic alone that is noisy.

⚠ **SCOPE, unchanged:** this validates the conversion on a LINEAR stage under white noise. A11 (non-stationary
LDO delay modulation, and `ρ_k` across periods for the three jitter metrics) is untouched and remains weeks of
work with Demir 1996 as its starting point.

### A11 opened, and half of it was never Demir's: `ρ_k` is a cosine transform of something that already ships (Andreas: "Do a11")

⚠⚠ **THE ENTRY I WROTE FOR A11 THIS MORNING WAS WRONG, AND BY A WIDE MARGIN.** It said `ρ_k` — the correlation
between crossing errors k periods apart — is what "this route cannot supply", filed it under Demir 1996, and
called the whole item weeks of work. The conflation: a single-instant sampled **variance** supplies none of it
(that part is right), but **`sampled_noise` returns the one-sided PSD of the SAMPLE SERIES `y(t₀+kT)`**, in its
own docstring's words — and the autocovariance of a series is the cosine transform of its spectrum:

`R_k = ∫ S(f;t₀)·cos(2πf·kT) df`,  `ρ_k = R_k/R_0`

So for a DRIVEN chain the whole of `ρ_k`, and with it the k-cycle and cycle-to-cycle metrics, follows from
machinery that already shipped and was already validated. The measurement took an afternoon, not weeks.

✅ **BUILT: `PAC.jitter_metrics`** — `σ_t`, `ρ_k`, k-cycle `√(2(R_0−R_k))/|s|`, cycle-to-cycle
`√(6R_0−8R_1+2R_2)/|s|` (coefficients `[1,−2,1]` on the second difference).

⚠ **THE FIXTURE WAS CHOSEN SO THE ANSWER IS KNOWN, NOT MERELY CROSS-CHECKED**, and A8's own fixture could not
have done this job: at `τ = 0.2T` the first correlation is `e⁻⁵` = 0.0067, far below the ~0.03 floor of the
~1000 crossings that would check it — **a quantity beneath the floor of the instrument that would test it is
not a test**. Moving the pole to `τ = RC = T` on the one LINEAR stage (LTI noise path: gm, R, C constant, the
drive only sets the waveform) makes the sampled series exactly AR(1), so `ρ_k = e^{−k}` in closed form.

| k | analytic `e^{−k}` | cosine transform | Monte Carlo (1176 crossings) | MC σ off |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0.367879 | 0.368067 | 0.346404 | 0.74σ |
| 2 | 0.135335 | 0.135404 | 0.131894 | 0.12σ |
| 3 | 0.049787 | 0.049813 | — below the MC floor | — |
| 4 | 0.018316 | 0.018325 | — below the MC floor | — |

Ratio **1.0005 at every lag** — a constant offset, not one growing with k — and the MC (no PSS, no adjoint, no
spectrum anywhere in it) agrees within 1σ at every lag it can resolve, with `σ_t` matching at 0.9901.

✅ **THE BAND QUESTION IS SETTLED BY MEASUREMENT.** With the `[0,fmin)` rectangle added back, `ρ_k` is identical
to four digits over a **100× range of fmin** (50 → 0.5 Hz); without it the error grows with k exactly as
truncation should (0.9889 of analytic at k = 4, recovering to 0.9994 at fmin/10). ⚠ The rectangle assumes the
series PSD is flat below fmin — exact for white, **wrong for 1/f** — so it is OFF by default, consistent with
`sampled_variance` refusing to extrapolate. With a coloured source, lower fmin instead.

⚠ **THE ALGEBRA CHECK THAT MATTERS**: on an uncorrelated fixture (`τ = 0.02T`, ρ → 0) the two metrics must
collapse to `√2·σ_t` and `√6·σ_t`. Measured 1.4142 and 2.4495 — exact. Second-difference coefficients are easy
to get wrong in a way no correlated fixture would reveal.

⚠⚠ **AN ERROR OF MINE WORTH THE SPACE: I COMPARED A DIFFERENCE AGAINST A SLOPE.** Guarding against a caller
asking for jitter at a waveform PEAK, I measured the peak's central difference as −8.86e-07 and read it as a
slope against the steepest slope 4.94e+05 — a ratio of 1.8e-12 — and set the guard at 1e-6. But −8.86e-07 is an
undivided difference; over `2h` = 5e-9 the slope is **−176.7**, i.e. **3.58e-04** of the steepest. Worse, on a
400-point grid 3.58e-04 is the *smallest ratio any instant can have*, because no grid point lands exactly on a
peak — so **any relative-slope threshold is a statement about the grid** and moves with N. The guard is now on
the grid-independent thing: the LINEARISATION. A crossing is displaced by `δy/slew` only while that displacement
stays small against the period, so `σ_t ≥ T/2` is refused. At this fixture's peak `σ_t` = 0.179 T, so 100× the
source PSD puts it past T/2 and the test proves the guard fires. The earlier `slew == 0.0` guard was unreachable
and therefore never a guard at all. **Same shape as the campaign's "correct number under a wrong name".**

**WHAT IS LEFT OF A11 IS THE LDO, AND ONLY THE LDO** — bias-dependent, non-stationary, outside the
modulated-stationary support. Still weeks, still Demir 1996 as the starting point.

### A11 autonomous reconnaissance: additive edge jitter on an oscillator, and four instrument failures (Andreas: "Continue")

⚠⚠ **THE SCOPE SENTENCE IS WITHDRAWN BY BOTH OF US.** A11 said the remaining half was "bias-dependent,
non-stationary delay modulation… outside the modulated-stationary support". Andreas 2026-09-17: *"Agree that my
mentioning of non-stationary is wrong. Is addative edge jitter on an autonomous circuit."* A8's original entry
had already settled it — the LDO is injected as a **supply-node noise source** in *"one ordinary autonomous PSS…
no multirate anything"*. I had invented a support problem from a plausible physical story without checking the
entry that already answered it.

**THE REAL GAP**: `sampled_noise` refuses autonomous PSS, so the non-accumulating jitter at the last buffer has
no route; `c` is only the walk. The split already ships — `K(t_j+nT) = P(t_j) + n d u_j u_jᵀ` — so the additive
number is the bounded part, **obliquely projected**: `σ_t² = eᵀ Π P(t_j) Πᵀ e / slew²`,
`Π = I − u_j v_jᵀ/(v_jᵀu_j)`.

✅ **ESTABLISHED**: a converging fixture (vdP tank + 3 tanh buffers, `τ_buf = 0.5`); `c_from_growth/c = 0.998992`
with `d_residual` 5.49e-12 at m = 5; the analysis side converging to `2A` ≈ 1.066e-06 (1.023870 → 1.055112 →
1.062936e-06 at npts 240/480/960). ⚠ `τ_buf = 0.1` makes the frozen free-period Jacobian singular (T/τ = 66.6);
`reselect` rescues it onto the **same orbit** (period to 4.44e-16). ⚠ The library's "seed BELOW the fundamental"
message is **wrong for this failure** — the seed was the measured period and the bare tank converges from it.

⚠⚠ **NOT VALIDATED, AND THE REASON IS THE INSTRUMENT.** The MC's seed-to-seed spread is 17–41 %. Two seeds at
npts 960 average 1.0032 of the analysis value — but that is 1.00 ± 0.06, and the same seeds one grid coarser give
1.1375. Confirming at 3 % needs ~45 seeds ≈ 3 h. **Reporting 1.0032 would have been the day's worst error.**

⚠⚠ **FOUR INSTRUMENT FAILURES, ALL MINE, AND THE PATTERN IS THE POINT — every one mimicked the phenomenon:**

1. **A SEED ON THE EQUILIBRIUM, TWICE.** `x0 = zeros` is van der Pol's fixed point, so the transient returned a
   dead waveform and "0 crossings". Both of my early "the fixture doesn't oscillate" readings were that.
2. **AN ADAPTIVE RUN READ AS FIXED-STEP.** Without `fixed_timestep=True` the solver returns non-uniform points;
   reconstructing `t = k·h` gave "the transient stopped at 15.193 of 80.0". It had not stopped — I mislabelled
   its time axis. A8's `t = k·h` was valid *only* because that harness passed the flag.
3. **A SELF-DEFEATING EXPERIMENTAL DESIGN.** To make the intercept dominant I shrank the tank noise 1000×. But
   the phase variance is exactly what the projection removes, so the projected-vs-unprojected discriminator
   collapsed from 15.8 % to **0.02 %** — the change that made the signal measurable destroyed the thing being
   measured.
4. ⚠⚠ **A STRADDLING DIFFERENCE IS NOT A SLEW ESTIMATOR, AND IT WAS WRONG ON BOTH SIDES.** `(v[j+1]−v[j−1])/2h`
   samples whichever pair happens to bracket the crossing, and which pair that is jumps with `h` — it read
   −0.720 %, −0.077 %, **+2.028 %** across refinement, changing sign while period and swing converged cleanly.
   Interpolating the crossing and taking a local quadratic's slope gives 1.527458 / 1.527793 / 1.528023 at npts
   240/480/960 — flat. So the PSS slew I had used throughout, **1.486101, was wrong by 2.8 %**, inflating every
   `var/slew²` by 5.6 %. I first blamed the Euler orbit for the discrepancy; it was my estimator on the *PSS*
   side.

⚠ **AND A FIFTH, IN THE FIT ITSELF**: the first MC regressed `Var(Δ_k)` over k = 8…24 to dodge `ρ_k`, where
overlapping differences leave ~N/k ≈ 6 effective samples. It returned a slope 43 % high and an intercept of
**−1.26e-06 — a negative variance**, which is impossible and is the only reason I caught it. Same shape as A8's
refinement sweep earlier the same day: *a fit whose window is where the instrument is weakest*.

### A11 BUILT: `PAC.oscillator_edge_jitter`, and two more of my own numbers corrected (Andreas: "push continue with A11")

✅ **BUILT** — the additive, non-accumulating edge jitter of a free-running oscillator, which `c` does not
contain and which `jitter_metrics` cannot reach (`sampled_noise` refuses autonomous PSS by design).
`σ_t² = eᵀ Π P(t_j) Πᵀ e / s²`, `Π = I − u_j v_jᵀ/(v_jᵀ u_j)`, with `P` and the rank-one `growth_samples[j]` from
`oscillator_covariance(samples=True)`. Test
`test_the_additive_edge_jitter_of_an_oscillator_is_the_projected_bounded_covariance` (10.7 s).

**VALIDATED — AND THE 4σ "DEFECT" I COMMITTED IN BETWEEN WAS MY OWN HARNESS.** Final, over **124** seed-runs on
three grids, comparing like with like:

`MC/analysis = 1.0066 ± 0.0102` — **0.64σ from 1.000**

⚠⚠ **THE RETRACTION.** An 80-seed campaign gave `1.0571 ± 0.0141` and I committed it (`7abd2ea`) as "the route
under-predicts the measured crossing scatter by ~5.7 %, grid-independent, therefore on the analysis side". It was
the **comparison** that was wrong. `σ_t = √var/slew`, so it goes as `1/slew²` — and the Monte Carlo finds its
crossings on an **Euler** orbit while the analysis divides by the PSS's **gear** slope. At finite `h` those
orbits differ: Euler's period is 0.39 % short and its slope at the crossing is low by **2.717 / 1.361 / 0.686 %**
at npts 240/480/960, halving exactly as first-order convergence requires. Squared: predicted ratios
**1.0566 / 1.0278 / 1.0139**, with the 960 value pinned *before* its seeds ran.

| npts | raw | ÷ measured slew correction | σ from 1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 240 (n=64) | 1.0493 ± 0.0162 | **0.9931 ± 0.0153** | 0.45 |
| 480 (n=44) | 1.0513 ± 0.0165 | **1.0229 ± 0.0160** | 1.43 |
| 960 (n=16) | 1.0165 ± 0.0264 | **1.0025 ± 0.0261** | 0.10 |

Pooled **1.0066 ± 0.0102**, residual trend **+0.0047 per doubling** against a per-grid scatter of 0.019.

⚠⚠ **THE ERROR IS WORTH MORE THAN THE NUMBER: I asserted "grid-independent" from TWO POINTS 0.99σ APART.** Two
noisy points cannot distinguish flat from halving — and halving is what it was doing. I read an *absence of
evidence* as evidence of a property, then built a structural conclusion on it ("therefore the analysis's"). ⚠ The
480 mean also wandered 1.0824 (n=16) → 1.0274 (n=7) → 1.0335 (n=28) → 1.0513 (n=44) as seeds accumulated; any of
those subsets would have read as a result, and refusing to quote whichever was in front of me is the only reason
the trend stayed readable.

**OLD TEXT, SUPERSEDED, kept for the shape of the mistake:**

⚠⚠ **THE NINE-SEED FIGURE (1.032 ± 0.033, "consistent with 1.000 at 0.99σ") IS SUPERSEDED.** The centre barely
moved; the error bar halved and the agreement did not survive it. Andreas asked for the campaign after I
recommended against it on the grounds that it would only sharpen a settled number — **it did not sharpen the
number, it changed the conclusion, and my recommendation was wrong.**

✅ **It is a centre shift, not a tail**: skew 0.050, median 1.0648 against mean 1.0559, trimmed means 1.0481 /
1.0798 tracking the means, 67.5 % of all 80 draws above 1.0. ✅ **And it is GRID-INDEPENDENT** — npts 240 gives
1.0493 ± 0.0162 (n=64), npts 480 gives 1.0824 ± 0.0291 (n=16), differing by **0.99σ** — so it is the analysis's
~6 %, not the Monte Carlo's discretisation.

**WHAT STANDS**: a factor of 2 in the variance is excluded at >15σ, so the OBJECT is right and the structure is
gated. **WHAT IS OPEN**: a ~5.7 % scale. ⚠ `ρ_k` is excluded as the cause **by sign** — a non-zero `ρ_k` drives
the k-lag variance DOWN via `(1−ρ_k)` and the discrepancy is upward. Named but unmeasured: the oblique projection
removing slightly the wrong component; `P` read at the nearest grid sample while the slope is taken at the exact
instant; and the MC's threshold being `0.5(max+min)` of a *noisy* record.

⚠ **A DRIFT SCARE ALONG THE WAY, RESOLVED AS FLUCTUATION**: at n=47 the ratio regressed **+0.00234 per run
(1.72σ)**, a +0.11 drift across the campaign — the size of the effect being measured. I held at "neither trust
nor dismiss" and prepared a decisive early-seed re-run rather than acting on it. By n=64 the slope had collapsed
to **+0.00037 (0.42σ)**. Two controls had pointed that way throughout: `ncross` was constant and per-run time flat.
⚠ I also sized the campaign from an 8-seed spread estimate (0.0804) when the true per-seed sd is 0.129, so it
landed at 1.4–1.7 % rather than the 1 % asked for — **I sized the experiment meant to cure four small-sample
over-reads by making a fifth.**

⚠⚠ **TWO NUMBERS I HAD ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN WERE WRONG, BOTH FROM THE SAME SLOPE ERROR.**

**(1) "The analysis side converges" is WITHDRAWN** — it was committed that way in `39acd11`
(1.023870e-06 → 1.055112e-06 → 1.062936e-06, "increments shrinking ~4×"). With the slope taken at the **requested
instant** rather than the nearest grid point, `2A` reads **1.055708e-06 / 1.055112e-06 / 1.062936e-06 — flat**.
What I had reported as the covariance converging was *my own slope error shrinking with the grid*. The
straddling/nearest-point slope reads 1.485472 on a 240-point grid against a converged 1.528: 2.8 % low, and
everything here goes as `1/s²`, so 5.6 % in the variance.

**(2) The 15.8 % projection share is NOT a property of the method** — it is a property of the SOURCE MIX.
Measured 0.1611 / 0.0173 / 0.0017 / 0.0002 as the tank's noise falls 1e-6 → 1e-9 against a fixed 1e-6 at the
buffers, because the phase direction is exactly what tank noise drives. ⚠ So a fixture with a quiet oscillator
shows the projection doing nothing and teaches the opposite lesson; the test asserts BOTH ends (0.10–0.25 with a
noisy tank, < 0.01 with a quiet one) so neither reading can be mistaken for the general case.

⚠⚠ **THE "UNEXPLAINED gear-240 ANOMALY" IS WITHDRAWN — IT WAS A THREE-SAMPLE ARTEFACT, AND I COMMITTED IT.**
`3e0ff8e` records gear-2 at 240 points showing a seed spread of 0.152 against Euler's 0.028 on identical draws,
"not sampling noise and I have no account of it". At **eight** seeds the spreads are Euler **0.0804** and gear
**0.1369** — a ratio of 1.7, not 5.4 — and an 8-sample standard deviation is itself uncertain to ±0.021 and
±0.037, so the difference is **1.35σ**. There was never an anomaly. ⚠ Two controls say the same: a crossing
audit found **zero** miscounted crossings in either integrator (spacings 0.96–1.16e-03 either way, counts 172/173
differing only by the period), and the per-lag breakdown is flat in `k` for both (sd/mean 0.02–0.13 Euler,
0.06–0.26 gear), so the scatter was always in each run's crossings rather than in the lag-differencing.

**This is the fourth time in three days I have over-read a spread estimated from three samples** — after the
"1.2 % deficit" (±0.8 % from three agreeing seeds, truly ±3.6 %), a one-seed 1.0143, and a two-seed 1.0032. A
mean of 2–3 draws cannot estimate its own spread, and I keep proving it.

✅ **AND THE HEADLINE STRENGTHENS**: sixteen seeds (eight per integrator) give `MC/analysis` = **1.03 ± 0.03**,
reproducing the 1.032 ± 0.033 committed from nine. Euler alone 1.0203 ± 0.0284, gear alone 1.0380 ± 0.0484 —
each consistent with 1.000 at 0.7–0.8σ.

⚠ **AND A VACUOUS-PASS SHAPE CAUGHT IN MY OWN TEST**: the driven-refusal check originally built its fixture
INSIDE `pytest.raises`, so a `ValueError` from the *fixture* would have satisfied the context without the method
under test ever being called — and a green dot cannot distinguish that from a real refusal. The fixture is now
built outside, with an assertion that it is actually driven.

### B10 closed: LSOAC measured against the bordered solve, and it is strictly worse (Andreas: "Do B10" → "Do a")

**B10 was marked PRIORITY DROPPED** after a peer session read the paper (Mei & Roychowdhury 2006 DATE) and found
its evidence MPDE-specific. Put to Andreas as (a) build the defensible gate the entry names, or (b) close it on
the existing argument; he chose (a). The measurement now closes it on evidence rather than on reading.

⚠ **THE ROADMAP'S OWN GATE AIMED AT THE WRONG OBJECT and was not used.** It proposed reproducing `λ₂` and `Q` —
but LSOAC replaces the phase row in the **bordered small-signal solve**, not in an eigenproblem, so that gate
would pass or fail for unrelated reasons. The entry's alternative (non-smoothness across a frequency sweep) was
also predicted to be vacuous, for a reason already in the tree: for `α ≠ 1` the system `(I − αM)y = b` is
NONSINGULAR, so `y` is unique and both routes must return it. **So the gate was reframed to CONDITIONING**, which
is the thing the border actually buys.

**MEASURED** (van der Pol Q = 15.92, n = 4, exact dense `pinv` so the iterative solver is not a confound):
at offsets 1e-1 / 1e-2 the two routes agree to **1.4e-12 / 1.6e-11** — no ambiguity to resolve — and below
1e-6 the minimum-norm error grows **linearly in 1/offset** (1.6e-7 at 1e-6, **1.6e-4 at 1e-9**) while
`σ_min(bordered)` stays **flat at 2.858e-02** against a plain `σ_min` tracking the offset from 2.810e-03 to
2.810e-09.

✅ **Every prediction was recorded before the harness ran**, including the one that would have REVIVED B10 —
minimum-norm holding accuracy at 1e-9. It did not: it is out by 1.6e-04 there. ⚠ A rank-tolerance artifact in
`pinv` was flagged in advance as the thing to suspect if moderate-offset agreement came back at ~1 instead of
~1e-11; residuals ran 6e-16 → 8e-08, tracking the conditioning, so the comparison is clean.

⚠ **LIMITATION: a null on ONE fixture.** The conditioning argument is structural and should generalise, but that
is not shown. No test added — it would gate a rejected method, and
`test_the_deflated_solve_is_capped_by_the_TANGENT_not_by_the_PPV` already pins the uniqueness property that makes
LSOAC unnecessary.

### E2 spike: TR-BDF2 batched against gear-2, on identical machinery (Andreas chose "spike one stage method first")

`benchmarks/stage_method_batched.py`. The backend reaches its integrator only through `compute_integration`
('euler'/'gear'/'trap'), so a stage method cannot be asked for there at all; both marches are therefore written
against the circuit callables with ONE hand-written Newton, identical convergence tests, identical `vmap` and an
identical fixed grid — only the companion differs. 128 lanes, one CUDA device, half-wave rectifier:

| steps | TR-BDF2 warm | err (V) | per-step µs | gear-2 warm | err (V) | per-step µs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 100 | 0.060 s | 1.571e-03 | 597 | 0.038 s | 1.585e-02 | 377 |
| 200 | 0.100 s | 3.823e-04 | 499 | 0.053 s | 3.613e-03 | 265 |
| 400 | 0.171 s | 9.432e-05 | 428 | 0.093 s | 8.442e-04 | 232 |
| 800 | 0.314 s | 2.336e-05 | 393 | 0.173 s | 2.020e-04 | 216 |

Both cleanly second order. Gear-2 is 1.7–1.8× cheaper per step (one solve vs two stages); TR-BDF2's error constant
is ~10× smaller, so gear-2 needs √10 ≈ 3.2× the steps at equal accuracy. **Net: TR-BDF2 ~1.4–1.6× faster at equal
accuracy.** Yardstick validated the only way that means anything — same method, same 1600-step grid, both
backends: worst lane **1.9e-12**, nonconv 0.

⚠⚠ **THREE THINGS I GOT WRONG HERE, ALL CAUGHT BY MEASUREMENT AND ALL WRITTEN INTO THE HARNESS.**
(1) The first run reported **20× over the CPU loop** — it was EIGHT BIT-IDENTICAL LANES. `params_tree` is consumed
only through the evaluation groups, and `_eval_groups` is built LAZILY inside `Circuit.G`; a march that never calls
`G` leaves it empty, `batched_contributions` returns None, and every lane runs the build value. `solve_batched`'s
own comment describes exactly this ("a parameter sweep that sweeps nothing, with no symptom"), which is what the
spike reproduced. Caught by printing per-lane finals — *are the lanes even different?* — not by the ratio looking
implausible.
(2) The first gear column compared against `solve_batched`, which has no `fixed_timestep` and so ran ADAPTIVE at
7.3e-4 against a fixed march at 5e-14: different work at different accuracy, so "20–47× vs gear" measured nothing.
Retracted and replaced by the work-precision table above.
(3) My reference gate compared 12800-step JAX against 1600-step CPU and flagged 5.75e-6 as a disagreement — that IS
the coarser grid's own second-order error, predicted 5.8e-6 from the 800-step row (a 1 % match). The gate now
compares the same grid on both backends.

**Recommendation, which is the spike's deliverable: do not port GLM on this evidence, and do not port TR-BDF2
either without one more measurement.** Four unknowns is a fixture where fixed overheads (a 3×3 dense solve, the
traced `while_loop`) dominate per-step cost, so the 1.7× ratio need not survive at the sizes a batched backend
exists for; and the spike is fixed-step, while LTE control, rejection, breakpoints and the rescue ladder are both
what a port must add and where this backend's complexity lives. If a stage method ever goes there it should be
TR-BDF2, not GLM (five stages, badly scaled tableau, already 2.4–4.7× slower than radau on the CPU). Next cheap
step: the same harness at a larger `m`.

### E2's own next measurement: the same harness at a larger `m` — the answer survives, after two fixture defects (Andreas: "Do E2")

The spike's closing line asked for exactly one thing, *"the same harness at a larger `m`"*, and `build()` now
takes `nsec` diode-RC sections. **m = nsec + 3**, not the 2·nsec + 2 I first wrote: R and C hang on the SAME
section node, so a section adds ONE unknown, and the harness printed 4/13/28 where I had predicted 4/22/52. An
RC ladder was rejected deliberately — it is linear, converges in one Newton iteration, and would have measured
linear-solve cost while hiding exactly the per-stage NONLINEAR work that separates two stages from one.

**m = 4 / 13 / 28, grids 400/800/1600 against a 12800-step reference, 128 lanes, every row `nonconv 0`:**

| m | per-step tr/gear | err ratio gear/tr | gear steps for equal acc | TR-BDF2 net at equal acc |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | 1.97 | 8.55 | 2.92 | 1.48 |
| 13 | 1.88 | 8.63 | 2.94 | 1.57 |
| 28 | 1.77 | 8.67 | 2.94 | 1.66 |

**Both halves of the m=4 finding survive**, which is precisely what was in doubt. Gear-2 stays cheaper per step
at every size (1.44–1.97 across all nine cells, never approaching 1), and TR-BDF2 still wins at equal accuracy
at every size (1.48–2.13). The **error-constant ratio is the stable part** — 8.55–9.32 over every cell, and
8.55/8.63/8.67 at the finest grid — so "gear needs ~3× the steps" is a property of the two methods, not of the
fixture's size. Cost grows superlinearly in m, as a dense solve should: 390 / 784 / 2388 µs per step at 1600.

⚠ **No trend in m is claimed.** The per-step column looks monotone at the finest grid (1.97 → 1.88 → 1.77) but
is NOT monotone at 800 steps (1.81 → 1.87 → 1.69), and every cell is a SINGLE timing whose grid-to-grid scatter
at fixed m (m=28 reads 1.44 / 1.69 / 1.77) is as large as the variation across m. Having just retracted a 4σ
claim built on two noisy points, I am not reading a trend off nine single-shot ones.

⚠⚠ **TWO FIXTURE DEFECTS, both caught by a guard rather than by a number looking implausible.**
(1) **The far sections were DEAD.** At va=5 the series drops exhaust the source by section 8: nodes b9..b24 sat
at 5e-11 down to 1e-161 V. So my first "m=28" was nine live nonlinear sections plus sixteen unknowns doing
linear-algebra work only — the very defect the RC ladder was rejected for, rebuilt in another shape. The tell
was error columns **identical to every digit** at nsec=10 and nsec=25: two different circuits cannot do that,
and they were solving the same live subcircuit. The drive now scales (va = 5 + 0.85·(nsec−1)) and a LIVENESS
GUARD asserts the deepest section carries volts against the first. ⚠ The slope is now measured where it is
USED: my first 0.42 V/section was read off the shallow end of a chain that died at section 8, and extrapolating
it to 25 sections left the deepest node at 1.5e-6 V. Swept over va = 15.1–55 V the drop is flat — 0.431 V near
the source, 0.474 V mid-chain — with v(b) ≈ 0.775·va − 1.06. And the drive is bounded on BOTH sides: at va=55
the fixed-step Newton starts failing (nonconv 44 where 15.1–40 give 0), so "more drive" is not a free fix.
(2) **`params_tree` is keyed by CLASS, not by instance name**, and a hit replaces the WHOLE group's params, so
`{'R': {'r': …}}` addresses every resistor at once and must carry one column per resistor. At nsec=1 the
(lanes,1) and (lanes,nsec) shapes coincide, which is why the original fixture ran for a day without showing it.
Worth recording the asymmetry: a shape that is too small fails **loud** (a vmap size mismatch), while a key that
stops matching fails **silent** (every lane runs the build value) — opposite failure modes from the same tree.

⚠ **Liveness cost the coarse end of the curve.** At the higher drive the 100-step grid no longer converges at
m=13 or m=28, and 200 fails at m=28 (gear2 nonconv 7800, err `nan`), so the harness marks such rows EXCLUDED
rather than letting them be quoted — a nonconverged row's error is from a march that did not converge and its
timing is from a Newton that ran to MAXITER. The sweep now runs 400/800/1600. The reference was raised for a
second reason: 3200 against 800 is only 4× finer, which puts ~6 % of its own second-order error into TR-BDF2's
error — the DENOMINATOR of the ratio. At 12800 that is 1.6 %, and the sweep asserts ≥4× the finest grid.

**Cost, honestly.** The GPU measurement is ~1–3 s per configuration; most of the elapsed time went to
self-inflicted detours — a 900 s "hang" that turned out to be `main()`'s hard-coded 1600-step, 4-lane CPU
validation eating ~597 s of a ~600 s run, with three hypotheses (my patch, GPU contention, a regression) formed
and refuted before I localised it. Measured separately: the CPU yardstick costs 65 s at grid 100 and agrees to
**3.197e-14** — better than at grid 400 (8.704e-14), since a coarser grid accumulates less round-off and both
sides run the same grid — so validation belongs once, at a coarse grid, not per size.

**The recommendation is unchanged**: if a stage method ever goes on that backend it should be TR-BDF2, not GLM.
What changed is that the 1.5–1.7× net win is no longer a four-unknown artefact — it holds from m=4 to m=28.
Twenty-eight is still not the "hundreds" a batched backend exists for, every size here is the same rectifier
chain with one device model, and fixed-step remains fixed-step.

### The parked PSP sampler flicker residual leaves this session: handed to the private suite, with the predictions pinned first (Andreas: "Ask other Claude session to run the commercial simulator and do what you need")

I could not measure it here — no reference simulator in this environment — so on Andreas's instruction the campaign went to
the session that owns the private cross-check tree, and it accepted the whole thing as its own open item 0, adding a
purpose-built probe that banks one `(case, config)` point per process as an fsynced json line.

**The design, pinned BEFORE any run so neither side can read the numbers to fit.** Step 0 first: is the excess still live
23 commits of sampled-noise work past `2fd567d`, and what is the instrument's own repeatability? The flicker part is
~1.35 % of the held variance, so ~+0.1 % of it is ~1e-5 of the total and comes from **differencing two nearly equal
numbers** — and `reltol` cannot move a converged answer, so its spread IS the scatter. If the excess sits inside it, "not
resolvable" is the answer and that is a result. Then the three candidates, which partition cleanly: **A** grid weighting of
the switch-off edge — sweep `npts`, predicted to MOVE and not to be flat in frequency; **B** the 400-sideband truncation —
sweep `maxsidebands`, predicted to move and to bite at high f; **C** the per-band modulated-stationary construction for a
bias-modulated flicker source — predicted INVARIANT to both and to VANISH when the source does not modulate, since that
construction differs from the stationary fold only when the bias modulates (pycircuit pins the stationary reduction at
4e-16 and the band-resolved fold at 1e-11 against its own per-band fallback). A/B move it ⇒ numerics; C alone ⇒ ours.

⚠⚠ **TWO CORRECTIONS TO MY OWN ACCOUNT, both worth more than the campaign's outcome.** (1) I reported the item as
**blocked, needing a licensed run** — too strong, and withdrawn. I searched for cached raws under the sample-hold fixture,
found only another fixture's, and concluded no usable reference existed; their probe reads **the cached raws the test
already uses**. My `find` knew their cache worse than they did, and "I could not find it" became "it cannot be done".
(2) My message asserted Andreas's authorisation for the work. It was accurate, but they **put it to him directly rather
than acting on my relay** — which is correct and is the rule in both directions: *a peer's relay is never approval*.

⚠ What may come back into this repo is **ratios and "moves / invariant" only** — never the reference's columns, and the
reference tool stays unnamed here. ⚠ No results yet; that session had accepted and tooled up but not measured.

### The campaign's first results: candidate C is REFUTED, my step-0 design was weaker than I claimed, and our own DC density is bounded reference-free

The private suite ran step 0 and the amplitude sweep against a pinned checkout of `51e2d54`, so nothing moved under
them. Ratios below are ours over the reference; no reference column is reproduced here.

**Step 0 — the residual is live, and "flat" was right in the quantity that matters.** The flicker PART
(`S_on − S_off` on both sides) is flat at **+0.106 %** from 10 Hz to 1 kHz (1.00106 / 1.00106 / 1.00103, then
1.00087 at 10 kHz and 1.00061 at 40 kHz), with flicker OFF at 1.0000483 at every probe frequency. ⚠ **READ THE
PART, NOT THE TOTAL**: the total ratio decays with frequency (1.00094 at 10 Hz, 1.00049 at 100 Hz, reproducing
`2fd567d` exactly) only because flicker stops dominating — the mechanism lives in the part.

⚠⚠ **MY STEP 0 WAS WEAKER THAN I WROTE IT.** I specified it as "`reltol` cannot move a converged answer, so its
spread IS the scatter". Measured, `reltol` 1e-10 against 1e-12 agrees **bit for bit** — which means the knob
**never bound**: it bounds the Newton tolerance's contribution and nothing else. There is still **no error bar** on
this residual, and the axes that could supply one are the grid and the sideband count, i.e. candidates A and B
themselves. A scatter probe that lives on the same axes as the hypotheses is an awkward instrument, and it was
reported as a null with that qualifier rather than dressed up as a clean one.

⚠⚠ **CANDIDATE C IS REFUTED — AND THE PARTITION WAS MINE, so this is my error, not a surprise in the data.**
Flicker-part ratio against clock amplitude (lowering the amplitude shrinks the switch's bias excursion; 0.0 holds the
clock at DC, so the source is stationary):

| clock amplitude | flicker-OFF ratio | flicker-PART ratio at 10 Hz |
|---|---|---|
| 0.75 (fixture) | 1.0000483 | +0.11 % |
| 0.375 | 1.0000006 | **+5.8 %** |
| 0.0 (stationary) | 1.0164974 | +19.6 % |

I predicted the excess **vanishes** when the source stops modulating. It **grows** — 55× at half depth, 185× at
zero. So the per-band modulated-stationary construction does not by itself explain it, and **A/B/C does not span the
mechanism**. ⚠ The refutation rests on the **0.375 row alone**, whose flicker-OFF control (1.0000006) is *cleaner
than the fixture's own* (1.0000483); the stationary row is contaminated — its flicker-OFF reads 1.0165 where a clean
case reads ~1.000, an always-on switch being far wider band than 400 sidebands plausibly capture — and must not be
quoted alone. A truncation check at npts 2000 / msb 800 is running there.

✅ **WHAT I COULD SETTLE HERE, reference-free and with no PSS.** Their hypothesis: as the modulation shrinks the
switch sits at low Vds for the whole cycle — the regime that carried the `(0.2 mV)²` floor fixed in `2fd567d` — so a
residual difference in the flicker DENSITY would grow exactly this way and involve none of the sampled-noise
construction. Our code makes that plausible on its face: the core runs on the ordered `vdsa = sqrt(Vds²+(2e-4)²)`
and the flicker contribution carries a single `sgn² = (Vds/vdsa)²` factor (`compact.py` ~908), whose own comment
concedes it removes the floor "to first order" and is "`1 − 4e-8/Vds²`" away. That form even lands on their numbers:
+0.106 % ↔ Vds ≈ 6 mV, +5.8 % ↔ Vds ≈ 0.8 mV, both credible for a switch driven less hard.

**But the measurement kills it as the explanation of the GROWTH.** Flicker part = `CY(1 Hz) − CY(1 MHz)` on the IHP
n-channel 10/1 µm card, `Sfl/Vds²` over Vds = 0.05 … 10 mV:

| Vg | spread of `Sfl/Vds²` | shape |
|---|---|---|
| 0.8 V | **0.272 %** | monotone, HIGH at low Vds — but **saturating**: 1.002720 / 1.002707 / 1.002680 at 0.05 / 0.1 / 0.2 mV |
| 1.2 V | **0.047 %** | **opposite sign** (0.9995 at low Vds) |

Over that range `sgn²` runs 0.0588 → 0.9996, so a factor of **seventeen** is being compensated to a few parts in
10³. Two consequences. At the fixture amplitude our density's own non-ideality (0.05–0.27 %, bias-dependent) is the
**same order as the whole +0.106 %** and could account for it outright. But it **cannot give +5.8 %**: it flattens
exactly where their effect accelerates, and it changes sign with gate bias instead of growing. ⚠ This also
re-measures rather than trusting the gate test's docstring — which records 6.4212 / 6.4205 / 6.4197e-16 at 0.1 / 0.5
/ 1 mV, a 0.023 % drift, consistent with the above.

**So the campaign's next step is sharper than when I handed it over**: their DC-bias density comparison now decides
where the growth lives. A growing low-Vds gap there is the reference's density, since ours is pinned flat-ish and
saturating; agreement there puts the growth back in the sampled path, where truncation (B) is the live suspect and
the sweeps are already running. Scratch harness `flicker_dc.py`.

⚠ **A SEPARATE FINDING OF THEIRS, recorded because it is ours to own eventually and NOT investigated here**: on the
1600-point sampler orbit the ODE's step ratio did not move (2.70 → 2.54) while `PAC`'s more than doubled
(10.02 → 23.43) — two convergent computations whose Richardson limits sit ~5.2e-04 apart, so the gap is **not
discretisation**. Their own "sampling" lead is refuted by its own refinement (interpolating `a(t)`, `b(t)` with 8
sub-steps moved the answer 1.391e-04 at 200 points and 2.190e-06 at 1600 — second order, 230× under the gap).

⚠⚠ **CORRECTIONS, 2026-09-18 (same day, from the campaign's next round) — ONE OF THEM RETRACTS MY OWN INFERENCE ABOVE.**

1. **The "stationary" row was only CLOCK-stationary**, and the table above inherits that error from the run that
   produced it. Setting the clock amplitude to 0 leaves the fixture's INPUT SINE swinging 0.2 V, so the device goes
   on modulating. Candidate C is still contradicted — by the 0.375 row, which is clean and has modulation plainly
   present — but "185× at zero modulation" overstates what was done. A genuinely time-invariant point (both
   sources DC) is the right control and was queued.

2. ⚠⚠ **RETRACTED: "our density could account for the fixture's whole +0.106 %".** A DC-bias comparison of the two
   densities — no PSS on either side, same 10/1 µm card — reads **ours/reference = 0.99999998 at every one of
   sixteen points** (Vds 0.05–10 mV × Vg 0.8 and 1.2 V): flat, no bias dependence, no growth toward low Vds. So our
   density accounts for **none** of the sampled excess. **My error was comparing our density against a `Vds²`
   IDEALISATION and treating the deviation as disagreement with the reference** — the reference deviates from `Vds²`
   in the same way, which is exactly why their ratio is flat. *Deviation from an idealisation is not disagreement
   with a reference.* The 0.272 % / 0.047 % measurement stands; the inference hung on it does not.

3. **Candidate B is refuted on BOTH counts**, including one neither of us was varying: ours 200→400 identical to
   seven digits, and the reference's own sideband count 200→400 identical for all three amplitude rows. ⚠ Its
   counts ABOVE 400 are artefacts, not findings: the white ratio reads exactly 1/3 at 800 and 1/5 at 1600, i.e. the
   PSD scaling as 1 + count/400 — aliased replicas, with an undersampling warning in the log. A factor of three
   that would have made a fine result, caught by reading the log instead of the number.

4. ✅ **THE RESIDUAL NOW HAS A REAL ERROR BAR, replacing the `reltol` null**: re-running the reference at identical
   settings under a different name moves the fixture's flicker part 1.00106 → 1.00109, about **3e-05 against a
   1.06e-03 residual — 35:1**. Measured, not a knob that never bound.

5. **A (grid) is half-answered**: at npts 2000 the flicker-OFF case is identical to npts 1000; the flicker-ON point
   decides it.

**So if A comes back invariant, nothing in my A/B/C survives**, and the object to explain is an excess that is flat
in frequency, invariant to both grids and both sideband counts, identical in density at DC, and that grows as the
modulation falls. 💡 **A hypothesis sent back, checkable from data already in hand**: if the excess is a CONSTANT
ABSOLUTE offset, the growth is pure normalisation — the flicker part shrinks as the clock closes down while the
offset does not. That predicts `part(0.75)/part(0.375) = 54.9` and `part(0.75)/part(clock-stat) = 185.1` exactly,
one division away, and would recast the search as "what additive 1/f term is in one side and not the other, and is
NOT gated by the clock".

⚠⚠ **MY CONSTANT-OFFSET HYPOTHESIS IS FALSIFIED (2026-09-18), and two reference-free checks came out of chasing it.**

The one division settled it, in the direction opposite to my prediction. Absolute flicker parts at 10 Hz (V²/Hz,
npts 1000 / msb 400) **GROW** as the clock closes — 5.3× at amplitude 0.375 and 29.9× at clock-stationary — while the
excess grows **291× and 5543×**. I predicted `part(0.75)/part(0.375) = 54.9`; it measured **0.19**. So the excess is
neither normalisation nor a constant additive term, it grows FASTER than the quantity it sits in, and the
"always-on additive 1/f term" search it would have opened does not arise. **Third inference of mine to fall this
week**, and like the others it fell on data already in hand before anything was built on it. ⚠ No power law is
fitted to it: three amplitudes give two exponents (3.40 and 2.53 against the part), which is exactly the evidence
base that produced four retractions between the two sides this week — "grows faster than the part, exponent
unmeasured" is the honest statement.

✅ **OUR COLOUR-FIT PATH IS EXONERATED, 3.8e-14.** Worth testing because it fitted every constraint at once: the
fold does NOT use `CY` directly — `_cy_components_model` FITS `C(w) = A + B (w1/w)^EF` per element and evaluates
that MODEL at every band, so a fit honouring its anchors while deviating between them would be invisible to a
two-frequency DC density check, to sideband count, and to grid alike. The code keeps a per-band path as fallback,
so the same sampled flicker part was run both ways on our own switched-cap sampler — same grid, same sidebands,
only the path differing: **fitted/per-band = 1.0000000000, worst 3.8e-14** (9.99e-16 flicker-off). Not it.
⚠ The fit is also better guarded than I had assumed: three frequencies fit and **two verify**, one of them at
`150 f0` — the far end of the band range the fold reaches — with a miss returning `None` and falling back to
per-band rather than being used silently. Scratch `fit_vs_perband.py`. ⚠ A monkeypatch trap on the way: taking
`PAC._colour_fit` by attribute returns the bare FUNCTION, and restoring that turns a `staticmethod` into a normal
method, so the next call receives `self` — take the descriptor from `__dict__`.

✅ **AND A PRIOR FOR THE TIME-INVARIANT CONTROL**: our fold of a STATIONARY 1/f source already has an independent
CLOSED-FORM gate — Sepke eq. (33) on a reset-RC, asserted at 3e-3 with measured worst 1.2e-3 mid-window and 5e-4
for the 1/f part at the window end (1e-5 at 1000 points), and NOT vacuous (a window one step different fails by
>5e-4; the stationary reference differs by >10 %, both asserted live). So an excess of tens of percent in a plain
fold is unlikely to be our folding. ⚠ Stated as a prior, not a proof: that fixture is LTI-inside-the-window with a
noiseless reset switch and a stationary `CY` on one node, not a MOS switch with a bias-dependent density.

**Standing tally of the partition**: C contradicted (on the 0.375 row), B refuted on both sideband counts,
densities identical to 1e-8, our colour-fit path null at 3.8e-14, our stationary-1/f fold gated at 1e-3, A pending.
If A is invariant and the both-sources-DC control still shows the excess, nothing either side has named survives.

💡 **An operational fact worth more than either result.** The harness there stopped six long runs in a day at
18–19 GB available against a measured 260 MB peak, including a 17-minute solve two minutes short of reporting — the
same spurious "low memory" kills that forced the five-way chunked suite on this side. Their fix was not retrying but
**DETACHING** (`setsid nohup`, poll the log); everything then ran first try and one job survived a session restart.
That is a better answer than chunking and should be tried here.

### The PAC-vs-ODE gap: `PAC.covariance` is CONSISTENT, and chasing it found a discretisation floor described as physics in our own docstring (Andreas: "Do the PAC vs ODE gap")

The private suite reported `PAC.covariance`'s held value and an independent ODE integration converging to limits
**~5.17e-04 apart** — both convergent, so a CONSISTENCY question rather than an accuracy one, and their own
"sampling" lead was already refuted by its own refinement (second order, 230× under the gap).

⚠ **THEIR COMPARISON CARRIES TWO DISCRETISATIONS**: the ODE takes `a(t)`, `b(t)` from a solved PSS, so refining the
grid moves the orbit AND the variance integration together. This removed the second one.

**THE INSTRUMENT.** On our switched-capacitor fixture the switch is its own closed form —
`g(t) = goff + (gon−goff)(1+tanh((V_ck−vth)/vs))/2` with `V_ck` a known sinusoid — so the cap-node variance obeys
`dV/dt = −2(g/C)V + 2kT·g/C²` with **exact** coefficients and no grid at all. Solved to periodic steady state
(stiff, **closing error exactly 0.0**), with the reconstructed clock **gated against the solved PSS node** before
anything downstream used it.

⚠⚠ **THE FIRST VERSION WAS INSENSITIVE, AND WHY IS THE INTERESTING PART.** With only the switch's own thermal
noise, `b = 2kT·g/C²` and `2a·(kT/C) = b`, so **`V(t) = kT/C` identically, for ANY `g(t)`** — fluctuation-
dissipation, with the periodic solution unique because `g > 0` contracts. The reference then does not depend on the
thing under test, and no error preserving kT/C could be caught. (An earlier attempt was worse still: nested `quad`
over a tanh switching in 50 mV on a 1 V sine could not reproduce itself — the "exact" value moved 0.1 % between
grids where a grid-independent quantity cannot move.) Fixed by **breaking the FDT balance** with an extra white
source not tied to `g`: the exact profile then spans **15×** over the period and the test has teeth.

✅ **RESULT — `covariance` CONVERGES TO THE RIGHT LIMIT**, error against the exact profile at npts 200…1600:

| phase | errors | ratios |
|---|---|---|
| hold | −1.07e-02 → −4.76e-03 → −2.25e-03 → −1.09e-03 | 2.24, 2.12, **2.07** |
| track | −2.60e-01 → −1.53e-01 → −8.43e-02 → −4.44e-02 | 1.70, 1.82, **1.90** |

First order in both phases — exactly the O(h) its piecewise-constant injection predicts — converging to the exact
continuous answer. **So the peer's gap is not a consistency defect in `PAC.covariance`**; it is far more likely to
sit in the ODE side's coefficient extraction (`a(t)`, `b(t)` off a PSP device, where the noise is not a simple
`4kT g` and "per-instant C" is a choice).

⚠⚠ **AND A CLAIM OF OURS IS WITHDRAWN.** `covariance`'s docstring said *"the tracked variance is 0.957 kT/C, NOT
kT/C: a sinusoidal clock holds the switch at full `gon` only instantaneously, so the capacitor is never in
equilibrium with `Ron`; both tools agree on that independently."* That is a **discretisation floor described as
physics**. The capacitor IS in equilibrium at every instant, by the argument above, and the tracking value reads
0.7398 / 0.8467 / 0.9157 / 0.9556 kT/C at 200 / 400 / 800 / 1600 — errors 0.260, 0.153, 0.084, 0.044, halving,
converging to kT/C. ⚠ The test was already right and is unchanged: it asserts the hold near 1.0 and pins tracking
only against a CONTROL at "the same O(h/tau) floor", naming it a floor.

⚠⚠ **THE PART THAT GENERALISES**: that docstring cited the reference agreeing at **0.99878** on the tracking
instant as corroboration. Two tools discretising the same period at comparable step counts agree about a **SHARED
ARTEFACT**. *Cross-tool agreement cannot establish a LIMIT — only a closed form or a refinement ladder can.* This is
the same lesson A8 paid for, where cross-family agreement could not show a number was right; it is worth auditing
any other record that leans on "both tools agree" for a quantity nobody refined.

⚠ Also corrected: "the held value converges at better than second order" is a property of the **kT/C fixture**,
where the exact profile is a constant and the leading terms cancel (it reads third order there). With the balance
broken the held value converges at first order like everything else. Scratch: `pac_vs_ode2.py`, `pac_vs_ode3.py`.

### The grid has two sides, our side converges, and a limit that is really a stall (the campaign's next round, 2026-09-18)

**THE REFERENCE'S OWN GRID CARRIES 43 % OF THE RESIDUAL, and only one side was ever being varied.** With the
reference converged (its own max-step ladder settling, its harmonic count 32→64 alone worth −3.5e-04) the excess
falls from **+0.106 % to +0.078 %** — still 26× the 3e-05 scatter, so something survives, but ⚠ **its SIZE was
never a property of this library alone**, and both sides had been quoting it as though it were. Separable and
additive: our grid adds +1.4e-04 per doubling at both reference settings; theirs removes −4.6e-04 at both of ours.

⚠ **MY CANDIDATE A WAS HALF WRONG, and the wrong half was load-bearing.** I predicted a grid effect would move with
npts AND not be flat in frequency. It moves and it **is** flat (1.00078 / 1.00078 / 1.00075 over 10 Hz…1 kHz). The
flatness argument I had used to push *away* from grid explanations is therefore withdrawn as well.

✅ **OUR SAMPLED PATH CONVERGES — FIRST ORDER, MEASURED REFERENCE-FREE.** Their sharpest claim was that our side does
not settle. A convergent quantity must settle against *itself*, which needs no reference: on our behavioural
switched-cap fixture with a 1/f source, flicker part at a fixed phase, **sidebands held fixed at 200** so npts is
the only knob:

| npts | part @100 Hz | successive diff | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| 500 | 2.52669878e-11 | | |
| 1000 | 2.51470282e-11 | −1.1996e-13 | |
| 2000 | 2.50855008e-11 | −6.1527e-14 | **1.95** |
| 4000 | 2.50543058e-11 | −3.1195e-14 | **1.97** |

First order, settling, ~1.2e-03 relative error left at npts 4000 — the same O(h) the covariance injection has.
⚠ **Their "not converging" rests on ONE interval** (npts 1000→2000, measured at two reference settings — one
doubling twice, not a ladder). A first-order sequence also moves at every doubling; what separates it from
non-convergence is whether the NEXT difference halves. **Prediction sent before their npts 4000 landed: ~+7e-05,
half of the +1.4e-04 they saw.** Another +1.4e-04 means something here genuinely does not settle.

⚠ **A COUPLING WORTH KNOWING**: `sampled_noise` REFUSES a sideband count above the grid's own Nyquist — at npts 250
it rejected msb 200 by name (Nyquist 123 at 249 points). So msb 800 is legal at npts 2000 but would be refused at
npts 1000, which may be why the other side's white clock-stationary case moved both knobs at once. Forced or not,
it remains a confound: one knob at a time needs npts 2000 with msb held at 400.

⚠⚠ **AND A LIMIT THAT IS PROBABLY A STALL.** They refined the reference's own step and corrected three of their
records to match this log's finding (the 0.957 tracked value is a floor, not physics — that part is settled on both
sides). But they then read the limit as **~0.974 kT/C**, by Aitken on 0.95489 / 0.96723 / 0.97128 / 0.97239. ⚠ Those
errors **decelerate** — ratios 1.38, 1.14, **1.04** — and a ratio heading to *one* is a sequence that has stopped
moving; Aitken on it returns roughly where it stalled rather than a limit. Our ladder reads 1.70, 1.82, 1.90 toward
2, which is convergence. **Recorded as unresolved in `covariance`'s docstring rather than decided in our favour**,
together with their correct caveat: the kT/C result is a theorem only for a fixture whose noise is tied to its own
conductance, and a real device (their measured `sid/(4kT g)` running 1.09→3.17 through turn-off) breaks it. Two
one-line answers decide the rest: their npts 4000, and whether their switched-cap case is behavioural or a real
device. Scratch `selfconv.py`.

### The tracking dispute closes, and both sides' rebuttals were wrong in the same way (2026-09-18)

**One question settled it, and the answer was a one-liner: the reference side runs THE SAME BEHAVIOURAL SWITCH.**
`pcswitch.va` contributes `g*V(p,n)` and `white_noise(4*kb*temp*g)` with the same
`g = goff + (gon−goff)*0.5*(1+tanh((V(cp,cn)−vth)/vs))` that `_SwitchHdl` implements — keeping one set of
equations on both sides is the point of the fixture. So fluctuation-dissipation holds there too, `V ≡ kT/C` is a
theorem about **both** sides' circuit, and there is no real device anywhere near it.

⚠⚠ **BOTH REBUTTALS WERE WRONG, IN THE SAME SHAPE, AND THAT IS THE TRANSFERABLE PART.** They told me my `g(t)` was
"a TANH MODEL of the switch" rather than the fixture — written without opening the model source; the tanh is the
fixture, on both sides. And I had already written into `covariance`'s docstring that "a real device breaks it",
citing their ~0.974 as **that** circuit's limit — attaching their number to a circuit it did not come from. Same
error, both directions, both caught in one exchange **because the fixture question was a one-liner**. Cheapest
disambiguation of the campaign: *ask which circuit before arguing about its limit.*

✅ **~0.974 IS WITHDRAWN BY THE SIDE THAT MADE IT**, on the argument recorded here: error ratios 1.38 → 1.14 →
**1.04** head to ONE, which is a stalled sequence, and Aitken on a stall returns roughly where it stalled. It is a
floor in the reference's own sampled-noise integration; `tau/320` and `tau/640` are running there to characterise
it. The docstring now records it as a withdrawn floor rather than as anyone's limit, and keeps the real-device
caveat as a statement about OTHER fixtures.

✅ **"Your side is not converging" conceded as stated** — one interval measured twice is not a ladder. Their npts
4000 is running against the **+7e-05** prediction (versus another +1.4e-04).

✅ **THE ORDER RECONCILIATION IS ADOPTED, AND ITS CHECKABLE CONSEQUENCE IS ALREADY MEASURED HERE — the opposite way
round from the guess.** The balance shows in the **HOLD's** order, not the track's:

| | hold ratios | track ratios |
|---|---|---|
| FD balance intact | 6.25, 7.33, **7.80** (≈3rd order) | 1.70, 1.82, 1.90 (1st) |
| FD balance broken | 2.24, 2.12, **2.07** (1st order) | 1.70, 1.82, 1.90 (1st) |

The track is first order either way and carries no signature of the balance; the hold drops from third order to
first when it is broken — with the balance intact the exact profile is a CONSTANT, so the leading terms cancel, and
the cancellation shows where the variance is frozen rather than where it is being driven. A near-balanced hold is
therefore exactly what a fast, accelerating held ratio means.

⚠ The Nyquist refusal explains the other side's coupled knobs — `msb 800` is legal at npts 2000 and refused at
npts 1000 — so that confound was forced rather than chosen; npts 2000 with msb held at 400 is running there to
separate them.

### A6 step 2: closing the loop PINS the marginal phase mode, at exactly the loop bandwidth (Andreas: "Do step 2")

A6's own record left one question open: *"a free-running integrator's phase row is a marginal mode — measured
`dx_end/dx_0 = 1.000000` — so `I − M` is singular there by construction. Locking it needs feedback (a PLL) … the
fold repairs the residual's VALUE, not the Jacobian's RANK."* If feedback does not move that multiplier, a driven
PSS cannot represent a locked PLL and A6 is research rather than a build. **It moves, and by the predicted amount.**

**The fixture is the smallest thing that closes**: `VcoHdl` with `f0` exactly the reference frequency and
`modulus = 1` — so at zero gain the phase advances one cycle per reference period and *every* offset is a solution,
which is the marginal mode itself — plus a multiplier PD and an RC filter. **No PFD**: the question is about the
Jacobian's rank, and a linear PD is the minimal feedback that can pin a phase. (A PFD also carries a state machine,
which is a `hidden_state` question this did not need to open.)

**The law, named before the confirming run.** Equilibrium at `f0 + kvco·Vctl = fref` with `Vctl = (K/2)cos(2πθ)`
gives slope `∓πK` V/cycle, so `dθ/dt = ∓kvco·πK·θ` and over one period `|λ|_phase = exp(∓π·kvco·K·T)`.

| | measured | predicted | rel |
|---|---|---|---|
| stable (accumulator seeded +0.25 cycle) | **0.999371484** | 0.999371879 | 4.0e-07 |
| saddle (natural zero seed) | **1.000628121** | 1.000628516 | 3.9e-07 |

Across `K` = 1e-3…1e-1 the agreement runs **4e-09 to 4e-05**, and a second, independent axis — `kvco` at fixed `K` —
reproduces it to **all printed digits wherever the product `kvco·K` matches** (1.000062830 at both `(1e-3, 2e4)` and
`(1e-2, 2e3)`). So the loop-gain product governs, and one axis could not have carried that alone.

⚠ **The negative control holds**: at `K = 0` the driven fixed-period solve does **not** converge — correctly
underdetermined, exactly as the roadmap predicted.

⚠⚠ **CONVERGENCE DOES NOT SELECT STABILITY.** A multiplier PD has two equilibria per cycle (`θ = ±1/4`), and from
the natural zero seed the solver converges happily onto the **saddle**. Lock has to be read from the multiplier —
the same lesson A6's injection-locking gates already carry. ⚠ And the branch is selected by the integrator's
**accumulator**, not by the `ph` node: `ph` is a dependent output, and seeding it alone never left the saddle.

⚠ **A false alarm of my own on the way, worth recording because of its shape.** The first pass read as a failure —
the multiplier "stayed at ~1 and did not fall". The deviations were **6.28e-5, 1.88e-4, 6.28e-4, −1.887e-3,
6.26e-3** against a predicted `±6.28e-5 … ±6.28e-3`: the magnitudes already matched to 3–4 digits and only the SIGN
varied. *A quantity that matches in magnitude and flips in sign is a branch, not a defect.*

**What this settles for A6**: steps 1, 3 and 4 (the PFD or a phase-domain detector, lock detection, and phase noise
through the loop) are a build. The practical obstacle is unchanged and is not about correctness — a PLL's period is
the reference period while the VCO runs N× faster, so realistic N waits on envelope-following (A5). Scratch
`a6_step2.py`, `a6_step2b.py`.

### The flicker residual is a LIMIT, not a corner — and my convergence prediction was right in direction, wrong in magnitude (2026-09-18)

**npts 4000 landed and our side converges — faster than I predicted.** Their ours/reference ladder reads
1.0010587 / 1.0012034 / 1.0012406 at npts 1000 / 2000 / 4000, i.e. successive differences **+1.447e-04 →
+3.726e-05, ratio 3.88** — about second order. "Your side is not converging" is withdrawn by them; it rested on one
interval measured twice, as this log said.

⚠ **MY PREDICTION WAS +7e-05 AND THE MEASUREMENT IS +3.726e-05, so it is worth separating what it did from what it
did not do.** It worked as a **discriminator** — converging vs not converging were the two hypotheses, and it
separated them before the run on a number neither side could tune afterwards. It failed as a **quantity**, and the
failure has a name: **I took an order measured on MY fixture (first) and predicted with it on THEIRS (second).**
The hypothesis only implied "the difference shrinks by at least two"; the 7e-05 carried an extra assumption I did
not flag. *State the prediction the hypothesis actually implies, not the one a second fixture's constant suggests.*

**So the number to explain is now a LIMIT**: Richardson puts it at ~+0.125 % against the reference at its default,
and **~+0.079 %** once the reference's own −4.6e-04 grid error is removed — against a 3e-05 scatter. Every named
candidate is eliminated: C contradicted, B refuted on both sideband counts, the two DC densities identical to
1e-8, our colour-fit path null at 3.8e-14, our stationary-1/f fold gated at 1e-3, and A now measured and
converging on both sides.

⚠ **The coupled knobs are separated and it is the grid**: at the clock-stationary bias npts alone carries
+1.7e-02 and the sideband count adds +3.5e-03. But that bias is also where the REFERENCE is unconverged in its own
sideband count (0.8 % between two valid counts), so it is the least trustworthy row either side holds; the
fixture-amplitude ladder is the solid one.

✅ **THEIR TIME-INVARIANT CONTROL FAILED, AND IT IS NOT OURS — I went looking for a mis-branch and did not find
one.** With both sources DC the circuit takes the autonomous path and the free-period Jacobian goes singular.
`_is_autonomous` is structural and deliberate, and its docstring already gives the reason: a circuit driven only by
DC *"has a constant `u` and a one-parameter family of periodic solutions, which is what makes fixed-period shooting
ill-posed for it."* There is genuinely no unique fixed-period solution to find. Their own diagnosis was right and
better than my suspicion.

⚠ **The OPPOSITE failure is real and silent, and it is the one to watch whenever a control shrinks the drive.** The
predicate is `max|u(t) − u(0)| > AUTONOMOUS_U_TOL · max(|u(0)|, 1)` with `AUTONOMOUS_U_TOL = 1e-12`, so their
planned 0.01 V clock clears it by ten orders. The reason to check at all: this used to STRIDE the grid, and clocks
at 5 %, 1 % and 0.5 % duty were all read as autonomous then — routed to the free-period system, *"which solves for
`T` and DISCARDS the period the caller asked for."*

💡 **A timeout lesson from their side, checked here rather than filed**: a 900 s guard killed an entire refinement
ladder on its finest rung, losing rungs already simulated — a finer step is a longer run, so a fixed guard scales
the wrong way. Checked: our ladders bank each rung as a flushed line, so a timeout costs the unfinished rung only;
and the suite now carries **no timeout at all** — since adopting their detaching tip it runs under `setsid nohup`
with a watcher polling the log (four full suites, all completed, 27–29 min). That failure mode would have hit the
retired chunked arrangement squarely.

### The frequency axis is the knob nobody varied — and our side is invariant to it (2026-09-18)

With every named candidate eliminated, the observation that opened a new one: **every knob either side has swept is a
time-grid or sideband-count knob** — npts, maxsidebands, max-step, harmonic count. The **frequency axis** has never
been varied, and for a 1/f source that is precisely where a *point-evaluated* fold and a *bin-integrated* one part
company, because the density is steepest near DC.

It fits all four surviving constraints, which is why it was worth testing: a reference integrating the density over
a frequency BIN where we evaluate it AT a point differs by an amount that is flat in frequency in the flicker part,
invariant to both grids and both sideband counts (the near-DC band is always present), leaves the DC densities
identical (both sides compared densities *at* frequencies, never integrals *over* bands), and grows as the
modulation falls.

✅ **OUR HALF IS INVARIANT TO MACHINE PRECISION.** Flicker part at 100 Hz, npts 1000, msb 200, fixed phase, varying
only the frequency LIST:

| list | part @100 Hz | rel |
|---|---|---|
| `[100]` alone | 2.5147028211e-11 | — |
| `[10, 100]` | 2.5147028211e-11 | 2.2e-16 |
| `[1, 10, 100]` | 2.5147028211e-11 | −6.7e-16 |
| `[100, 1e3, 1e4]` | 2.5147028211e-11 | 0.0 |
| 20-pt logspace 10…1e4 | 2.5147028211e-11 | 2.2e-16 |

⚠ That also settles a second route found while reading the fold: `_sampled_series` anchors its colour fit at
**`min(freqs)`**, so those rows fit the colour at 100, 10 and 1 Hz — and it makes no difference, because this
fixture's density is white plus an exact 1/f, which *is* the fit form `A + B(w1/w)^EF`. (On a density outside that
form the anchor could matter, and the fit's two verify points at `2 f0` and `150 f0` are what would catch it.)

So the test is asymmetric and one run on the other side: vary the **sweep resolution** with every time-domain knob
pinned, reading a fixed frequency present in every sweep. If their value moves, the residual is a **reporting
convention**; if it is invariant like ours, this candidate dies too. ⚠ Probe where flicker DOMINATES (10–100 Hz) —
a near-DC binning difference is diluted to nothing at 40 kHz, and an invariant reading there would prove less than
it appears. Scratch `freqaxis.py`.

### A6 step 4: the loop shapes its VCO's phase noise, against a closed form with nothing fitted (Andreas: "Do as you suggest")

Step 2 licensed this: with the marginal mode pinned, the locked loop is an ordinary driven PSS and the whole noise
stack applies. The payoff is that the answer has a **closed form**, so it needs no reference tool at all.

`VcoHdl` injects white FREQUENCY noise of PSD `sf`, and the `ph` node carries phase in **cycles**, so
`S_φ,free = sf/(4π²f_m²)`. A first-order loop high-passes it at `f_c`, and the RC's own pole makes the loop second
order:

    S_φ,closed(f_m) = sf (1 + 2 f_c/f_RC) / (4π² (f_m² + f_c²))

**Nothing in that is fitted.** `f_c` is read from the **Floquet multiplier**, which never looks at noise —
`|λ| = exp(−2π f_c T)` gives **100.063 Hz** against `kvco·K/2 = 100.000` — and `2f_c/f_RC` comes from R and C.
Measured agreement ~1e-4 across three decades of offset, and **grid-independent to eight digits** (npts 400 / 800 /
1600 all read 1.00125857 before the second-pole term was included).

⚠⚠ **A UNITS ERROR OF MINE, AND THE SHAPE IS WHAT CAUGHT IT.** The first comparison read **0.0253** — a factor of
39.5 — at *every* offset. `1/0.02533 = 4π²`: I had applied `VcoHdl`'s docstring formula `S_φ = sf/f_m²`, which is
the **radian** form, to a node carrying **cycles**. Both are right; mixing them is not. **A scale error that is flat
across decades is a UNITS problem; a shape error would have been physics.** I nearly reported a factor-39
discrepancy as a finding.

⚠ **AND THE SECOND-POLE TERM WAS FOUND, NOT ASSUMED.** With the convention fixed, a **constant 1.2586e-3** remained.
Two hypotheses died before the right one: it is **not discretisation** (identical to eight digits at npts 400, 800,
1600 — my prediction that it would halve was wrong), and it is **not the loop's first pole** (identical at
f_m = 10 Hz, below the corner, and at 10 kHz, a hundred times above it, where the loop does nothing). It scales
linearly on **two independent knobs**:

| knob | residual | 2·f_c/f_RC |
|---|---|---|
| `Cf` = 1e-10 | 1.2569e-4 | 1.2568e-4 |
| `Cf` = 1e-9 | 1.2586e-3 | 1.2576e-3 |
| `Cf` = 1e-8 | 1.2716e-2 | 1.2647e-2 |
| `Cf` = 1e-9, PD gain ÷100 | 1.257e-5 | 1.2566e-5 |

So it is the loop filter's second pole — real physics of a second-order loop, not an error on any side.

⚠ **Step 2's saddle warning is load-bearing here**: the fixture must be seeded on the STABLE branch (accumulator
+0.25 cycle), since noise about an unstable orbit is meaningless, and the natural zero seed lands on the saddle.
The test asserts `|λ| < 1` before it reads any noise. ⚠ One conditioning limit met on the way: at `Cf = 1e-7` the
adjoint solve refuses (`relative residual 1.788e-08`), naming the cause — near a harmonic the operator is genuinely
near-singular. Test `test_a_locked_loop_shapes_its_vco_phase_noise_with_nothing_fitted` (6 s). Scratch
`a6_step4.py`, `a6_step4b.py`.

### A6 step 3: the PLL lock range, gated as a LOCUS — a third independent route to one constant (Andreas: "Continue as you recommend")

Steps 2 and 4 had already pinned `f_c = kvco·K/2` twice, from a Floquet multiplier and from a noise corner. The
hold-in range of a first-order loop is **the same number**: the VCO can be pulled by at most `kvco·(K/2)`, so
`|f0 − fref| ≤ Δf_max = kvco·K/2`. That makes this a **third route to one constant, from a third physical
quantity** — a multiplier, a noise spectrum, and a detuning edge.

**And the whole locus is predicted, not just its edge.** Inside the range the equilibrium sits at
`cos(2πθ) = −Δf/Δf_max`, so the linearised rate is `kvco·πK·|sin(2πθ)|` and, with step 4's second pole,

    −ln|λ| / (π·kvco·K·T) = s + s²·(f_c/f_RC),    s = √(1 − x²),  x = Δf/Δf_max

a unit semicircle in `x`, tilted by the RC. Measured:

| Δf (Hz) | x | normalised | predicted | rel |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0.00 | 1.000629116 | 1.000628319 | 8.0e-07 |
| 40 | 0.40 | 0.917043604 | 0.917042927 | 7.4e-07 |
| 60 | 0.60 | 0.800402655 | 0.800402124 | 6.6e-07 |
| 90 | 0.90 | 0.436009662 | 0.436009275 | 8.9e-07 |
| 99 | 0.99 | 0.141080973 | 0.141079863 | 7.9e-06 |

**Six to seven digits with nothing fitted** — `Δf_max` and `f_c` from `kvco` and `K`, `f_RC` from R and C. Lock is
lost between **99 and 100.5 Hz**, bracketing `Δf_max = 100 Hz`, and no locked branch survives at 105 or 120 Hz.

⚠ **Step 4's second-pole term was what closed it.** Without it the locus matched only to 6.3e-4 — and that residual
was not scatter: it read exactly `f_c/f_RC` at Δf = 0 and fell with the *effective* loop rate, which is why the
correction carries an extra `s`. A term discovered while chasing a constant offset in the noise gate turned out to
predict the tilt of the lock-range curve, which is the sort of agreement that is hard to arrange by accident.

⚠ Seeded on the STABLE branch and continued, never from a fixed seed — step 2 showed the natural seed lands on the
saddle, and A6's own injection-locking record says a fixed seed hops branches. Test
`test_the_pll_lock_range_is_the_loop_bandwidth_and_the_whole_locus_is_predicted` (115 s); the step-2 test is
unchanged (the detuning parameter defaults to zero, and it re-ran at its old 111 s). Scratch `a6_step3.py`.

### The flicker campaign at LOW MODULATION: our side converges, and our trend runs the other way (2026-09-18)

Candidate B was refuted at the **fixture amplitude** only, while the excess grows at **low** amplitude — an
untested regime, and one where folding could plausibly differ (the switch stays partly on, the response is wider
band, and 1/f concentrates near DC). ⚠ The control used there does not cover it either: **a clean flicker-OFF row
validates the WHITE path's sideband adequacy, not the FLICKER path's.**

⚠ **A mislabelled first attempt, corrected**: with `vs = 50 mV`, a clock amplitude of 0.1 V still swings the
control ±2 softening widths and modulates `g` over 0.018…0.982 of `gon` — nearly FULL modulation. "Near-stationary"
needs `va ≪ vs`. The rerun reaches ±3 % (`g` swinging 0.470…0.530).

✅ **OUR SIDE IS CONVERGED AT EVERY DEPTH, one knob at a time:**

| knob | result |
|---|---|
| npts 500/1000/2000 | differences halve at every amplitude (ratios 1.94 … 2.00) |
| msb 100/200/400 | differences **1.3e-18 absolute at every amplitude** — a numerical floor, not a truncation (3e-06 relative even where the part is smallest) |

⚠⚠ **AND THE TREND RUNS OPPOSITE TO THEIRS, which may be the useful part.** Our flicker part against clock
amplitude: **2.51e-11 → 2.06e-12 → 5.45e-13 → 4.39e-13**, i.e. it FALLS by 57× as modulation falls. Theirs RISES
by 29.9× over the comparable range. The fixtures differ in exactly one relevant way: ours has a **constant-PSD**
flicker current source, theirs the **device's own bias-dependent** flicker. So their growth looks like the SOURCE
getting louder as the switch sits mid-conduction rather than the sampling doing something new — which reframes the
target. The narrow question then is not "why does the part grow" but "why does the EXCESS grow FASTER than the
part" (291× against 5.3×), a ratio of two quantities that both scale.

⚠ Suggested to them, needing no PSS: compare the device's DC flicker density at the mid-conduction bias against
the fixture-amplitude operating point; if that ratio is near 29.9×, the sampling is not implicated at low
modulation at all. ⚠ Limit stated plainly: our fixture **cannot** reproduce their trend by construction, since a
constant-PSD source cannot get louder — so this is a pointer, not a result about their circuit. Scratch
`lowmod.py`.

### The A5 spike: plain PSS on a PLL reaches N ≈ 32, and the binding constraint is CONVERGENCE, not cost (Andreas: "Do 1 and 2")

A5 (envelope-following) is marked LAST, and that ordering was set when nothing depended on it — no longer true,
since A6's analysis half is demonstrated only at N = 4…8. Rather than assume A5 is needed, the cost was measured,
in the shape that made E2 decidable: a spike producing a number, and the number deciding.

**Held fixed so only N varies**: VCO at `N·fref` with modulus N, `kvco ∝ N` so the loop bandwidth stays 100 Hz, a
constant **32 points per VCO cycle** (npts = 32N), and a smooth phase-domain divider rather than `DividerHdl`'s
limiter (a cost measurement should not also be an event-handling measurement). ✅ The design check held: **|λ| =
0.999371 at every converged N**, identical to the N=1 loop, so the loop dynamics really were invariant.

| N | npts | fixed seed | continuation |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 32 | 0.19 s | 0.19 s |
| 2 | 64 | 1.01 s | 0.82 s |
| 4 | 128 | 3.96 s | 1.65 s |
| 8 | 256 | 2.64 s | 7.89 s |
| 16 | 512 | 4.48 s | 23.31 s |
| 32 | 1024 | **not converged** | 36.75 s |
| 64 | 2048 | 248.98 s | **not converged** |

⚠ **MY PREDICTION OF A POWER LAW IN N WAS WRONG IN SHAPE.** The fixed-seed timings are non-monotone — N=8 (2.64 s)
beats N=4 (3.96 s) — because cost here is dominated by Newton iteration count, not by grid size. Over the clean
continuation range N = 4…32 it does behave, at roughly **N^1.5** (1.65 → 36.75 s for 8× the points: npts ∝ N *and*
iterations growing).

⚠⚠ **THE BINDING CONSTRAINT IS CONVERGENCE FRAGILITY, NOT COST — and this is the spike's actual deliverable.**
Each seeding strategy produces exactly ONE failure, **at a different N**: the fixed seed fails at 32 and succeeds
at 64; continuation succeeds at 32 and fails at 64. The failure *moves with the seeding*, so it is a basin
problem rather than a size limit. Extrapolating the clean range gives ~3 min/solve at N = 100 and ~2 h at N = 1000
— but that extrapolation ignores the failures, which are the larger obstacle and do not obviously shrink with a
better integrator.

**So the recommendation is NOT "build A5 next".** Envelope-following would address cost; it would not obviously
address a seed-dependent basin. The cheaper attack is the seeding itself — A6's own record notes the PROBE
technique "widens the basin rather than removing the dependence", and it already exists (B5). A5's case should be
re-made after seeding is addressed, on a fixture that then converges reliably. Scratch `a5_spike.py`.

### Do PSS and PAC/pnoise support a non-uniform grid? Yes — but the FOLD drops from spectral to first-order accuracy (asked 2026-09-19)

**PSS: yes, and by design.** `solve(grid=...)` takes a frozen non-uniform grid of step FRACTIONS, with
`lte_grid`, `refine_grid` and `event_grid` to derive them. `_period_grid`'s docstring gives the reason: *"a
transient adapts because it cannot see the future; PSS re-solves the SAME interval over and over, so it can be
handed a grid that was chosen well ONCE and then frozen. The grid still never moves inside a solve, so the
shooting Newton stays exact — **freezing is what makes it a Newton**."* Measured payoff on vdP μ=100: 1105 steps
taken from an adaptive transient converge where 1105 uniform steps do not, and beat a 20000-point uniform grid.
⚠ So **adaptive-derived, yes; adaptive DURING the solve, no** — and deliberately not.

**PAC/pnoise: they accept it and remain CONSISTENT, but lose the spectral accuracy of the harmonic extraction.**
The coloured/cyclostationary folds take `np.fft.fft(Cs, axis=0)` over the per-step samples with **no time
argument** — the sample index is treated as uniform time — and nothing in the noise path checks uniformity. That
reads like a silent wrong answer, so it was measured rather than asserted.

**The test**: switched-cap fixture (a genuinely time-varying `CY` — an LTI circuit could not detect this, since
the FFT of a constant is exact at any spacing), known answer kT/C, computed two ways on the same solve —
`covariance` (per-step Lyapunov, uses each step's own `h`, **no FFT**) as control, and `sampled_variance` (the FFT
fold) as test. Non-uniform grid of the **same point count** and the **same 3:1 shape at every N**, so a
uniformity-assuming fold would leave an error that does not vanish.

| N | covariance | sampled_variance | gap | ratio | gap on a UNIFORM grid |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 200 | 0.98350481 | 0.98399992 | 4.951e-04 | | 2.4e-06 |
| 400 | 0.99762180 | 0.99670587 | 9.159e-04 | 0.54 | 2.1e-06 |
| 800 | 1.00000364 | 0.99954677 | 4.569e-04 | **2.00** | 2.0e-06 |
| 1600 | 1.00017610 | 0.99994839 | 2.277e-04 | **2.01** | 2.0e-06 |

✅ **The gap HALVES per doubling from N = 400, so the fold is consistent — my suspicion of a silent defect is
refuted by measurement.** ⚠ But on a uniform grid the two routes agree at a **2e-06 floor at every N** (the DFT of
period samples is spectrally accurate there), while on a non-uniform grid the agreement is **first order** and
still 100× above that floor at N = 1600.

⚠⚠ **THE PRACTICAL CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS THE POINT: the two capabilities do not compose as well as one would
assume.** A non-uniform grid buys the PSS solve up to 18× fewer points, and then costs the noise fold roughly two
decades of points to reach the accuracy a uniform grid gives for free. **Use a derived grid to make a stiff PSS
converge; do not assume the same grid is a good one for the noise analysis on top of it.** Scratch
`nonuniform.py`.

### The harmonic extraction is an INDEX DFT: on a scale-invariantly non-uniform grid it does not converge (2026-09-19)

Asked why professional shooting simulators all use adaptive grids if non-uniform grids cost accuracy. The answer is
that they are right and **our quadrature is the problem** — so the earlier framing in `6622f50` ("the two
capabilities do not compose") is corrected here: they do not compose *in this implementation*, which is a
different and fixable statement.

**It is a Fourier INTEGRAL.** `C_k = (1/T)∫₀ᵀ C(t)e^{−j2πkt/T}dt`, evaluated as a discrete sum. On a uniform grid
that sum **is the periodic trapezoid rule**, which for a smooth periodic function converges **spectrally** — every
Euler–Maclaurin boundary term cancels. That is why the uniform case sits at a floor instead of converging at an
order. Isolated on an analytic integrand (the switch conductance), against dense-quadrature truth:

| N | uniform, index DFT | **non-uniform, index DFT (what the code does)** | non-uniform, true phase + `h_n` | non-uniform, trapezoid weights |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 64 | 1.635e-04 | 5.958e-01 | 1.847e-02 | 6.022e-03 |
| 1024 | **2.411e-16** | **5.912e-01** | 1.199e-03 | 3.138e-06 |
| rate | **spectral** | **1.00 — none** | 2.00 (first order) | 4.00 (second order) |

⚠⚠ **The index DFT on a non-uniform grid does not converge at all** — a flat 59 % error at k=1 (133 % at k=3).
Both the phase (`2πkn/N`) and the weight (`1/N`) assume equal steps; `adjoint_sideband_row` uses the true times
for the phase but still a flat `1/N` weight. ⚠ And **no non-uniform quadrature recovers spectral accuracy**:
correct `h_n` weights buy first order, trapezoid weights second. The remedy for full accuracy is to **interpolate
onto a uniform grid before transforming**, which is what professional tools do.

**End to end, on the suite's own cyclostationary IDENTITY** (the same physics two ways; pinned at 1e-12 uniform):

| grid | A stationary | B cyclostationary | B/A | rate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| uniform, N = 200…800 | 5.8548e-02 | 5.8548e-02 | **1.00000000** | — |
| non-uniform (3:1), N = 200 | 4.1152e-02 | 2.9910e-02 | 0.7268 | |
| non-uniform (3:1), N = 800 | 4.1226e-02 | 2.9933e-02 | **0.7261** | **1.00** |

A **27 % identity break that does not converge**, and the stationary route shifts 30 % to a different limit. ⚠ No
orbit confound is possible: that circuit's operating point is static and its only time variation is an analytic
source, so the difference is entirely the fold.

✅ **THE SHIPPING DEFAULT IS NOT IMPLICATED, and I checked rather than assumed.** `break_events` defaults ON for
one-step methods, which sounded like it would hand users a non-uniform grid unasked — but on the fixture tested it
produces a **uniform** grid with an adjusted step count (204 steps against 199; `1 distinct h`, max/min =
