1.000000). The 48 %/30 %/15 % spread it causes at npts 200/400/800 is ordinary discretisation between two uniform
grids, halving cleanly. **The exposure is to `grid=` — i.e. `lte_grid` and `refine_grid`** — whose non-uniformity
is *scale-invariant by design* and therefore does not wash out with refinement, unlike a fixed number of snapped
event points.

⚠⚠ **THREE VACUOUS FIXTURES ON THE WAY, ALL THE SAME SHAPE — a fixture that cannot respond to the thing under
test.** (1) An orbit control that compared a waveform which was identically zero on both grids. (2) An event
fixture whose event times (0.17 T, 0.31 T at npts 200) landed exactly on grid points, so the snap changed nothing.
(3) That same fixture being LTI with a **constant** `CY`, where the FFT of a constant is exact at any spacing.
*Before running a control, ask what it would read if the defect were present.* Scratch `quadrature.py`,
`cyclo_nonuniform.py`, `default_bites2.py`.

### Should the Fourier quadrature match the transient's integrator? Measured: YES — its order must reach the integrator's (Andreas, 2026-09-19)

Driven linear RC, exact `C_1 = H/(2j)`, harmonics taken from REAL PSS samples (which carry the integrator's
error, unlike the analytic integrand used before). Relative error at N = 800 and the rate per doubling:

| method (order) | uniform, index DFT | non-uniform 3:1, `h`-weights (rectangle) | non-uniform 3:1, trapezoid weights |
|---|---|---|---|
| gear (2) | 1.82e-05, rate **4.0** | 3.98e-04, rate 1.9 | 2.45e-05, rate **4.0** |
| trap (2) | 4.55e-06, rate **4.0** | 4.09e-04, rate 2.0 | 5.45e-06, rate **4.0** |
| radau (5) | 1.66e-15, rate **32** | 4.13e-04, rate 2.0 | 1.18e-06, rate 4.0 |

* **Uniform: SAMPLE error dominates** — the harmonic's error tracks the integrator's order exactly, so the DFT's
  spectral accuracy is never the bottleneck (the earlier 2.4e-16 came from an analytic integrand).
* **`h`-weighting is the bottleneck for EVERY method** (rate 2.0 throughout; 22× worse than uniform for gear, 11
  orders for radau). ⚠ That is the form `coloured_diffusion_resolved` uses, so propagating it as-is is the wrong fix.
* **Trapezoid weights suffice for the second-order methods** (within 1.2–1.35× of the uniform error) and remain
  the bottleneck for radau by nine orders.

**Rule: the quadrature's order must reach the integrator's.** Trapezoid for gear/trap; radau needs a fifth-order
rule, i.e. its own collocation polynomial (its stage values — this test used step endpoints only). Scratch
`sample_vs_quad.py`.

### Level 3: dual consistency does NOT select the quadrature — accuracy does, and trapezoid weights fix the sideband row (2026-09-19)

`adjoint_sideband_row` is the exact discrete adjoint of (forward driven solve → DFT sideband `l`): its inject
weights `e^{-j(l w0 + w) t_n}/N` ARE the DFT row. So the identity closes for ANY weight vector used on both sides —
which is how the shipped `1/N` passes its 1e-15 dual-consistency check on a non-uniform grid and is still 30 % wrong.

Measured by patching the weight in a scratch copy of the method (source untouched), on circuit A of the
cyclostationary identity (constant `CY`, so only the sideband rows matter), 3:1 grid, against a uniform reference:

| N | `1/N` shipped | `h_n/T` rectangle | trapezoid | uniform, same N |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 200 | 2.971e-01 | 2.485e-03 | 1.765e-04 | 6.6e-09 |
| 800 | 2.959e-01 | 6.003e-04 | 1.090e-05 | 3.1e-10 |
| rate | 1.00 — none | 2.0 | **4.0** | |

Same verdict as level 1: `1/N` converges to the wrong limit, the rectangle rule is first order, trapezoid weights
reach the integrator's (gear's) second order. ⚠ Uniform remains four orders better here, so a weight fix makes
derived grids CORRECT, not competitive. **Rule for the fix: the SAME trapezoid weight vector on both sides** —
the adjoint inject and the forward solve's `freq_analysis`. Scratch `level3.py`.

### The flicker residual has a candidate address: the coloured fold takes sqrt(PSD), which cannot carry a SIGN (2026-09-19)

**What the other side found.** Mapping the ratio against clock amplitude shows the "grows as modulation falls" trend
was three points straddling a feature: the reference's sampled flicker part has a **sharp notch at clock amplitude
0.20** (down ~57× from its neighbour, converged in every reference knob to under 2 %), while ours rises smoothly
through it — so the ratio peaks at ~567 because the reference nearly cancels, not because ours spikes. Also closed
by them: the frequency-axis candidate (their sweep resolution changes nothing at 10 and 100 Hz), the tracking
dispute (their 0.972 floor was the reference's SIDEBAND COUNT: msb 400/800/1600 → 0.97239/0.98459/0.99021, heading
to kT/C as the theorem says), and the 0.375 row's sideband convergence (measured on the flicker row itself).

**The candidate.** 1/f noise is correlated across the whole cycle, so contributions from different instants add
COHERENTLY: the low-frequency part is `|Σ h(t) m(t)|²`. In the ordered-device formulation the flicker current is
multiplied back by `sgn(Vds)`, so `m(t)` is signed and the two conduction polarities can CANCEL. Our PSP element
contributes `flicker_noise(n_sfl * sgn²)` — a PSD — and the fold takes its square root, i.e. `|m|`, so the lobes
always ADD. `_cyclostationary_fold` already documents this ("no PSD-specified model can represent the coloured
process … this fold computes the |m| one … Only the element knows the sign", measured 0.56× / 1.33×), but the check
lives only in the pnoise fold and looks for a KINK in sqrt(PSD), which our smooth `sgn² = Vds²/(Vds²+4e-8)` does not
have. It fits every accumulated constraint: white noise is uncorrelated across instants (flicker-OFF 1.00005), a
density is sign-blind (DC densities identical to 1e-8), invariant to both grids and sideband counts, flat in
frequency. ⚠ My candidate C failed because I tied it to "modulated vs stationary"; the variable is whether `m(t)`
CHANGES SIGN, and a DC clock does not stop `Vds = v_in − v_out` swinging through zero with the input sine.

**Reproduced in shape here, reference-free** (flicker through one multiplier, `V_lo = vo + sin`; A keeps the sign
and is exact, B is the PSD-specified source through the sqrt(PSD) fold):

| vo | A signed | B sqrt(PSD) | B/A |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.5, 1.0 (sign-definite) | 2.2505e-03, 1.0005e-03 | identical | **1.000** |
| 0.5 | 2.5050e-04 | 5.1572e-04 | 2.06 |
| 0.1 | 1.0500e-05 | 4.0941e-04 | 39 |
| 0.0 | 5.0000e-07 | 4.0531e-04 | **811** |

⚠ NOT YET CONFIRMED on the PSP sampler: the checks sent are whether Vds changes sign during conduction with the
signed integral crossing zero near 0.20, and whether zeroing the input sine collapses the excess at every clock
amplitude. If it holds, the fix is a modelling change — the element hands the fold a SIGNED flicker amplitude
instead of a PSD. Scratch `signed_notch.py`.

### 2026-09-19 — The flicker residual's mechanism is CONFIRMED by the peer, on a route independent of our fold

The candidate above (the coloured fold takes `sqrt(PSD)`, which cannot carry the
SIGN of a flicker current that follows `sgn(Vds)`) was checked in the private
tree without using anything of our fold. At 10 Hz against a 100 kHz clock the
1/f source is constant over ~1e4 periods, so the low-frequency sampled flicker is
the PERIODIC steady state of our own linearised orbit driven by the flicker
modulation `m(t)`, read at the sample instant; `m(t)` is taken from the element's
own CY, signed or unsigned. Prediction: `ours / reference = (H_unsigned / H_signed)^2`.

    clock amp   measured ratio   predicted
    0.75        1.0011           1.0009      <- the fixture: the original "+0.1 %"
    0.60        1.0027           1.0026
    0.50        1.0073           1.0072
    0.375       1.0582           1.0616
    0.30        1.3869           1.4245
    0.25        3.2383           3.6162
    0.20        567              2.9e6       <- H_signed ~ 0: the notch
    0.15        5.8666           5.1982
    0.10        2.2728           2.1836
    0.0         1.1963           1.2034

Their self-check: our measured flicker part over `H_unsigned^2` is one scale
factor to +-7 % across all ten amplitudes, so the unsigned reduction tracks our
fold everywhere and the sign is the only thing changed. ⚠ What it does NOT
claim: the notch DEPTH (567 = a cancellation filled at ~1.8e-3, the size of the
k != 0 terms the reduction drops -- said before the run), nor agreement beyond
the +-7 %.

**So the residual that survived every grid, sideband, tolerance and
frequency-axis knob was never numerical.** Vds changes sign while the device
conducts at EVERY amplitude including the fixture's (two crossings per cycle),
and the sign is discarded before the fold. ⚠ My check (2) -- zero the input sine
-- cannot discriminate here: it also drives Vds to zero, and PSP flicker goes as
Vds^2, so there is no flicker left.

NOT BUILT. The fix is a modelling change: the element hands the fold a SIGNED
amplitude `sgn(Vds) sqrt(Sfl)` instead of the PSD `n_sfl*sgn^2`, and the coloured
fold accepts one. The gate falls out for free: the sampled flicker part must dip
by > 50x between clock amplitudes 0.25 and 0.20 -- a sign-blind fold cannot.

### 2026-09-19 — The sites the pnoise fix left on an index DFT: measured, converted; and a phase multiplier that is not 1

Measured on a 3:1 grid against the uniform answer (van der Pol, a = 0.30) and
against an exact one (driven RC, `H/(2j)`):

    site                 before                     after
    carrier_phasor       7.5 % off, not converging  6.2e-04 / 1.7e-04 (N = 400/800)
    fpss                 index DFT (disagreed with carrier_phasor by 59 %)   = sqrt(2) x carrier_phasor
    modal_spectrum       8-13 % off, not converging total/(pnoise/2) = 1.0134 / 1.0029 / 1.0006 / 1.0001
    orbital_correlation  same `fcoef`               converted with it
    ProbeShooting._spectrum   UNAFFECTED by construction: it solves its own uniform grid

pnoise itself, fixed in 3bdc8ff, tracks the uniform answer on the oscillator at
0.9987 / 0.9997 (N = 400/800) -- the fix holds on an autonomous circuit too.

⚠ NOT CLEAN: each of modal_spectrum's three PARTS sits a common ~0.2 % below the
uniform value with no clean order (+1.9e-2, -1.3e-3, -3.1e-3, -2.2e-3 at
N = 200..1600); the total closes because the parts cancel 1.5:1. Unexplained,
and unreachable by a user, because:

**The phase multiplier is 1 only while the discretisation is time-translation
invariant.** On a uniform grid a shifted orbit is again a discrete solution and
the multiplier reads 1 + 6e-12; a non-uniform `grid=` breaks that at O(h^2):
1 - 2.1e-04 / 5.1e-05 at N = 200/400. Two consequences:
 * `modal_spectrum` finds no mode inside its 1e-6 window and REFUSES. Kept, and
   the message now says why. (The numbers above were taken with the mode forced.)
 * `ppv`'s dense path used the same window to DROP the phase multiplier, found
   nothing to drop, and reported the phase multiplier as `second_multiplier`:
   0.99995 for 0.96875, silently, f_amp 600x too small. ⚠ This one was found
   through MY harness going wrong (pnoise "1e6x off" -- the offsets were being
   taken from that f_amp), not by looking for it. Fixed: with nothing in the
   window the multiplier nearest 1 is dropped; a uniform grid never gets there.
   After: 0.96912 / 0.96881 / 0.96875 against 0.96885 / 0.96875 / 0.96874.

### 2026-09-19 — The signed flicker fix, BUILT (Andreas: "Build the signed flicker fix")

**The contract was already in the HDL and nothing read it.** `white_noise`'s
docstring: *"A scale factor multiplies the AMPLITUDE ... the factors carry the
(possibly complex, possibly signed) transfer."* But `CY = amp^2 * pwr`, and the
periodic folds factor `CY` by its square root, so the sign of `amp` never
reached them. A coloured source is correlated ACROSS the period:
`R(t,t') = k(t) k(t') R_n(t-t')` keeps the sign product.

**Built.**
 * `Element.noise_amplitudes(x, w)` (hdl.py, both compile paths, compiled as
   `funcs['NA']`): the SIGNED injection columns of the coloured sources, `(n, S)`,
   with `CY_coloured = sum_s W_s W_s^dagger`. ⚠ Emitted ONLY where the model
   states a sign: one source whose POWER follows `x` under a constant scale
   factor -- `flicker_noise((k V)^2)`, `kf |ids|^af` -- and the element states
   nothing, because `sqrt(pwr)` would present the |k| process as signed and
   SILENCE the fold's warning. (My first version did exactly that; the test's
   warning assertion caught it.)
 * `PAC._signed_amplitudes`: per flicker component, used only if `W W^dagger`
   rebuilds the fitted component to 1e-6; else sqrt(PSD) and the warning, as
   before. Consumed by `_cyclostationary_fold` (pnoise) and `sampled_noise`.
 * `PspMosLongChannel`: `sgn * flicker_noise(mult * n_sfl, ef)` for
   `flicker_noise(mult * n_sfl * sgn * sgn, ef)`. `CY` is unchanged.

**Gate 1, nothing fitted, no reference.** A constant 1/f source through a signed
multiplier (a stationary fold) is the exact answer. pnoise at 1e-3 f0:

    LO offset   sqrt(PSD)/exact   signed/exact
    1.5           1.000           1.000000000
    0.5           2.059           1.000000000
    0.2          10.415           1.000000000
    0.0         810.627           1.000000000

**Gate 2, the PSP sampler (IHP open PDK card, 200 points, 10 Hz, mid-hold).**
Sign-blind over signed, beside the peer's measured old-fold/reference ratios:

    clock amp      0.75    0.50    0.375   0.30   0.25   0.20   0.15   0.10
    blind/signed   1.0007  1.0069  1.0602  1.420  3.585  401    5.16   2.18
    old/reference  1.0011  1.0073  1.0582  1.387  3.238  567    5.87   2.27

Two independent routes to the same column. The notch at 0.20 is ours now.
⚠ NOT YET DONE: the peer's run of the reference against this commit -- the
table above compares my two folds with THEIR old ratios, which is the right
shape but not the closing measurement.

**Found by the sweep: `_uniform_exponent` let a weightless entry vote.** At 0.375
ALONE the two folds read bit-identical: one orbit sample at Vds ~ 0 carried a
flicker entry of 1.4e-31 against 7.2e-20, its fitted exponent (from differences
of `CY`) was rounding of the white part beside it (1 - 2.2e-09), and that failed
the whole component into the per-band route -- silently, and slower. Entries
below 1e-9 of the scale no longer vote; the reference exponent is the LARGEST
entry's. After: 1.0602. ⚠ A bit-identical pair in a sweep is a fallback, not
agreement.

⚠ NOT CONVERTED, deliberately: the SPICE-style models in `elements_hdl.py`
(`kf * |ids|^af`, seven sites). Their 1/f current presumably follows sgn(ids)
the same way, but that is a modelling decision per device, not measured here;
they state no sign, so they keep the |m| fold AND its warning.

### 2026-09-19 — The rest of the library's flicker sources state their sign too

Six sites in `elements_hdl.py` wrote `flicker_noise(kf |I|^af)` -- a power that
follows `x` under a constant scale factor, so by `noise_amplitudes`' own rule
they stated NO sign and stayed on the |m| fold (with its warning). A 1/f current
is a slow relative conductance fluctuation TIMES the current, so it follows the
current's sign: diode, Gummel-Poon base, EKV, MOS level 1 and 3, Statz MESFET now
write `(I/|I|) * flicker_noise(kf |I|^af)` with `|I| = safe_abs(I)` (eps 1e-30).
`CY` is unchanged to (1e-30/I)^2. Gated per device: the amplitudes rebuild the
flicker part of `CY` (<= 1.6e-11) and flip with the current. The VCO's `kff` is
x-free: nothing to sign. ⚠ THE FIRST GATE FAILED ON THIS, a real regression:
written INLINE the factor enters `CY` squared as `I^2 |I|^(af-2)`, and `I^2`
overflows at the diode's 1e4 V sweep point (`TestNoFloatingPointGarbage`). As a
`var` it is formed once as a ratio and squared at magnitude 1 -- all six sites.
⚠ NOT measured on a circuit: no library fixture drives
one of these through a current reversal under a periodic fold; the PSP sampler
is the measured case.

The fold's 2026-09-09 SCOPE note -- "a DEVICE's own noise has no sign to lose;
sqrt(PSD(x(t))) >= 0 IS the process" -- is WITHDRAWN in place. It was reasoned,
not measured, and it is the sentence that kept this defect out of view: the
warning it scoped away was the right one.

### 2026-09-19 — PLL seeding experiment: convergence at large N is a SEEDING problem, and no single pre-settle rule fixes it

A5 spike follow-up (same loop, f_c = 100 Hz, 32 points per VCO cycle, gear).
Seed = the analytic locked state, optionally pre-settled with `tstab`:

    N     fixed seed        tstab 2T          tstab 8T
    16    5.5 s             21.5 s            13.1 s
    32    FAILS (171 s)     FAILS (165 s)     27.6 s
    64    297 s             153 s             290 s

Every converged run: |lambda| = 0.999371 (the same loop, as designed).
⚠ Wall-clock is single-shot on a shared box with a suite running; read the
CONVERGED / FAILED column, and the times only as factors of several.
 * N = 32 is not a wall: 8 periods of transient turn a 171 s failure into the
   cheapest solve at that N (the spike's 36.75 s included).
 * It is not monotone: 2T fails at 32 and is the best at 64; the fixed seed
   fails at 32 and converges at 64 (the spike's CONTINUATION seed failed at 64).
   Three seedings, three different failure sets -- a fragile Newton basin, not a
   cost curve.
 * So the A5 recommendation stands and sharpens: envelope-following would not
   address this. What would: a seed that is already periodic in the FAST
   variable (the probe technique, B5, or a phase-condition on the VCO state),
   or a globalised Newton on the period map. NOT BUILT.

### 2026-09-19 — The peer's closing run on `accf5a4`: the fix is RIGHT where it engages, and my fallback repair was the wrong KIND

Ours over the reference, flicker part at 10 Hz, 1000 points / 400 sidebands
(ratios only, measured by the peer against an archive of the commit):

    clock amp   old fold   accf5a4     
    0.75        1.0011     1.00024
    0.50        1.0073     1.00017
    0.375       1.0582     1.05823     BIT-IDENTICAL to old  <- fallback
    0.30        1.3869     1.00018
    0.25        3.2383     3.23827     BIT-IDENTICAL to old  <- fallback
    0.20        567        1.036       the notch
    0.15        5.8666     0.99984

My prediction (within scatter of 1 away from the notch; "O(1), not hundreds" at
it) held at the six amplitudes where it could be tested.

⚠⚠ THE TWO FALLBACKS. `_uniform_exponent` again: at 1000 points / 0.25 four
entries at relative weight 7.12e-09 carry an exponent off by 8.05e-09. My repair
in `accf5a4` was a weight cut-off at 1e-9 -- so this entry voted, failed the
1e-9 tolerance, and the component went per-band, sign-blind, reproducing the old
commit TO THE LAST BIT. The exponent's noise goes as 1/weight: NO cut-off
separates noise from signal, and a finer orbit simply lands a different sample
near Vds ~ 0. The test is now on what a wrong exponent COSTS:
`50 r_i d_i <= 1e-9` (relative weight x deviation x 50 e-folds of band
frequency). The measured offenders cost 2e-19 and 3e-15; a genuinely different
exponent still fails from a weight of 2e-11 up.

⚠ I HAD THE RULE AND STILL SHIPPED THE BUG. "A bit-identical pair in a sweep is a
fallback, not agreement" was written down that morning -- after seeing it at ONE
grid. I fixed the instance, not the class, and did not ask what the repair would
do at another grid. The peer's point stands: a fallback that reproduces the old
answer is the most dangerous failure this fix can have, because it looks like
agreement. So it is now VISIBLE: where an element states signed amplitudes and
the component goes per-band anyway, `pnoise(cyclostationary=True)` and
`sampled_noise` warn, naming the element.

⚠ AND THE PEER'S GATE WOULD HAVE PASSED THE BROKEN COMMIT: with 0.25 on the old
path and 0.20 on the new, the dip read 177x. Their gate now also requires
agreement at the EDGE amplitude, and the dip threshold is 10x (the reference
itself dips 56.7x). A gate on a DIFFERENCE between two points passes when either
point is wrong in the helpful direction.

### 2026-09-19 — ⚠⚠ WITHDRAWN: "a fragile Newton basin" AND the A5 spike's cost curve. The PLL solves were CONVERGED; the step test could not see it

Two conclusions of mine, both pushed, both wrong, one cause.
 * The A5 spike (86ed68a): "plain PSS reaches N ~ 32, cost ~ N^1.5, the binding
   constraint is convergence fragility".
 * The seeding experiment (986eac8): "three seedings, three failure sets -- a
   fragile Newton basin; candidates: a fast-variable-periodic seed, or a
   globalised Newton". (`fsolve` already HAS a line search. I had not looked.)

I read both off a CONVERGED / FAILED column without once looking at a residual
history. Looking took one scratch script:

    N = 32, fixed seed          max|F|
    evaluation 0                3.2e+07
    evaluation 1                1.2e-03
    evaluation 2                7.2e-13      <- the solution, and it never moves again
    evaluations 3 .. 172        1e-14 .. 3.7253e-09, at random    -> "NOT CONVERGED"

`3.7253e-09` is 2^-28: ONE ULP of the VCO's frequency node, which is carried as
a node value of 3.2e7. The residual test passes from iteration 2. What fails is
the STEP test, on ONE unknown: the VCO output, a unit sine sampled at its zero
crossing (-1.5e-05), where `reltol |x|` vanishes and only `vabstol = 1e-12`
remains -- against a step that is rounding noise at 4e-12 .. 1e-09. The solve
passed when a draw happened to land under 1e-12 and burned its budget otherwise.
That is the whole of "which seed converges": luck.

    N      before                 vabstol = 1e-9     floor detected, default tolerance
    32     142 s, NOT CONVERGED    5.7 s              10.4 s
    64     297 s                  12.9 s              16.0 s
    128    (never reached)        16.3 s              38.6 s

Same |lambda| = 0.999371 = exp(-pi (kvco/N) K T) in every cell.

**Built, and then REBUILT the same day.** First version: `fsolve(floor_detect=True)`
DECLARED SUCCESS on the signature "the residual test has held for three
consecutive iterations while the step, in units of its tolerance, did not
contract" (a linearly converging Newton contracts every time). It made the PLL
ladder converge at the default tolerance -- 10.4 / 16.0 / 38.6 s -- and the full
suite REFUSED it twice (3243 passed, 2 failed):
 * `test_closing_a_pll_loop...`: with K = 0 the marginal mode makes `I - M`
   singular (multiplier 1 + 1.8e-11) and the test demands that a driven
   fixed-period solve does NOT report convergence. Mine reported it.
 * `test_probe_shooting...`: a placement leaving the inductor's DC free must read
   degenerate. Mine read converged.
**A singular Jacobian has exactly my signature**: the residual is met on a whole
manifold and the step wanders along the null direction. Floor and singularity
are both rounding amplified by the conditioning of `J`; the iteration cannot
tell them apart, and one of them has NO unique solution. Reporting convergence
there is the worst thing this code can do.

So it is COUNTED, NOT ACTED ON -- the rule `fsolve` already states for
`ls_unimproved`, ten lines above where I was editing. The iteration is bit for
bit what it was; the signature goes out in `infodict['step_floor']`; a FAILED
shooting solve that carried it now says so, names both causes, and says what
distinguishes them (`spectral_radius`). What makes the PLL converge is the
tolerance, not the detector -- see the `vabstol` entry below. ⚠ Modelling the
floor instead (`eps * I_scale` through `J^-1`) named the right unknown and
under-stated the noise 18x: rounding accumulates over the traversal.

**What this does to A5.** The cost curve that argued about envelope-following
was mostly iterations spent at the floor. Re-measured with a tolerance above the
floor (`vabstol = 1e-9`) it is 5.7 / 12.9 / 16.3 s at N = 32 / 64 / 128 -- about
LINEAR in N or better, as npts ~ N says it should
be, and it converges at every size tried. Plain PSS is not the wall at N = 32 it
was recorded as. A5 stays last, and now for a measured reason.

⚠ THE RULES I BROKE, both already in the catalogue: "never assert a defect from
a symptom -- look at the residual" and "one interval measured twice is not a
ladder". A pass/fail column is a symptom. The discriminating instrument existed
and cost 140 s.

⚠ NOT DECIDED HERE: `vabstol`'s default of 1e-12 V (SPICE's is 1e-6) is what makes
this reachable, and a VCO model that carries Hz as a node value (3.2e7) is what
puts the floor at 1e-9. Both are design decisions with reasons recorded
elsewhere. (Andreas decided the first the same day: see below.)

### 2026-09-19 — `vabstol`'s default is 1e-6 (Andreas: "Change vabstol to 1e-6 default"), and what it moved

DC, Transient, JAXTransient and PSS together -- the four share one meaning, so
they share one default. `iabstol` stays 1e-12 and so does `lte_vabstol`, which is
the step controller's quantity and not this one. ⚠ The default was 1e-6 once
before and was moved to 1e-12 deliberately (decision 0.3a/0.3d: "loosened node
convergence by 10^6"); Andreas was told that before deciding. The reason now:
1e-12 V is below what double precision delivers on a node once any unknown in
the circuit is large, and the PLL paid 142 s and a false "not converged" for it.
At the new default that ladder reads 8.0 / 11.6 / 23.2 s at N = 32 / 64 / 128,
no warning, |lambda| = 0.999371.

**What moved: 8 tests of 3246.** None re-recorded; each now asks for
`vabstol=1e-12` BY NAME, with a note saying why.
 * Five pin agreement far below a 1e-6 Newton floor and simply need the tight
   solve: the im-D order test (a 1e-13 spread must fall 10x), coupled Radau
   reaching the collocation root to 1e-12 (read 6.6e-12), the Radau transform
   against the dense solve to 1e-9 (read 9e-8), and the stage predictor's
   "same answer to 1e-11" and "no extra steps" (1.12e-11; 442 for 439).
 * One is a PREMISE: the warm-start test needs a cold start that FAILS in 8
   iterations, and at 1e-6 it gets there in 8.
 * ⚠⚠ TWO ARE A REAL DEFECT THE OLD DEFAULT WAS HIDING. `coupled_method=
   'bordered'` (opt-in; `approx` is the default and is unaffected, 93 -> 100
   steps) takes **8828 steps where it took 93** on a driven RC, and 653 for 264
   on the pulse -- same accuracy (2.4e-4), 88x the cost -- at ANY Newton
   tolerance of 1e-9 or looser, and `lte_vabstol` does not rescue it (6156 at
   1e-6). Its loop exits on `converged_x and |dh| < eta h`; at 1e-12 the solution
   test needed extra iterations, and those were incidentally the iterations in
   which `h` grows. Pinned where it was measured and recorded as OPEN in
   HANDOVER. NOT FIXED: the exit logic wants its own look.

⚠ A side effect worth having: `iabstol` and `vabstol` no longer share a value, so
a flavour swap between node rows and branch rows in a `newton_tolerance_vectors`
caller -- "invisible until someone changes one of them", in that function's own
words -- is now visible. The suite found none.

### 2026-09-19 — `bordered` FIXED: eq (12)'s `q^T dv0` term was counted twice, and the old `vabstol` hid it

The defect the default change exposed (8828 steps for 93). My HANDOVER note said
"start by printing `h` and `dh` per iteration"; per time point was enough:

    bordered, vabstol 1e-6                 approx, vabstol 1e-6
    h out / h in = 0.9775, 2 iterations    h out / h in = 1.322, 2 iterations
    (8821 of 8828 points)                  = 1.15 x 1.15

0.9775 = 1.15 x 0.85: `bordered` GROWS on the first iteration and SHRINKS on the
second, every time point, ratcheting down to a crawl. Not "fails to grow".

Eq (12): `dh = -(f + q^T dv0)/denom`, with the LTE residual `f` at the iterate
BEFORE the Newton update and `q^T dv0` predicting how it moves. The code takes
`err` from `_lte_in_band(ctrl, x_stage1, ...)`, `x_stage1 = x + dx0` -- the update
is already in it -- and kept the term. `denom = err w'/w` is tiny wherever the
error is, `q ~ 1/etol`, so a `dx0` of 1e-8 makes the term O(1) and it, not `f`,
picks the sign of `dh`. At `vabstol = 1e-12` the loop kept iterating until `dx0`
had decayed to ~1e-13, and the term with it.

PREDICTED before the run (scratch: the method's source with the term removed,
exec'd): 8828 -> ~100 at 1e-6, 93 unchanged at 1e-12, error unchanged. Measured:
100, 93, 2.500e-4 / 2.490e-4. Held.

⚠ The honest consequence: the term was what the branch's own comment credits
for being "not merely sec. 3.4 in disguise". Without it `bordered` and `approx`
agree to every printed digit on the smooth fixture. The comment is kept, marked
superseded. ⚠ Not revisited: the "TESTED AND REJECTED" degree-slicing note in the
same branch ("that pairing is what the branch's measured record was tuned on")
was tuned with the defect in place.

The two `vabstol=1e-12` pins I had put on the bordered tests are REMOVED -- they
were hiding the defect, which is what the note on them said.

### 2026-09-19 — A library device's signed flicker, gated at CIRCUIT level with an exact answer (no reference)

The six converted SPICE-style models had only an element-level gate ("no library
fixture drives one through a current reversal under a periodic fold"). Closed for
MOS level 1, with the trick that made gate 1 exact: with `af = 2` the device's
signed 1/f amplitude is EXACTLY `kappa ids(t)`, so the same physics is a CONSTANT
1/f source times the SENSED drain current (a CCVS on a series sense branch, into
a multiplier) -- a stationary source through an LPTV gain, which has no sign to
lose. Track-and-hold, drain current reversing twice per period, pnoise at 1e-3 f0:

    ck amp    device signed / exact    device sign-blind / exact
    0.90         1.000113                  13.2
    0.60         1.000047                  22.9
    0.45         1.000028                  33.8
    0.30         1.000050                  58.1

⚠ THE CONTROL NEARLY FOOLED ME. A harness print showed the sensed current as
[0, 0]. Had that been true the multiplier contributed nothing, A was thermal
only, and "B/A = 1.0000" meant nothing except that flicker was negligible -- the
vacuous-control shape, fourth time this campaign. It was my indexing: measured,
the sensed current spans -7.3e-05 .. +1.2e-05 A with two sign changes, and
flicker is 44 % of the exact total. The test ASSERTS both before it compares.

⚠ Scope: MOS level 1 at `af = 2` (the exactness needs amplitude linear in the
current). The other five share the same one-line construction and the same
element-level gate; they are not each measured on a circuit.

constructions -- so naming the tolerance is neutral, the flicker fix breaks

### 2026-09-20 — The peer's WHITE-source disagreement: step 1, reference-free, says it is NOT our fold

The peer measured (ratios only, three grids) that our sampled WHITE noise
through the PSP track-and-hold converges -- first order from below, step ratios
2.24 / 2.32 / 2.38 -- to a NONZERO disagreement with the reference that grows
as the clock closes: ~5e-05 at clock amplitude 0.75, 9.1e-04 at 0.25, 5.5e-03 at
0.15, 7.7e-03 at 0.10. ⚠ Two rungs read as linear divergence; the third showed
convergence -- the two-point trap, caught on their side this time.

Step 1 on ours, nothing from the reference: two of our own routes to the held
noise, at the 5 us sample instant (flicker off, `nfa = nfb = nfc = 0`):

    amp   npts   cov(hold) [V^2]   sampled_variance/cov - 1   W - 1
    0.75   200     4.083e-11            -2.00e-04            -7.7e-12
    0.75   400     4.087e-11            -2.00e-04            -1.5e-12
    0.30   200     3.847e-11            -2.02e-04            -2.2e-06
    0.30   400     3.841e-11            -2.00e-04            -4.2e-07
    0.20   200     4.656e-11            -2.28e-04            -2.8e-05
    0.20   400     4.565e-11            -2.06e-04            -5.9e-06
    0.10   200     9.380e-11            -2.60e-04            -6.0e-05
    0.10   400     9.225e-11            -2.14e-04            -1.4e-05

`cov` is `PAC.covariance` -- the periodic covariance in the time domain, no
fold; `sampled_variance` is the sampled fold integrated over (10 Hz, f_clk/2);
`W = S(10 Hz) f_clk / (2 cov)` is 1 for an uncorrelated sample series.
 * The -2.0e-04 is the BAND: integrating from 10 Hz drops 10 / 50 000 = 2.0e-04
   of a flat spectrum. Predicted from that, matched to 1e-06 at 0.75. Our fold
   and our time-domain covariance agree at every amplitude.
 * The sample series is WHITE to 1e-05 even at amplitude 0.10 (W - 1 converging
   with the grid): the hold settles; `S(10 Hz) = 2 sigma^2 / f_clk` holds on our
   side with sigma^2 the exact periodic covariance at the sample instant.
 * What changes with amplitude is sigma^2 itself: 1.5 % under kT/C at 0.75
   (PSP's channel noise is not exactly 4kT g) and 2.2x kT/C at 0.10, still
   moving -1.7 % between N = 200 and 400 there.

So the disagreement with the reference is not a fold error on this side. It is
a difference in the HELD VARIANCE the two sides compute for a white source as
the clock closes -- the device model's noise in the bias region a weak clock
visits, or the reference's own integration (theirs converges first order from
below). The next measurement names the bias region (below).

### 2026-09-20 — The white disagreement tracks the switch's CONDUCTION at the sample instant: at low clock amplitude the "hold" sample is taken on a conducting, slewing node

The state of the switch at the 5 us sample, from the orbit and the element itself
(flicker off, N = 200):

    ck amp   Vck@5us   Vgs       Vds        |Id|       Sid(10 Hz)     held var / (kT/C)   peer: ours/ref - 1 (converged)
    0.75     0.000 V   -0.297 V  -0.198 V   4.3e-09 A  1.32e-27       0.985               ~5e-05
    0.30     0.450 V   +0.153 V  -0.190 V   1.4e-07 A  4.31e-26       0.928               ~1e-04
    0.20     0.550 V   +0.253 V  -0.179 V   2.9e-06 A  7.87e-25       1.124               ~2e-03
    0.10     0.650 V   +0.353 V  -0.109 V   2.1e-05 A  3.75e-24       2.264               7.7e-03

At 0.10 the clock never falls below 0.65 V: the device conducts through the whole
cycle (21 uA at the "hold" instant), the output is still 0.1 V from the input
and slewing, and the held variance is 2.26 kT/C -- the non-equilibrium channel
noise of a weakly-on device at Vds != 0, sampled on a moving node. The fixture is
a leaky follower there, not a track-and-hold. The disagreement rises with |Id| at
the sample instant across four decades; our own fold is self-consistent to 1e-05
at every one of these points (step 1 above).

So the 0.8 % is in the DEVICE NOISE MODEL in weak/moderate inversion at
Vds != 0, or in how the two integrators sample a slewing node -- not in a fold.
Handed to the peer as two DC-level asks: the reference's noise density at

included any bias this weak. ⚠ NOT a pycircuit defect found; a finding about the

### 2026-09-20 — Gear's adjoint modes made second order on a uniform grid (Andreas: "Build it")

**Where the first order was.** `collect` hands back both `ts[k]` -- the solve
`Jf_k^-T w1` made while replaying step k backwards -- and `states[k]`, the pair
`(w1; w2)` it leaves behind. `floquet_modes` took the pair's first block and
mapped it through pinv(C^T). Since `w1 = Jf^T t = (a0 C + G)^T t` and the adjoint
equation `C^T dq/dt = G^T q` turns the `G^T t` part into a time derivative, that
`q` was `a0 q(t + 2h/3)`: staggered by a fraction of a step. The solve `t` obeys
the BDF2-discretised adjoint recursion; the replay computes it for step k from
the pair at node k + 1, so `ts[k]` is the adjoint at node k + 1.

**Decided by measurement, not algebra.** Invariant `q^T C p` spread at
N = 200 / 400 / 800, uniform grid, four reconstructions:

    pair block, pinv(C^T)   (before)         9.9e-03  5.0e-03  2.5e-03   x2 per doubling
    ts[k] at node k         (one node off)   1.5e-02  7.6e-03  3.8e-03   x2
    ts[k] at node k + 1     (built)          9.3e-04  2.3e-04  5.6e-05   x4  SECOND ORDER
    midpoint interpolation                   2.3e-02  1.2e-02  5.7e-03   x2

`q^T p` unweighted is no invariant at all (spread 2.3 for every candidate): the
C-weighting the block's own note insists on is right. Rolling by a WHOLE sample
was worse (+2.7 %) because the true offset is a fraction of a step -- which is
why that earlier probe pointed away from the answer.

**The scale, and where second order ends.** `t = Jf^-T w1` carries `a0 ~ 1/h`,
invisible on a uniform grid (absorbed by `c0`) and a factor-3 modulation on the
3:1 grid, where every unscaled candidate read a spread of ~1.0. Four scalings
measured there -- `a0` of the node's own step, `a0` of the previous, a mean
reciprocal step, `1/h_j` -- ALL halve per doubling; the first is the best at 2x
the old accuracy (2.2e-2 / 1.1e-2 / 5.9e-3 against 3.4e-2 / 1.6e-2 / 8.0e-3) and
is what is built. That is the known limit: a variable-step multistep discrete
adjoint draws `a1`, `a2` from later steps, so it is the continuous adjoint's
only to O(h) once the step changes, and no per-node rescaling can lift it. A
non-uniform gear grid is refused by the modal spectra anyway; radau is exact.

**Built:** `q_j = a0_j ts[j-1] exp(mu t_j)`, node 0 from node N by periodicity,
for `fp.kind == 'solved_history'` only. ⚠ The first cut applied it to every kind
and CRASHED trap and radau: a one-step kind's `ts` is NESTED (per-stage solves per
step, as the replay's own comment says), and their state-block adjoint through
pinv(C^T) was already measured exact (radau) and second order (trap). They keep
their path.

**Gates.** Invariant: quartering on the uniform grid (ratios 4.09 / 4.05 and
3.94 / 3.98), halving at 2x on the 3:1 grid, as predicted. Parts: gear vs trap
on the uniform grid at N = 400, phase +4e-4 / orbital +9e-4 / correlation +5e-4;
gear's total closes on pnoise at 1.0004 / 1.0005 where it read 1.0145 / 1.0057.
⚠ One gate I had set measures something else: a mode's k = 1 Fourier
coefficient against a uniform N = 3200 reference stays at 3e-2 (N = 200) for
`p` AND `q` alike -- the fix never touched `p`, so this is the mode's PHASE
across N, an artefact of the `|U - U_ref|` metric, not the adjoint. `q`'s DC
coefficient improved 20x on the same metric. Recorded, not chased.

The test from 6c9625f pinned gear's drift HALVING; it now pins the quartering
(3.3 < ratio < 4.8) and its docstring is rewritten.

### 2026-09-20 — A separately-discretised continuous adjoint for gear on a NON-UNIFORM grid: prototype (Andreas: "Do it")

Decided first: the path is gated on STRUCTURE, not order -- `fp.kind ==
'solved_history'` AND a non-uniform grid (the `_period_quadrature` test). A
one-step method's transpose is already a consistent adjoint of its own order on
any grid (trap second order, radau exact, measured), and on a uniform grid the
separate scheme reduces to the transpose (`a0' = a0`), so using it there would
give up the exact-transpose identity for nothing. Order is a property to MATCH
(every ingredient must reach the forward integrator's), not to switch on.

The scheme: `C^T dq/dt = G^T q` integrated backwards with BDF2 whose coefficients
belong to the REVERSE grid's own step pair, `companion_coefficients(h_{n+1},
h_{n+2})`: `(a0' C_n + G_n)^T q_n = -C_n^T (a1' q_{n+1} + a2' q_{n+2})`. The
state is the pair (q_{n+1}, q_{n+2}); the backward map over one period is built
from 2m basis propagations; each forward mode is matched to its eigenvector by
nearest multiplier; periodic part and `c0` normalisation as in `floquet_modes`.
`G` at the nodes from `_G_at`, `C` from `_C_at`; one dense factorisation per node.

PROTOTYPE, 3:1 grid, gear, predicted before running and measured:

    N     invariant spread, transpose q      continuous q         |lam_adj - lam|
    200      2.2e-02  2.7e-02                1.81e-03  1.72e-03      1.2e-03
    400      1.1e-02  1.3e-02                4.57e-04  4.54e-04      3.1e-04
    800      5.9e-03  6.2e-03                1.14e-04  1.16e-04      7.7e-05

Quartering (3.96, 4.0): second order on the grid where the transpose halves;
the matched multipliers agree to O(h^2). The adjoint's own gate holds.

⚠ THE SECOND GATE EXPOSED A LATENT DEFECT INSTEAD: `orbital_correlation`'s R on
the gear 3:1 grid read 146 / 602 / 2449 x radau's (growing as N^2) for BOTH q's.
Not the adjoint: the routine classifies a mode as orbital when its multiplier
is more than 1e-6 off the unit circle, and on that grid gear's PHASE mode sits
at 1 - 5e-5, so it is swept into the orbital sum with a near-zero exponent and
blows up as 1/|mu|^2. `modal_spectrum` refuses exactly this; `orbital_correlation`
returns garbage silently. To be refused the same way. The R gate is re-run with
the phase mode excluded (radau's side excludes it automatically).

The R gate re-run with the phase mode excluded (the orbital mode alone, gear on
the 3:1 grid, against radau there; radau's phase mode is excluded by the window
automatically):

    N     transpose q          continuous q
    200     2.81e-02             2.28e-02
    400     1.52e-03  (/18)      7.20e-03  (/3.2)
    800     1.97e-03  (x0.78)    1.97e-03  (/3.7)

The continuous q converges monotonically at ~second order onto a floor of 2e-3
that BOTH share at N = 800; the transpose q dips below that floor at 400 and
comes back -- a first-order term cancelling against something else, the shape
that fooled the hostile gate. The shared floor is most plausibly p's own
3:1-grid error (both use the same p; eq (22) squares it), INFERRED from the
earlier p ladder, not measured separately here. For scale: radau 3:1 vs gear
uniform at N = 400 differ by 2.6e-4.

BUILT, after the uniform-grid build was committed on its own (5129485):
`PSS._continuous_adjoint` (dense 2m x 2m backward map, ceiling 200 unknowns,
docstring carries the two-adjoints split and the numbers), taken by
`floquet_modes` only for `fp.kind == 'solved_history'` on a non-uniform grid;
`orbital_correlation` refuses when no multiplier lies on the unit circle, naming
radau. One test: gear's invariant quartering on the 3:1 grid, the refusals for
gear and trap there, radau running. New test + the 27 mode-dependent tests pass;
full gate running.

### 2026-09-20 — The first-order Lyapunov route: the ladder names the PPV's sampled phase vector, and clears the injection (Andreas: "Add the HANDOVER row and run the ladder")

The shipped recursion rebuilt from `_lyapunov_pieces` (K1 over one period, the
bounded periodic start from a pseudo-inverse of `I - M kron M` in the full pair
space, the transverse cycle mean) reproduces the shipped `orbital_samples` at
N = 400 to 1.8e-7. ⚠ My first harness diverged to 1e145: projecting the phase
direction out of the STATE block alone leaves a two-step method's history block
its secular growth; and the state-space vector comparable to `ppv`'s `v` is
`C^T q`, not `q` (`v^T u = 0` gave inf). Both are in memory. Then one ingredient
at a time, each variant against its own N = 3200 value:

    N     base        T: trapezoid injection    P: project with C^T q (2nd-order phase mode)
    200   8.0e-02     8.0e-02                   4.6e-04
    400   4.0e-02     4.0e-02                   5.3e-04
    800   1.7e-02     1.7e-02                   1.8e-04
    cross at 3200:    T vs base 4.7e-06         P vs base 5.9e-03

Candidate (a), the injection placed at the step's end node, is REFUTED: moving
half of it before the map changes nothing to 5e-6. Candidate (b) is the cause:
with the second-order phase mode's `C^T q` in the oblique projection instead of
`ppv`'s sampled `v`, the error falls 150x at N = 200, and the two projections
still differ by 5.9e-3 at N = 3200 -- the size of a first-order `ppv` error
extrapolated there. The Lyapunov propagation (A_j, Q_j) is fine. So the
first-order ingredient is `ppv()`'s sampled phase vector, and its own
reconstruction is the next thing to ladder, before `ppv` is touched: it is a
shipped, heavily gated quantity (v . xdot = 1, the waveform gate at 4.2e-3, the
Lorentzian scale) and its consumers are many.

### 2026-09-20 — ⚠⚠ WITHDRAWN: "the Lyapunov route is first order" (5129485) and "ppv's sampled phase vector is first order" (055c1e0). It was a ONE-NODE SHIFT in the consumers' phase-vector list

The chain, in order, because the record should show how a wrong conclusion was
built and how it was caught:
 1. The hostile three-way gate compared eq (22) with a Lyapunov cycle-mean at one
    grid; with the second-order modes it read 4.66e-2. Self-convergence said the
    Lyapunov route was first order. TRUE OF THE NUMBER, wrong about the cause.
 2. The ingredient ladder cleared the injection (trapezoid = base to 5e-6) and
    found that projecting with the mode's `C^T q` instead of `ppv`'s `v` cut the
    error 150x. I read that as "ppv's samples are first order".
 3. I patched `_ppv_propagate` to make its samples the mode's `C^T q`. The
    post-fix ladder DID NOT MOVE (8.014e-2 to four digits) although a probe
    showed the new samples equal to `C^T q` at every node (cos 1.000000) and flat
    against the exact orbit derivative to 6e-5. Both cannot hold unless the
    ladder's phase-vector list is wrong -- and it was: `vs = [v0] + samples`,
    copied from the shipped test's route C, PREPENDS THE ANCHOR and pairs node
    j's covariance with the phase vector of node j - 1. A one-node shift is a
    first-order error in the step. The same prepend sat in three tests and in
    the shipped `oscillator_edge_jitter`.
 4. Unshifted, the transverse cycle mean against its own N = 3200 value reads
    3.3e-4 / 5.1e-4 / 1.8e-4 with the OLD ppv samples and 4.6e-4 / 5.3e-4 /
    1.8e-4 with the patched ones: the old `ppv` was fine all along, and the patch
    bought nothing while costing a gate (the unit-C `v . xdot` std 1.7e-3 against
    a bound of 1e-3 calibrated on the shipped construction). REVERTED.
 5. The hostile gate with its list unshifted: relAC = 3.07e-3 / 7.90e-4 / 2.12e-4
    at N = 200 / 400 / 800, ratios 3.9 / 3.7 -- the two routes agree at SECOND
    order. The gate now pins that (relAC(400) < 2e-3 and quartering from 200),
    the opposite of the halving I pinned in 5129485.

What stands from 5129485: gear's adjoint modes WERE first order and are second
order now (the invariant, the modal parts vs trap, eq (22)'s self-convergence
all measured directly on the modes); and the hostile gate did fail for a
reference-side reason -- the reference's LIST, not its route. What is
withdrawn: that `oscillator_covariance`'s orbital samples are first order (the
docstring note is removed), and that `ppv`'s samples are.

⚠ RULE, earned twice today: when a fix makes NO difference to the instrument
that motivated it, suspect the instrument's indexing before the fix. And a
cross-route agreement that improves 150x when one vector is swapped for another
"equal to cos 1.000000" is a shift, not an accuracy difference.

### 2026-09-20 — ⚠ WITHDRAWN: the fixed sample-instant offset (c0881f0). It was a level difference divided by a slope

The peer ran the test I asked for and then the check that killed it. Sampling at
exact grid instants, the ratio to the reference AT 5 us was linear in the offset
(R^2 > 0.9998 over +-11 ns) and crossed 1 at -3.5 / -3.8 / -4.0 / -4.3 / -7.6 ns
for clock amplitude 0.30 .. 0.10 -- my magnitudes (2.9 .. 8.4), the opposite
sign -- and they nearly sent "structure confirmed, sign inverted". Then, with the
reference's OWN curve against the instant, the same-instant ratio:

    offset (ns)   -11.25    -6.25     -1.25     +3.75     +8.75     spread
    amp 0.25      1.000909  1.000959  1.000835  1.000949  1.001028  1.9e-04
    amp 0.15      1.005588  1.005812  1.005057  1.005591  1.005956  9.0e-04

FLAT across 20 ns, the two curves' slopes with the instant agreeing to 2-3 %. A
timing offset would swing that ratio across the span. It is a LEVEL difference,
9.4e-04 and 5.6e-03 -- the converged numbers.

⚠ WHY IT LOOKED SO GOOD. Both sides ratioed a MOVING curve against the reference
at ONE FIXED instant; a constant level difference divided by d ln(var)/dt has the
dimensions of a time and always produces one. My "implied offsets" and their
"measured crossings" agreed because they were the same quantity computed twice.
The drift of the "common" offset with amplitude (2.9 -> 8.4 ns) was the tell, and
I read it as curvature. Also ruled out by them: a step convention (the apparent
offset converges, -1.7 / -3.1 / -3.8 ns at npts 1000 / 2000 / 4000, it does not
scale with dt) and an orbit phase difference (< 0.05 ns, no RMS gain).

WHAT STANDS: a near-constant FACTOR at the sample instant, growing as the clock
closes (~5e-5 at 0.75, 9.1e-4 at 0.25, 5.5e-3 at 0.15, 7.7e-3 at 0.10),
converged in our grid, invariant to both sideband counts, not the DC densities
at the sample-instant biases (3e-4 .. 9e-4, wrong sign), not timing, not orbit
phase. On our side the sample series is white to 1e-5 and the fold equals the
time-domain covariance, so if the factor is flat in frequency it is the held
VARIANCE; that integrates the channel noise over the whole conduction phase,
where |Vds| reaches 0.25 .. 0.67 V at amplitude 0.10 -- a bias region their three
DC points (|Vds| <= 0.19 V) did not cover. Asked of them: the same-instant ratio
at 1 kHz (flat in frequency or not), and the DC densities along the conduction
trajectory at the larger |Vds|. No candidate is claimed.

RULE: a timing hypothesis is tested by comparing both sides at the SAME
instant; a level difference divided by a slope is a time by dimensions alone.

**Item 3, BUILT** (after items 1 and 2 landed as 8c9a5a9): `_continuous_adjoint`
forms the dense 2m x 2m backward map only up to `CONTINUOUS_ADJOINT_DENSE_M = 8`
unknowns; above that it runs Arnoldi on the map as an operator (one backward
propagation per matvec, the per-node factorisations taken once with
`lu_factor`), grown from `PPV_RITZ_BASIS` toward `PPV_RITZ_MAX_BASIS` until the
Ritz pair matched to the forward multiplier certifies at `PPV_RITZ_RESIDUAL_TOL`,
and refuses by name if it never does. The old 200-unknown refusal is gone. Test:
the Arnoldi path forced onto the hostile fixture (the switch set to 0) equals
the dense path's q to 1e-6 in norm and cos 1 - 1e-8 at four nodes; the
prototype's table (basis 3 / 16 / 24 for 2m = 4 / 32 / 124, cos 1.00000000) is
the measurement behind it.

### 2026-09-20 — Gear as a first-class choice, item 1 (Andreas: "Do 1"): events. No restart was needed; the defect was the grid's, and shared by every method

**The question** was whether a multistep method loses ORDER across a landed
source edge (its history straddles the edge), which would want a one-step
restart there. Measured before anything was built: pulsed RC (R 1k, C 300p,
T 1 us, td 0.0125 T, pw 0.4 T), analytic periodic solution, max error over the
ACTUAL grid nodes, N = 50 .. 1600, gear / trap / radau, `break_events` off/on,
edges RAMPED (tr = 0.02 T) and a TRUE JUMP (tr = 0, which `Pulse` clamps to a
1e-18 ramp).

**Ramped edges: no order lost.** gear + events 3.16e-3 / 5.76e-3 / 3.15e-4 /
2.44e-4 / 6.31e-5 / 1.61e-5 (3.88x, 3.92x per doubling from 400), ahead of
gear-uniform at 5 of 6 N. The gain is 1.5x where trap's is 22x: BDF2 takes an
O(h^2 [x'']) hit at the ONE step after each corner (5.5e-7 -> 1.7e-4 across it,
30x trap's) -- a constant, not an order. A trap restart at corners would remove
it but puts a b != 0 step into every `solved_history` consumer; NOT built,
recorded as gear's honest cost on hard corners.

**True jump: every method first order, and it was the grid.** With edges
landed: gear 1.30e-3, trap 9.5e-4, radau 3.0e-4 at 1600, all halving per
doubling, the error injected AT the edge node and decaying over tau. The
diagnostic printed `u(node) = 1.000` at the edge: `event_grid`'s snap landed
the ramp's first event, then OVERWROTE that node with the second (1e-18 later),
so the edge sat on the post-jump side and the arriving step integrated its
whole length with the post-jump source -- each method's endpoint weight times
h dU/tau (radau 0.111 x 8.3e-3 = 9.3e-4 predicted, 8.5e-4 measured). **Fix: a
snap may move a grid point onto an event, never an event onto an event.** With
both ramp ends kept (a 1e-18 step): radau 5e-8 .. 5e-7 (exact to the ramp
node's time rounding), trap 2.5e-5 -> 2.3e-6, **gear 2.3e-3 -> 3.4e-6, second
order, with no restart**: variable-step BDF2 with h_n/h_{n-1} -> inf over a
consistent tiny step degenerates to the trapezoidal rule (the factor w/2
multiplies a difference that is O(1/w)). My prediction of "garbage or a
refusal" for gear on that grid was WRONG, and the measurement is what says so.

**Consequences built:** (1) `_resolve_break_events` returns True for every
method; the `companion_reach() == 1` predicate and the one-N table behind it
are gone (the table's jittered control was sound -- non-uniformity does cost a
multistep method -- the conclusion drawn from one N was not). (2) `event_grid`
keeps a `landed` set. (3) `_period_grid`'s zero-stability warning counts only
REPEATED up-steps (`PSS.RATIO_ISOLATION = 4`): an isolated ratio of any size is
harmless (2.5e9 and 20 measured); the 60 %-low alternating 3:1 grid repeats
every other step. Its text said the steps "are dropped to Euler by the
integrator" -- false for PSS, that is `Transient.check_order_drop`. (4) The
older `test_event_grid_lands_the_period_on_its_event_times` passed no
`break_events` to its "uniform" baseline, which under the new default became
the event grid, identical to the last digit -- pinned to False.

**Instrument lesson:** the first reference gave a FLAT 9.5e-5 for all three
methods on the two-node ramp -- `a - b tau` with b = 1/tr = 1e18 cancelling at
1e-16 x 3e11. A flat error across a ladder and across methods is the
instrument. `expm1` fixed it.

Open for gear (unchanged): 2 free-period on non-uniform grids, 3 index-2, 4 the
`method` docstring's measured row.

### 2026-09-20 — Gear as a first-class choice, item 2 (Andreas: "Do 2"): the free period on a non-uniform grid. Gear needed nothing; the ladder found the DEFAULT method's period column wrong wherever a circuit has a DC source

**Gear, measured** (van der Pol mu = 1, reference radau N = 3200, grid-independent
to 1e-10 ppm; and the phase fixture, exact period 1e-3): second order on the
uniform, the smooth (1 + 0.5 sin) and the alternating 2:1 grid, with the 2:1
constant equal to the uniform one (+4.86 vs +4.87 ppm at 1600); FIRST order on
the alternating 3:1 grid (-1615 / -841 / -429 / -216 ppm, ratios 1.84 .. 1.98),
which is beyond BDF2's zero-stability bound 1 + sqrt(2), and where `_period_grid`
warns (repeated up-steps). ⚠ The phase fixture is second order on 3:1 too (its
constant even SMALLER than uniform's, -2.27 vs +5.15 ppm): a linear rotation
does not compound the parasitic mode; the nonlinearity does. Pinned in
`test_gears_free_period_is_second_order_on_a_non_uniform_grid_inside_the_zero_stability_bound`.

**Radau FAILED the free-period solve on the tree's own phase fixture at every N
and every tolerance** ("the free-period Jacobian went singular ... seed below the
fundamental"), and the suite never saw it because its radau free-period test
seeds a source-free van der Pol. Refuted on the way: the seed (trap's converged
orbit fails the same way; the origin NaNs). Found by a spy on the 'full'
traversal: radau's period column at trap's orbit was `[-1e6, ~0, -1.8e-4, ...]`
against the finite difference `[0, -3.18, 6.28e3, ...]` (2 pi / T on the
rotating outputs, 0 on the node a 1 kV supply pins); the first Newton step on it
threw `x0` to 2.8e6, where the coupled stage matrix went singular -- the
wrapper labels ANY LinAlgError inside fsolve as the free-period Jacobian's.
Van der Pol + a decoupled 1 kV node isolated it to the SOURCE ROWS: oscillator
rows right to 4 digits, supply node -157.9 = -1e3/T, branch current +i_R/T.
**Cause:** `_traverse_full` and `_traverse_dirk` built the stage derivative as
`Ks = -i(Y)` -- `_i_at`'s docstring said an autonomous circuit has "no source
term". It has a CONSTANT one. On a source-pinned row `i = -u` at convergence,
so `-i = u` and the column reads `-u/T`. Both FD checks in the build were on a
source-free circuit, where the two coincide. **Fix:** `_k_at` = `-(i(x) + u)`,
`u` at t = 0 (constant for the autonomous circuits this is built for), at both
sites. Verified: max |Mt - FD| / max |FD| = 4.6e-11 (radau) / 7.9e-11 (trbdf2)
on the phase fixture, 1.2e-10 on vdp + supply; radau now finds the phase
fixture's period at order 5 (+1.52e-6 / +2.36e-8 / +6.5e-10 ppm at N = 100 /
200 / 400), trbdf2 at order 2; radau on vdp + supply converges to the
source-free reference period to 1e-9. The wrapper's message now says a stage
matrix inside the traversal can be the singular one.

**Lessons:** an FD check on a fixture where the wrong and right expressions
coincide is vacuous -- put a source in the autonomous fixture; a solver that
"went singular" after a NEWTON STEP was pushed somewhere by the Jacobian, so
check the Jacobian's columns against FD at the seed before reading the message.
Also: my Monitor pattern waited 30 min on a ladder stuck in radau's failing
rows at N = 1600 -- put failing rows LAST or cap their cost.

Open for gear (unchanged): 3 index-2, 4 the `method` docstring's measured row.

### 2026-09-20 — Gear as a first-class choice, item 3 (Andreas: "do 3 and then 4"): index 2. The forward solve, the period and the modes needed nothing; the PPV samples and PAC's period weights were both first order on a non-uniform grid — and the one-step kinds never see a non-uniform grid at all

**Forward (driven C-V loop, analytic reference, the tree's own subspace-split
instrument):** gear order 2 in BOTH subspaces on the uniform grid (2.01 / 2.01)
and on the smooth one (2.00 / 2.00) -- BDF2 has no index-2 order reduction;
trap 2 / 2; radau 5 / 3 as pinned.

**Autonomous index-2 fixture** (van der Pol with a DC source INSIDE a capacitor
loop: C(v-gnd), VS(v-a), C(a-gnd) -- index 2 by the projector criterion, and it
exercises the constant-source period column of 15579a9): gear's period second
order on uniform and smooth grids (4.0x), its adjoint-mode invariant quartering
on both (uniform 3.1e-4 / 7.5e-5 / 1.9e-5 / 4.8e-6; smooth 4.3e-4 / 1.05e-4 /
2.9e-5 / 7.5e-6) -- and its diffusion constant `c` against radau N = 3200:
uniform +1.2e-3 / +3.3e-4 / +8.7e-5 / +2.2e-5 (second order), **smooth -5.0e-3 /
-2.4e-3 / -1.2e-3 / -5.8e-4 (FIRST order)**, trap on the same smooth grid second
order.  The `_ppv_propagate` algebraic warning fires on every run (its own text
priced the fallback "second order on both" -- on uniform grids).

**Two stacked causes, separated by instrument:**
1. The PPV SAMPLES.  `_ppv_propagate`'s index-2 fallback keeps only the
   differential block of the pair-consistent correction (`G[D,NZ]`; the Schur
   complement does not exist there), dropping a coupling that is a DERIVATIVE
   term at index 2; it cancels between steps on a uniform grid and not on a
   non-uniform one.  Prototype: `c` from the continuous adjoint's phase mode as
   the state-space PPV `v_j = C_j^T q_j` (the continuous `q` IS Demir's
   equation-row `v_1`; `samples` carry `C^T v_1`), normalised `q_0^T C_0 xdot_0
   = 1`, integrated with a periodic trapezoid over the actual grid: -1.9e-4 /
   -2.2e-5 / +7e-8 / +8e-7 where the current samples gave -1.0e-3 / -4.4e-4 /
   -2.1e-4 / -1.1e-4 under the SAME integral.  Built in `ppv()` under the
   fallback's own condition (flag `_ppv_alg_fallback`, solved-history kind,
   non-uniform grid).  ⚠ `q` put in bare lands 16.7x off with the right shape
   -- the space matters, the shape does not tell you.
2. PAC's PERIOD WEIGHTS.  With the fixed samples PAC's `c` still read -1.9e-3 /
   -9.3e-4 / -4.6e-4: `_white_diffusion_at`, `colour_projection`,
   `coloured_diffusion_resolved` and `_cy_cycle_averaged` all weighted samples
   with `diff(times)` -- the LEFT RECTANGLE rule, which is the trapezoid rule on
   a uniform periodic grid and FIRST order on a non-uniform one by itself (error
   (1/2) integral h' y dt).  `PAC._period_weights` (periodic trapezoid) at all
   four sites; from the same samples +4.0e-5 / +3.6e-5 / +1.3e-5.  Uniform grids
   are bit-identical (0.5h + 0.5h == h).

**Found on the way, recorded, not changed:** `factored_period_full` and
`_dirk` (and the TR-BDF2 twin) replay the converged orbit on a uniform
`linspace` grid whatever grid the solve used -- radau's and trap's
`info['times']` on the "smooth grid" run are uniform (h = T/200, 1.1 s off the
waveform's nodes).  Their PPV, modes and noise surfaces on a non-uniform grid
are uniform-grid replays: accurate, and the reason radau read as "exact on the
3:1 grid" -- its adjoint machinery never saw that grid.  Every earlier "radau
exact on the non-uniform grid" statement is true of a uniform replay.  Only
the solved-history kind runs its adjoint on the caller's grid, which is why
only gear paid the two defects above -- and why gear is now the only method
whose oscillator surfaces are actually on a non-uniform grid.

**Instruments rejected on the way (the record):** cross-method comparison of
the PPV WAVEFORM on the index-2 fixture is not one -- the two kinds' sample
waveforms are related by no shift or sign (best 37 %) while their maxima and
`c` agree, because one kind's samples sit on a uniform replay; a noise VOLTAGE
inside the C-V loop gives `c = 0` exactly for every method (a voltage
perturbation of an index-2 constraint is a differentiated input the PPV
projection cannot represent -- an index-2 noise-source question for another
day); and the "not resolved at this accuracy, 2.3e6x tolerance" LTE warning on
this fixture was the shooting reltol 1e-10 I asked for against a grid
delivering 1e-4 -- a correct report.

Tests: `test_gears_ppv_samples_are_second_order_on_a_non_uniform_index2_grid`
(the fallback must fire; smooth-grid `c` within 1e-4 / 8e-5 of radau at 200 /
400, was 2.4e-3 / 1.2e-3; the uniform grid unchanged).  Item 4 next.

### 2026-09-20 — Honour the caller's grid (Andreas: "start the honour the caller's grid"): the one-step kinds' factored periods now replay on the grid the solve was on; and the uniform replay was a LIVE defect on the default method since 7735444

**What was true:** `factored_period()` passed `len(times) - 1` and
`factored_period_full` / `_dirk` / `_glm` built `linspace(0, T, npts + 1)`, so
the PPV, the Floquet modes, every noise fold, the harmonic readers and the
forced replays of a radau / trbdf2 / esdirk43 run -- and of trap and euler
through the TR-BDF2 twin -- on a non-uniform grid were computed on a uniform
replay from the converged `x0`.  Only the solved-history (gear) kind replayed
on the caller's grid.  Every "radau is exact on the 3:1 grid" statement in the
tree describes a uniform replay.

**Why it mattered before it was cosmetic:** `break_events` is ON for every
method since 7735444, so radau's SOLVE lands a pulse edge (nodes 55 / 105 / 205
/ 403 / 803 for N = 50 .. 800) while its replay did not -- and `carrier_phasor`
takes the solve's waveform with weights from `_period_quadrature(fp)`, which
returned None on the uniform replay: a plain MEAN over non-uniform nodes.
Pulsed RC, tr = 0, radau, first harmonic of v_C against the analytic periodic
solution: **1.04e-1 / 5.43e-2 / 2.77e-2 / 7.05e-3 / 3.53e-3 (first order)**;
with the replay on the solved grid **1.54e-3 / 3.77e-4 / 9.39e-5 / 2.33e-5 /
5.68e-6 (second order, 600x at 800)**.  A user-facing wrong answer on the
default method for every driven circuit with a source edge.

**Built:** the three builders take `grid=` (fractions; a bare `npts` is
uniform as before, so the direct-call tests are unchanged); `_replay_grid`
scales the fractions to `T` (an autonomous period is solved, the fractions are
of the period); `factored_period()` hands the solved fractions down only when
the grid is not uniform (`UNIFORM_GRID_TOL`), so uniform solves are
bit-identical.  The twin already solves with `grid=` and so flows through.

**Attempted first and abandoned:** a relaxation van der Pol (mu = 10 / 20) on
its own `lte_grid` -- radau's coupled stage Newton and trbdf2's outer Newton
both refused the 176:1 / 619:1 grids in the FORWARD solve (the benchmark that
solves mu = 100 on such a grid hand-rolls its shooting loop at tstab 1200); the
event fixture answered the question with an analytic reference instead.

Test: `test_one_step_factored_periods_replay_on_the_grid_the_solve_was_on`.
Consequence to re-measure when convenient: radau's PPV / modes / noise on
genuinely non-uniform grids now see those grids -- the earlier "grid-independent
to 1e-10" readings were of uniform replays.

**Two pins moved, and both were measuring a uniform replay against itself.**
`test_floquet_modes_under_gear_are_second_order_on_a_uniform_grid_and_radau_is_exact_on_a_non_uniform_one`
had radau's modal parts on the 3:1 grid equal to the uniform grid's at rtol 1e-8

### 2026-09-21 — (c) The one-step kinds' oscillator surfaces on grids they now actually see (Andreas: "Do C now"): radau is order five on alternating grids and the period QUADRATURE caps every method at second order on a smoothly varying one

Van der Pol (mu = 1) and its index-2 twin, radau / trbdf2 / trap(twin), grids
uniform / smooth (1 + 0.5 sin) / 2:1 / 3:1 alternating, N = 100 .. 800, against
radau N = 3200 uniform; every replay confirmed on the solved grid.

**Radau:** period order 5 on every grid (ratios ~33); `c` order 5 on the
alternating grids (3:1: -5.2e-9 / -1.6e-10 / -1.1e-11 / +6.3e-12; index-2 3:1:
-2.8e-9 .. +7.6e-14), phase multiplier at 1 to 2e-11, mode invariant 1e-15.  So
the alternating-grid claims survive on a genuine replay, at 1e-10 instead of
the uniform replay's 1e-12.  **On the smooth grid `c` is SECOND order** (+4.9e-5
/ 1.3e-5 / 3.3e-6 / 8.4e-7, ratios 3.9; index-2 the same) while the period,
the multipliers and the invariant stay at order 5 / 1e-11 / 1e-15 -- the
periodic trapezoid is spectrally accurate on uniform and alternating grids
(two interleaved uniform sums) and genuinely O(h^2) on a smoothly varying one.
`_period_quadrature`'s docstring already said "second order caps radau"; this
is that cap measured where it bites.  A higher-order non-uniform quadrature
would lift it for every method; recorded, not built.

**Trbdf2, and trap through its twin:** second order on every grid, no penalty
for alternation (3:1 c: 4.5e-5 / 1.1e-5 / 2.6e-6 / 6.5e-7 vs uniform 2.6e-5 ..
3.7e-7); phase multiplier leaves 1 at O(h^2) on the smooth grid only (1.1e-4
-> 1.7e-6, warned, as `_phase_mode_split` states) and stays at 1e-11 on the
alternating ones.  trap's c and multipliers equal its twin's, now on the
caller's grid.

Test: `test_radaus_oscillator_surfaces_on_a_genuinely_non_uniform_grid_are_order_five_except_where_the_period_quadrature_caps_them`
(3:1 at the reference floor, smooth at the quadrature's second order).
Open: the higher-order period quadrature; the sampled-noise tail closure (the
peer's 3.0 x (2/pi) fc/F, coefficient to be derived from the fold kernel); a
noise source on an index-2 constraint returning c = 0 silently.

### 2026-09-21 — The higher-order period quadrature (Andreas: "Do as you recommend"): a periodic cubic-spline rule on non-uniform EVENT-FREE grids lifts the second-order cap for every method; landed edges keep the trapezoid; uniform grids untouched

**Why:** (c) measured the periodic trapezoid capping every method's `c` at
second order on a smoothly varying grid (radau +4.9e-5 / 1.3e-5 / 3.3e-6 /
8.4e-7 at N = 100 .. 800 while its period, multipliers and invariant sat at
order 5 / 1e-11 / 1e-15) -- the grids `lte_grid` and `refine_grid` produce.
The trapezoid is spectral on uniform and alternating grids (two interleaved
uniform sums) and O(h^2) on a smooth one.

**Built:** `periodic_spline_weights(t, T)` -- the integral of the periodic
cubic spline through the samples as a weight vector, `wt - B^T A^{-T} d` with
the cyclic tridiagonal spline system solved sparse (O(n)).  Prototype, then
shipped: radau's `c` on the 1 + 0.5 sin grid **-5.5e-9 / -2.4e-10 / -7.3e-12 /
+2.6e-12**, the reference's floor from N = 400; `exp(sin)` on the same grid at
n = 64: trapezoid 3.5e-4, spline 1.8e-7; on a uniform grid the weights equal
the trapezoid's to 1.7e-18 (a periodic spline's `sum M_j = 0`).  Wired into
`PSS._period_quadrature` (harmonics, folds, the adjoint inject) and
`PAC._period_weights` (`c`, `colour_projection`, `coloured_diffusion_resolved`,
`_cy_cycle_averaged`, now taking `pss`), and into `solve`'s `fpss` branch,
which had its own inline trapezoid -- the `fpss` = `sqrt(2) carrier_phasor`
identity pinned at 1e-12 caught the split rule at 6.3e-6.  ONE rule, one
vector, every consumer.

**Gates:** (1) EVENT-FREE ONLY -- a spline is C^2 across every node and a
landed source edge puts a kink in the integrand there; a spline through a
kink rings where the trapezoid is exact-ish, so `event_times` non-empty
keeps the trapezoid (pinned on the pulsed-RC event grid: the weights ARE the
trapezoid's).  With `break_events` on by default, every switched circuit is
on the trapezoid, every smooth derived grid on the spline.  (2) Uniform grids
never reach it (`UNIFORM_GRID_TOL`), bit-identical.  (3) `n >= 4`.

Not measured: the spline on an alternating 3:1 grid where the trapezoid was
already spectral (the (c) test still pins that row at the reference floor,
so it is at least as good there); gear's `c` on smooth grids stays at its
samples' second order, as it should.  Tests: the (c) test's smooth row moved
from the cap to the floor; the honour-the-grid test gained the event gate.

### 2026-09-21 — Gear on ADAPTIVE grids (Andreas: "Do as you suggest"): what `lte_grid` hands gear, and how gear does on it

**Grid provenance (item 3, a read):** `lte_grid` runs `Transient(self.cir,
toolkit, reltol=rt)` -- no integrator passed -- and `Transient` defaults to
`Gear2Integrator()`.  So the adaptive grid is GEAR-SHAPED by default: right for
a gear PSS, and a mismatch for a radau / trbdf2 PSS, whose LTE profile on a
relaxation edge differs (a fifth-order method wants far fewer points on the
slow branch).  Not changed here; recorded as the reason a radau user's
`lte_grid` is denser than radau needs.

**The grid gear produced could not carry gear's PPV -- FOUR things in the
chain, found one behind the other, all fixed:** (1) `lte_grid`'s window ended
at the `tend`-landing step `Transient.solve` truncates (a tenth of its
predecessor), a 10.5x GROWTH across the period seam, beyond 1 + sqrt 2: the
integrator dropped two steps to Euler and the solved-history transposed replay
refused two-alpha steps -- `ppv()` raised "derived for a two-step companion"
on a converged gear solve.  The window now ends at the last natural step.
(2) The seam: rotating the cut unconditionally to the flattest seam moved the
truncated step into the interior (which is how (1) was found) and then, on
the tree's own mu = 4 `lte_grid` test, put the seed at a phase from which the
free-period Newton, 8 % off in period, ran away to 19.6 -- the seed's phase is
part of the basin.  The cut now moves only when the seam is outside the bound,
and by the least.  (3) `_period_grid`'s first-step subdivision (the plain
opener's protection) put ONE step of `fr.min()` in front of a coarse first
step: 2.6e-4 before 3.5e-2, a 138x growth, two Euler steps.  Skipping it for
the solved-history kind was tried and the coarse first step then defeated the
per-step Newton (the entering history is flat at the seed), so the protection
is needed: it is now a DOUBLING RAMP `d, 2d, 4d, ...`, every ratio 2, ~8
points.  (4) Transient's `h_curr / h_last < 0.1` drop to Euler -- by its own
comment a stalled-estimate heuristic for adaptive runs, not stability -- fired
on the ramp's first step at the seam (a 0.007x shrink).  A frozen grid has no
estimate to stall: `Gear2Integrator.shrink_guard`, off in PSS-driven
transients; the growth guard stays on everywhere.  After all four: seam inside
the bound, max growth 2.000, no two-alpha step, gear's PPV / modes / `c` run
on gear's own grid.

**The period passed to `lte_grid` must be close.**  My relaxation estimate
(3 - 2 ln 2) mu was 16 % low at mu = 10; the grid is FRACTIONS of the period,
so its fine regions sat 16 % away from the edges and gear's AND trbdf2's
per-step Newton failed on the coarse steps that landed there ("StandardNewton
failed to converge" -- which is also why yesterday's honour-the-grid prototype
gave up on this fixture).  Seeded at the reference period both converge.
Usage guidance recorded in the docstring; `lte_grid` still takes the period as
an input and does not measure it.

**Gear on its adaptive grid, measured** (radau uniform 8N references,
self-checked against 4N: mu = 10 to 5e-3 ppm, mu = 20 to 0.5 ppm)::

    mu   grid                   N      T err (ppm)   c rel       |rho-1|
    10   gear LTE               195    -519          +0.52       1.0e-1   (seed in the fine region)
    10   gear LTE 2x            390    -133          +0.15       2.7e-2
    10   gear LTE (shipped)     203   -1436          ...         1.0e-1   (seed at the window start + ramp; 3.7x per 2x)
    10   gear LTE 4x            780     -34          +0.040
    10   gear uniform           195   -6541          -0.15
    10   gear uniform 4x        780    -491          -0.068
    10   trbdf2 LTE             195     -66          +0.16       1.4e-2
    10   trbdf2 LTE 2x          390     -17          +0.040      3.4e-3
    20   gear LTE               211   +4935          +1.75
    20   gear LTE 2x            422    -243          +1.41
    20   gear LTE 4x            844     +55          +0.39
    20   gear uniform           211   FAILED
    20   gear uniform 4x        844   -1601          -0.33
    20   trbdf2 LTE             211    -378          +0.31
    50   everything at 228 / 912 FAILED; gear LTE 2x (456) -803 ppm, c garbage

At mu = 10 gear's period on the adaptive grid is second order (3.9x per 2x
split) and a 195-point adaptive grid equals a 780-point uniform one -- the
adaptive grid is worth 4x in points for gear.  **Its `c` is 52 % high, and the
cause is the discrete period map's unit multiplier sitting 0.10 off the
circle** -- `ppv()` solves its bordered system at exactly 1 and could not see
it; `c` tracks the departure at 5-12x across the table (gear 1.0e-1 -> 0.52,
2.7e-2 -> 0.15; trbdf2 1.4e-2 -> 0.16, 3.4e-3 -> 0.040).  The spline quadrature
is not it (trapezoid +0.528 vs spline +0.520).  **`ppv()` now warns from
`spectral_radius`** (`PPV_UNIT_MULTIPLIER_WARN` = 1e-2) with the size and the
remedy; before, `converged = True` and silence.  **And trbdf2 uses gear's own
grid 8x better than gear for the period and 3.5x for `c`** -- on a relaxation
oscillator's adaptive grid, trbdf2 is the method; gear's virtue there is
producing the grid.  At mu >= 20 the reltol 1e-5 adaptive grid (211 points over
668:1) is too coarse for any PSS method -- the tree's own mu = 100 benchmark
used reltol 1e-7 and 1105 points -- so the grid tolerance for a PSS on a
relaxation oscillator is a tighter one than the transient's; recorded, not
tuned.

Tests: `test_a_grid_that_opens_coarse_is_subdivided_but_a_benign_one_is_not` re-pinned to the ramp's shape (first step `fr.min()`, ratios <= 2, the coarse step's sum preserved, idempotent); the tree's `lte_grid` test unchanged and passing; new `test_gear_runs_its_ppv_on_the_lte_grid_gear_produced_and_is_told_when_its_unit_multiplier_left_the_circle`
(seam and growth after the fixes, no order-dropped step, gear's period second
order and told, trbdf2 5x better on the same grid and told).  Open from this:
item 2 (the 'closing' period-column convention, B7c) is still the next thing
for the free period on adaptive grids; `lte_grid` measuring its own period.

### 2026-09-21 — Item 2 of gear-on-adaptive-grids: the 'closing' period column on every kind (B7c completed), 'auto' on a caller's grid, and a proportional polish after it

**What B7c left:** `_period_column = 'closing'` was implemented on the plain
`_traverse` path only; gear's solved-history traversals and the stage kinds
used `dh/dT = h/T` unconditionally, so the convention B7c measured 46x closer
was never available to gear, radau or trbdf2.  "Default unchanged pending the
rest of B7c."

**Why it is the adaptive-grid fix:** under 'proportional' the outer Newton
rescales EVERY step with each trial period, so an `lte_grid`'s fine regions
slide off the relaxation edges while T is hunted -- from a seed 16 % low both
gear's and trbdf2's per-step Newton fail on the coarse steps that land there
(the failure that stopped yesterday's honour-the-grid prototype and today's
first ladder).  Under 'closing' the inner steps keep the absolute lengths the
transient validated and only the last step follows T: both converge.

**Built:** `_period_grid` rebuilds a closing grid (inner steps frozen at the
first call of a solve, the last closing to T, a proportional fallback with a
warning if it would collapse); the three solved-history sites, `_traverse_dirk`,
`_traverse_full` and `_glm_propagate` carry the closing column (`dh/dT = 1` on
the last step, 0 elsewhere; the LMM sites use `_dfdh`, which `solve_timestep`
already computed for the plain path); a `period_column` Parameter, 'auto' =
closing for an autonomous run on a caller's grid, proportional otherwise.

**Measured, and the design it forced:** from the 16 %-low seed, closing
converges -- and the closing step then holds the whole 3 s correction, a
growth far beyond the zero-stability bound: gear's step is dropped to Euler
(replay refused), trbdf2's answer is -1.1 % in period and -97 % in `c`.  So
'auto' solves ONCE MORE, proportionally, on the CALLER'S fractions at the
solved period from the converged state, when the closing step ended outside
the bound.  ⚠ My first second pass re-fractioned the closing-distorted grid
and kept the giant step (still -1.1 % / -97 %); the polish must be on the
caller's fractions.  With that: gear -1464 ppm / c -8.6 %, trbdf2 -195 ppm /
c +2.0 % from the bad seed -- the reference-seeded answers (-1436 / -195).
At the reference seed closing and proportional agree (-1594 vs -1436; -197 vs
-195); the mu = 1 smooth-grid ladder is bit-identical between conventions;
at mu = 20 trbdf2 under closing gives c +9.6 % against proportional's +230 %
(same seed); mu = 50 stays out of reach for every method on a 1e-5 grid.

Test: `test_the_closing_period_column_takes_a_free_period_solve_from_a_seed_the_proportional_one_cannot`.

**One pinned number moved under 'auto', and it says what a default change
does:** `test_gears_ppv_samples_are_second_order_on_a_non_uniform_index2_grid`
solves an index-2 oscillator on a smooth grid from a seed period 8 % low;
under 'auto' the closing pass and the polish converge to a point 0.7 ppm from
the proportional solve's -- both within tolerance -- and `c` at N = 200 moves
by 1.5e-4 between them (+2.9e-5 vs -1.2e-4), the fixture's own sensitivity.
The test's subject is the samples at a fixed discretisation, so it now pins
`period_column='proportional'`.  Andreas, shown the record above: "Set default to auto" -- the default is
'auto', decided.

### 2026-09-21 — The sampled-noise "tail": the peer's coefficient 3.0 was the GRID, not the kernel; a derived 1/nu^2 closure (coefficient 1) and a resolution warning. And a noise source on an index-2 constraint is now named instead of returning c = 0 silently (Andreas: "Do the sampled-noise tail closure and then the warning")

**What the peer measured** (LTI limit of the switched capacitor: g = 0.5 mS,
C = 100 pF, fc = 795.78 kHz, f0 = 100 kHz): the held variance short of kT/C by
3.0 x (2/pi) fc/F, F = M f0, stable to 3 % over M = 400 .. 1600 at npts = 1000,
and the condition that the 3.0 be DERIVED from the fold's kernel or not built.

**Derivation, then measurement.**  `_sampled_series` sums `|n| <= L`
symmetrically, `nu = f + n f0`, so the covered source spectrum is |nu| <
