(L + 1/2) f0 entirely and the pure tail of a Lorentzian is `1 - (2/pi)
atan(F/fc)` -- coefficient ONE.  No 3 in the kernel.  On the tree's own
fixture at `vck = 0` (the same g, C, fc) with npts = 2M + 4 the coefficient
read 2.53 / 3.02 / 3.38 / 3.64 at M = 50 .. 400 -- not constant -- and at a
FIXED M = 100 with rising points, 3.03 / 1.93 / 1.33 / 1.12 / 1.05 at 204 /
400 / 800 / 1600 / 3200 (omega h at the top sideband 3.1 / 1.6 / 0.79 / 0.39 /
0.20 rad per step), converging to 1 at first order in h.  **The 3.0 is the
discretisation of the covered top sidebands** -- gear's discrete transfer at
omega h ~ 3 is not the continuous one -- held steady by the peer's fixed
M/npts.  A closure built on 3.0 would have been a fitted constant, exactly
what the peer warned against.

**Built:** `tail=True` on `sampled_noise` / `sampled_variance`: per instant
and series frequency, the `1/nu^2` extrapolation of the two OUTERMOST covered
sidebands over their uncovered half-lines -- `A = nu_L^2 dens_L`, `A / (f0 (F
+ f0/2))` per side -- exact for one pole, `(fc/F)^2` in general, nothing
fitted; the per-sideband densities are kept for it (white, power-law and
per-band components alike).  And a one-time warning (`SAMPLED_RESOLUTION_WARN`
= 1 rad per step) when the top sideband sits above `omega h = 1`, naming the
remedy: more points, or fewer sidebands with the tail.  Default `tail=False`:
no result moves unasked.  Measured with the closure: radau at 100 sidebands, 204 points: 0.9479 without (the pure tail, its covered bands accurate even at omega h = 3) -> 0.9981 with; gear at fixed M = 100: 0.847 -> 0.874 / 0.926 / 0.969 / 0.989 / 0.995 at 204 / 400 / 800 / 1600 / 3200 points -- the tail removed, the remainder the discretisation error falling with h.  ⚠ With the covered edge INSIDE the spectrum's corner (L = 6 at 204 points, F = 0.8 fc) the closure reads 0.71: the 1/nu^2 form needs F well above fc, so the warning's remedy is points first, the tail second..

**Recorded for the peer:** their coefficient is (1 + the discretisation
deficit at their omega h), and their reference's 1600-sideband point was
their own undersampling; on both sides the sideband ladder at a fixed grid
mixes two limits.

**The index-2 noise source, named:** a voltage noise in series with the DC
source inside a capacitor loop perturbs an index-2 constraint -- a
differentiated input, a charge jump -- which the PPV projection cannot
represent; `c` came back exactly 0 for every method (item 3, 2026-09-20),
silently.  `_white_diffusion_at` now warns once when the circuit is index >= 2
at the orbit point (`G[A,Z]` singular, computed for every kind -- gating on
the PPV fallback's flag would have covered the solved-history kind only,
which is how the first version stayed silent under radau) and `CY` has power
on an algebraic row; silent for a source on a differential row (3.2e-9 on the
same circuit).

Tests: `test_the_sampled_noise_tail_closure_is_derived_and_the_resolution_limit_is_named` and
`test_a_noise_source_on_an_index2_constraint_is_named_not_silently_zero`.

### 2026-09-21 — Adaptive grids, the three follow-ups (Andreas: "Do 1 then 2 then 3", built in a worktree while the tail gate ran): `lte_grid` measures its own period; the adaptive run steps with the PSS's method; the method table's guidance

**1. `lte_grid` measures its own period.**  The grid is fractions of the
period, so a hint 16 % off put the fine regions 16 % from the edges and every
method's per-step Newton failed (the closing convention and a second pass were
built to recover from that).  The run already contains the period: rising
crossings of the fastest-swinging state over the last 8 hints, each refined by
linear interpolation, read 19.1003 against the reference 19.0986 (+9e-5, the
transient's own discretisation), consistent to 5e-5.  `lte_grid` cuts its
window at the observed period, leaves it in `pss.lte_period`, warns when the
hint is more than 1 % off, keeps the hint (and says so) when no consistent
recurrence is found.  Seeded from `lte_period` on that grid: **gear -17 ppm
(proportional) / -22 (auto), trbdf2 +10 / +10, no second pass under either
convention**; gear's `c` -4.5 %.  The -1436 ppm recorded yesterday for gear on
this fixture was mostly the window's length, not gear.

**2. The adaptive run steps with the PSS's own method.**  `lte_grid` and
`refine_grid` built their Transient with no integrator -- the default
`Gear2Integrator()` whatever the PSS's `method` -- so a radau PSS got a
gear-shaped grid.  Now `integrator=self._integrator_for(self.par.method)`.
Each method on the grid its own run made (mu = 10, reltol 1e-5, seeded from
`lte_period`)::

    method   steps   span     T err (ppm)     c rel
    gear     195     176:1    -22 / -1461     -4.5 % / -8.6 %   (two windows)
    trbdf2   286     833:1    -8.7            +0.29 %
    radau    110     130:1    -0.32           -0.05 %

**Gear's two numbers are two windows** of runs differing only in the period
hint (16.1 vs 19.1): same count, same span, a 70x different period error.
Rotating the converged grid to start at its finest step (no ramp, no seam)
moves each by ~40 ppm only, so it is neither: gear's period on a relaxation
oscillator's frozen adaptive grid depends on where that window's fine regions
sit against the converged edges.  Recorded as gear's property on such grids;
radau on its own 110-point grid is at -0.3 ppm.

**3. The `method` table** carries the adaptive-grid guidance: each method's
own-grid numbers, gear's window spread, "choose trbdf2 or radau there; gear
makes the grid", the reltol the PSS needs (1e-5 too coarse above mu ~ 10 for
every method; the mu = 100 benchmark used 1e-7), and seeding `solve` from
`pss.lte_period`.

Tests: `test_lte_grid_measures_its_own_period_and_the_solve_seeds_from_it`,
`test_lte_grid_steps_with_the_pss_own_method_so_a_radau_grid_is_radau_shaped`;
the tree's `lte_grid` test and the adaptive-grid tests unchanged.  Built on
branch `lte-own-period` in a worktree during the tail gate, cherry-picked and
gated once on the main branch.

### 2026-09-21 — ⚠ CORRECTION: gear's "70x window-to-window spread" on adaptive grids was neither gear's nor real (Andreas: "Do the gear's 70x window-to-window")

Measured before anything else: trbdf2 and radau on the SAME two gear grids,
and a split ladder on both.  **trbdf2 spreads too** (+9.6 vs -180 ppm, 19x);
radau does not (-0.01 vs -0.04): the two windows differ in quality for any
second-order method -- the "bad" one has 12 growth-by-2 steps to the good
one's 5 and 4x the total LTE; the adaptive run's rejection and growth pattern
lands differently each period, and a frozen window inherits whichever it got.
**And the good grid's -22 ppm was a sign-change cancellation**: -21.8 -> +6.9
-> +3.3 under 2x / 4x splitting, against the bad grid's clean second order
-1461 -> -361 -> -92.  Gear's honest number on a 195-point adaptive grid of
this oscillator is O(1000 ppm), second order; the window that read -22 sat
where the error crosses zero.  The `method` table, the test docstring and
the HANDOVER row now say so.  Nothing to fix in gear; the guidance stands
(trbdf2 or radau on a relaxation oscillator's adaptive grid), and the
catalogue gains the rule again: **a suspiciously good number at one N is
refined before it is believed.**  A variable-order BDF is not called for by
this.

### 2026-09-21 — Adaptive grids, the next three (Andreas: "Do 1", then "before running the full suite do item 2 and 3 also"): `lte_grid` folds every settled period; the adaptive run's reltol; `refine_grid` superseded. And 'auto' reversed to 'proportional' on the evidence

**1. The fold.**  A frozen single window inherits whichever rejection-and-
growth pattern one period got (two windows: gear -1461 / -22, trbdf2 -180 /
+10 ppm).  `lte_grid` now folds the last `LTE_FOLD_PERIODS` (24) settled
periods' accepted steps onto a common phase (phase 0 at the rising crossing
the period detector finds), takes the per-phase MINIMUM of the local step as
the density, and re-meshes with growth capped at the controller's own 2x,
the walk overshooting by at most one step and the whole grid rescaled (no
tiny closing step).  Per-phase statistic, measured on the same run: minimum
gear +40 / trbdf2 +10 ppm, 25 % quantile +267 / +13, median +957 / +16, the
last window re-meshed +150 / +6; radau -0.01 everywhere.  **The seam goes at
the COARSEST phase** (the slow branch): at the finest phase (mid-edge) the
period is the same but `c` pays -- gear +28 % vs -3.9 %, trbdf2 +8 % vs
+0.4 % -- the PPV's sensitivity lives at the edges.  The coarse opener is
`_period_grid`'s ramp, now built TOP-DOWN (`f0/2^k, f0/2^k, ..., f0/2`): the
bottom-up ramp's remainder could be a sliver before the next coarse step, a
growth beyond the bound, an Euler drop, gear +918 ppm.  `fold=False` keeps
the single-window cut; a run without a consistent recurrence falls back to
it.  Gear's period on a ~200-point folded grid is an O(300 ppm) constant
whose SIGN depends on the remesh phase (+285 / +20 / -28 measured), second
order under splitting (+285 -> +82); trbdf2 +7 .. +57; radau exact.  ⚠ A
-0.06 ppm gear reading on one remesh was a sign-change cancellation, caught
by the split ladder before it was believed.

**2. The reltol.**  Each method on the folded grid its own run made, mu = 10::

    reltol   gear (pts, ppm, c)        trbdf2                 radau
    1e-4     95   -900   +179 %       146   -2.0   +1.0 %    70   -3.9   -1.9 %
    1e-5     207  +20    +22 %        290   -7.7   +0.27 %   116  -0.17  -0.04 %
    1e-6     425  -3.6   +7.8 %       602   -1.0   +0.05 %   191  -0.004 -0.002 %
    1e-7     928  -1.6   +2.6 %       1294  -0.25  +0.01 %   331   0.000 -0.0002 %

The PSS default 1e-4 makes a grid gear cannot use; 1e-5 is the coarsest that
serves every method at the 10-ppm level: `lte_grid` and `refine_grid` default
to `min(reltol, LTE_GRID_RELTOL_MAX = 1e-5)`; the table is in the docstring.

**3. `refine_grid`.**  One pass on the folded grid: trbdf2 290 -> 6180 points
(21x) for -7.7 -> -0.01 ppm, and its output carries ratios beyond the bound
(gear's transposed replay refused it).  Superseded for adaptive grids by a
tighter `lte_grid` reltol; recorded in its docstring, not fixed.

**'auto' reversed.**  On the folded grid the closing column FAILS from a seed
2 % low where proportional converges (seam mid-edge) or lands +690 ppm off
(seam on the slow branch); its evidence was a raw single window with its
seam on the slow branch, from 16 % off -- a grid `lte_grid` no longer makes
-- and `lte_period` removes the bad seed at its source.  `period_column=
'auto'` now resolves to 'proportional' (Andreas set 'auto' on the earlier
evidence; reversed the same day on this measurement, one line to put back);
'closing' stays selectable and its test pins it on a `fold=False` window.

Tests moved to the folded grid: `test_gear_runs_its_ppv_on_the_lte_grid...`
(|gear| < 500 ppm, second order, told iff |rho - 1| > the threshold), the
own-period test, the tree's mu = 4 test (solves at `lte_period`, as the
contract says), the subdivision test (the top-down ramp), the closing test
(opt-in, raw window).

### 2026-09-21 — ⚠ The fold on a DRIVEN circuit folded on a crossing, half a period from the switch edges (found by the check Andreas asked for before the push)

Every fold measurement was autonomous.  On the switched-capacitor sampler
(clock swinging), `lte_grid(fold=True)` put its finest steps at phases 0.26 ..
0.31 of the drive while the converged output is steepest at 0.75: the fold's
phase 0 was a crossing of the fastest state -- right for an oscillator, whose
phase is its own -- but a driven circuit's sources are functions of absolute
time and its edges sit at fixed phases of the drive.  `fold=False` had hidden
it: the default `tstab` = 200 T starts the single window at exactly the
drive's phase 0.  Fixed: `lte_grid` decides drivenness (`_is_autonomous` on a
coarse time grid); for a driven circuit the period is the drive's (no
detection), the fold boundaries are `k T` exactly, phase 0 stays at the
drive's t = 0 (no rotation to the coarsest phase -- `solve` reads its
fractions from t = 0, and the landed events with them), the seed is the state
at a period boundary.  Test: the ten finest steps within 0.05 T of the ten
steepest phases (was 0.45 off).

The same check found a second, smaller defect that the autonomous fold had
masked: `_fold_periods` clipped the step straddling a period boundary to its
sliver inside the period, and the per-phase MIN over 24 periods whose natural
steps drift against the boundary drove the first and last phase bins to
0.0009 T on the sampler (natural steps there 0.034 T) -- a fine seam on a
flat part of the drive, three of the ten finest fractions.  An oscillator's
crossing seam sits on an edge that is already fine, so the autonomous
measurements never showed it.  A straddling step now keeps its own width.
The test reads the fold's OWN fractions: the solve's grid adds
`_period_grid`'s opener ramp and closing step at the seam, which are
structural and not where the fold put its steps.

### 2026-09-21 — The driven fold MEASURED for accuracy (item 5 of the non-uniform-grid list; Andreas: "Do 5 then 2"), and gear's Euler backstop step found unreplayable by every solved-history consumer

Two drives on the switched-capacitor sampler (`Ron C` = T/100), each
method's fold against uniform grids of the same and double count, the
waveform against a radau uniform-3200 reference of the same drive, the
held variance against kT/C:

    sine clock         pts   fold        uniform N   uniform 2N   | held: fold  / uni N / uni 2N
    gear               125   1.2e-3      4.5e-2      1.5e-2       | 0.778 / 0.976 / 0.995
    trbdf2             261   1.5e-4      2.1e-3      4.8e-4       | 1.0003 / 1.0058 / 1.0014
    radau               95   3.7e-5      2.4e-4      5.1e-5       | 1.00000 / 0.99992 / 0.99999
    pulse clock (edge AT phase 0, tr = T/200)
    trbdf2             199   1.0e-4      2.2e-3      2.2e-3       | 0.9939 / 0.9938 / 0.9984
    radau               87   1.1e-4      4.3e-3      4.3e-3       | 0.9981 / 0.9998 / 1.0000

The waveform is 6.5–40x better on the fold at the same count for every
method and both drives, and the fold at N beats uniform at 2N.  Under the
pulse the uniform error does not move from N to 2N: a uniform grid puts ONE
step across the T/200 ramp whatever N until its cell is shorter than the
ramp, and the turn-on (`tau` = T/100, half the ramp) happens inside that
step; the fold's finest steps sit inside the ramps (phases 0.003 / 0.502).
The seam at the drive's phase 0 with the edge exactly there costs nothing
measurable (radau 1.1e-4 with the edge at the seam, 3.7e-5 with the sine
clock's edges mid-period).  Noise on the fold is at the reference for trbdf2
and radau.

**Gear's held variance on its own fold is 0.78 against 0.98 uniform at the
same count.**  Not the fold's arithmetic: over the period, the fold's
tracking phase runs at h ≈ 1–1.4 tau (46 points over 0.45 T, against
uniform's 56) because the state's LTE asks for nothing there, and gear's
covariance recursion has its recorded O(h/tau) tracking floor (0.52–0.66 x
kT/C on the fold's tracking steps, 0.65 uniform); the held value is that
floor carried through the turn-off.  The fold resolves the STATE; a noise
quantity with its own time scale is resolved only where the state's grid
happens to be fine.  trbdf2 and radau have no such floor.  Recorded in
`lte_grid`'s docstring as guidance, not fixed: the floor is gear's
covariance item, and the fold's grid is the right grid for the solution.

**The pulse clock under gear crashed `PAC.covariance` (`IndexError`) on
every grid, uniform included.**  `event_grid` lands the ramp inside a T/63
cell; the sliver from the ramp's end to the next node (0.0029 T) grows 5.4x
into the next cell, past the zero-stability bound, and
`Gear2Integrator.check_order_drop` takes that step at order 1 — `alphas =
(1/h, −1/h)`, no third coefficient.  `PAC.covariance`'s step map read
`alphas[2]` unguarded; `_monodromy_matvec_transposed` REFUSED a two-alpha
step as "unreachable through `solve`" (true when written: gear had no
events landed until 2026-09-20); the PPV sample conversion read `_al[2]`
too.  A one-step companion is a two-step companion with a zero third
coefficient — the C ring keeps rolling across the step (the transient's own
rule) and the step does not read its second entry — so all three sites take
`a2 = 0` for a two-alpha step.  Verified: with the Euler step in the ring
the forward covariance and the reverse `sampled_variance` agree at the hold
instant to 5e-4 at N = 252 and 2e-3 at 126 (at 63 the sampled sum's 31
sidebands truncate it by 5.6 % — the recorded resolution item; `tail=True`
overshoots there, 0.957, because gear's floor-limited spectrum is not the
1/ν² the closure assumes), and the held variance climbs gear's O(h/tau)
floor (0.686 / 0.740 / 0.902 x kT/C).  ⚠ Three instrument errors in one
afternoon, all mine and all caught by the test refusing them: I wrote the
verification's numbers into the source comment BEFORE measuring (0.9878 /
0.9985 — invented); the hold window was the pulse's TRACKING phase; and I
read 0.686 against 0.647 as "agreement to 1e-3" because the print put a
mean beside a point value.  The lesson is the old one, twice over: name the
number AFTER the instrument has read it, and read the instrument's units
before calling two of its readings equal.

### 2026-09-21 — Item 2: the period quadrature breaks its spline at landed events (Andreas: "Do 5 then 2")

`periodic_spline_weights` lifted the trapezoid's second-order cap on
event-free grids only; under a landed edge every period integral — the noise
folds' `_period_quadrature`, `PAC._period_weights`, the free-period Fourier
branch — kept the trapezoid, because a spline C² across a kink rings.  A
cubic spline fitted PER SEGMENT between the event nodes (not-a-knot ends,
node 0 always a break when any event exists — an edge at the drive's t = 0
is dropped by `event_grid` and would sit inside the periodic seam) never
crosses a kink.  `periodic_spline_weights(t, T, breaks)`; `PSS._event_nodes`
maps the landed fractions to node indices; the three callers pass them.
Uniform grids are untouched (the callers return before the rule).

Exact ladder, `exp(sin) + triangle` with both corners landed on a
1 + 0.15 sin grid, N = 52 .. 1602: periodic spline +4.0e-5 .. −2.2e-8 with
no order (ringing); trapezoid +1.8e-5 .. −7.8e-9, second order; piecewise
−8.6e-7 .. −1.8e-15, fourth and better.  Breaking only at node 0 on a
smooth integrand: −8.9e-7 .. −6.1e-13 against the periodic rule's +1.7e-8
.. +2.5e-13 — a constant, not an order.

On the circuit (pulse-clocked sampler, ramp ends landed, the output's
harmonics from `_period_quadrature` against a radau uniform-3200 reference):

    radau on its fold      N    trapezoid H1   H2      |  piecewise H1   H2
                          90    5.2e-2   3.1e-1        |  5.3e-4   2.1e-2
                         177    1.3e-2   7.5e-2        |  1.6e-5   9.6e-4
                         351    3.2e-3   1.9e-2        |  1.3e-5   5.4e-5   (H1 at the reference's floor from 177)
    radau uniform    90/176/350: 5.6e-3 / 5.6e-3 / 3.6e-4 either rule — the samples' own error (one step across the ramp), the rule is not the bottleneck there
    gear on its fold  72/134/259: H1 2.0e-1 / 4.2e-2 / 9.9e-3 trapezoid, 5.4e-2 / 1.3e-2 / 1.7e-2 piecewise; H2 1.2 / 2.7e-1 / 6.5e-2 → 1.1 / 5.6e-2 / 3.3e-3; H0 3–4e-3 either rule

Against an ANALYTIC reference (the pulsed RC of the honour-the-grid test,
tr = 0, radau, edges landed, `carrier_phasor` vs the exact coefficient):
3.77e-4 / 9.39e-5 / 2.33e-5 at N = 100 / 200 / 400 under the trapezoid,
7.9e-8 / 4.6e-8 / 1.4e-9 under the piecewise rule — 4800x at 100, at the
reference's own floor from there (its X1 is a fine mean of the exact
solution).  That test's pin moves from "the weights are the trapezoid's,
second-order ladder" to "the weights are the piecewise spline's, errors
below 5e-7 / 2e-8".

Radau (the default) gains two orders on H1 and reaches the reference's floor
at 177 points; every method gains on H2.  Gear's harmonics on its fold are
limited by gear's own samples (H0 3e-3 under either rule, the Euler
backstop step in the ring) and its H1 is a wash between the rules at the
sample-error level — a fourth-order rule applied to second-order samples is
second order, and which constant you get depends on the error's shape.
Recorded, not fixed: the samples are gear's item, not the quadrature's.

### 2026-09-21 — Item 1: why 'closing' "failed" on folded grids — a conditional polish, and 'auto' = closing restored (Andreas: "Do 1 and then 2")

The reversal's evidence — "on the folded grid closing lands +690 ppm off
(seam on the slow branch) or fails from 2 % off (seam mid-edge)" — measured
again on the current fold (seam at the coarsest phase), vdP μ = 10, seeds
0 / 0.5 / 2 / 5 / 16 % low, the reference 19.098600502:

    gear, fold (205 pts, last step 0.037 T)
      seed   proportional   closing (as shipped)   stretch   polish forced
      0 %    +1431.3 ppm    +1614.9                1.04      +1408.5
      0.5    +1431.5        +2470.0                1.19      +1408.6
      2      +1431.8        +3646.1                1.61      +1408.9
      5      FAILS          +1410.8 (2nd pass)     —         —
      16     FAILS          FAILS
    gear, raw window (195 pts)
      0 %    −1298.4        −1442.7                0.96      −1319.1
      0.5    −1298.3         −963.1                1.10      −1319.0
      2      −1298.0          −33.5                1.48      −1318.7
      5      −1297.4        +3128.6                2.28      −1318.1
      16     FAILS          −1312.4 (2nd pass)
    trbdf2, fold (290 pts, last step 2× its neighbour): +3.3 ppm at 0 .. 5 % —
      the bound-triggered polish already fired from 0.5 % on; ⚠ at 16 % BOTH
      columns CONVERGE to −24541.7 ppm, a periodic solution 2.45 % short (a
      spurious root of the discretised map, the line-search lesson again: a
      converged solve from a bad seed is not a correct one — `lte_period`
      removes the seed, and this is the reason it must).
    trbdf2, raw window (286 pts): −8.7 ppm; closing at 0.5 % read +2.2 at a
      1.57× stretch (no polish), −8.7 polished — the same seed-dependence at
      the 10-ppm level.

Closing's converged answer MOVED WITH THE SEED: the closing step absorbs the
whole period correction, and a stretch inside the zero-stability bound
(1.04 .. 2.28×) is a change of discretisation that stayed in the answer,
because the polish fired only beyond the bound.  The window's "−33.5 ppm" at
2 % is a cancellation (the shape rule: a suspiciously good number at one
seed).  The "+690 ppm" of the record was this.  The "fails from 2 %" was the
old mid-edge seam, whose last step was too short to absorb 2 % of T; with
the seam at the coarsest phase closing converges from 0 .. 5 % on the fold
where proportional fails at 5 %, and from 16 % on the window.  The
seed-exact case stretches too (1.04 on gear's fold: the discretisation's own
+1431 ppm period error is absorbed by the last step).

Fixed: the proportional polish on the caller's fractions at the solved
period is UNCONDITIONAL (one Newton from a converged state, one or two
iterations); the closing answer is then proportional's at T*, seed-
independent to 0.4 ppm.  `period_column='auto'` = 'closing' again ON A
CALLER'S GRID for an autonomous run — Andreas's original call, restored on
the diagnosis; on a uniform grid 'auto' stays proportional (my first
restore made closing universal and two uniform-grid gear fixtures that
converge proportionally — the λ₂ = 0.9 stall fixture, the periodic-state
fold — did not converge with one step of N absorbing the correction);
'proportional' stays selectable (one solve, the seed's own grid).  The closing test is re-pinned:
'auto' ≡ 'closing' bit for bit, closing from 0 / 2 / 5 % agrees with itself
to 2e-6 and with proportional to 1e-4.  (The polished answer sits 23 ppm
from proportional-from-the-seed on gear's fold, +1408 vs +1431: both are
proportional solves on the same fractions from different x0 — the grid's
phase relative to the orbit is anchored by x0 — a discretisation difference
inside gear's own +1400 ppm, not a defect.)

### 2026-09-21 — Item 2: the recurrence detector on a state that crosses its midline more than once per period (Andreas: "Do 1 and then 2")

The premise I offered — "`_observed_period` gives up SILENTLY" — was wrong:
a `None` is warned ("no consistent recurrence was found ... the grid is cut
at the period passed") and the grid falls back to the single window.  What
is true is the capability loss behind it: the detector took EVERY rising
crossing of the fastest-swinging state, so a strong third harmonic
(`sin + 1.2 sin 3`, three crossings per period) or two pulses of different
height per period (two crossings) spread the spacings past the 1 % rule and
the fold was refused — on exactly the harmonic-rich oscillators whose grids
need folding.  Measured on synthetic waveforms with the period known: `sin`,
`sin + 0.8 sin 3` and `sin + 0.5 sin 2` (one crossing each — the extra
harmonics do not cross the midline) read the period exactly; `sin + 1.2
sin 3` and the two-pulse case returned None from hints at 1.0 and 1.1 T.

`_crossing_chain(tw, y, T_hint)`: every rising crossing when they are
already one per period (bit-identical to before), else the chain walked back
from the last crossing choosing, per period of the hint, the crossing
nearest the expected time within a quarter period — the hint names the
branch.  `_observed_period` and `_fold_periods` both use it, so the fold's
boundaries are the same chain the period came from.  Test: the two
multi-crossing cases return the period to 1e-6 from hints 10 % either side,
the single-crossing case is unchanged, the chain is one per period.

### 2026-09-21 — Item 3: the "gear swing" on folded grids was the fold's ALIGNMENT, and it was every method's (Andreas: "Start with 3 then 4")

The record said gear's period constant on folded grids swings O(300 ppm),
sign-changing with the remesh phase, and asked whether that is the
variable-step BDF2 coefficient at the seam.  Instrument: six folds of vdP
μ = 10 from six transient starts, each grid solved by gear (1×, 2×, 4×),
trbdf2 and radau — the same grid under three methods separates the grid from
the method:

    start   N    gear 1x    2x      4x    | trbdf2 | radau
    +2.0   205   +1408.6  +320.1  +77.6  |  +149.6 |  -0.6
    +1.0   205    +301.5   +85.3  +22.6  |   +46.2 |  -0.0
    -1.5   204   +1562.5  +361.8  +89.0  |  +172.5 |  -0.5
    +0.5   205    +297.9   +84.3  +22.4  |   +45.7 |  -0.0
    +2.5   192    +272.7   +79.0  +21.2  |   +43.9 |  -0.0
    -0.3   205   +1488.5  +340.4  +82.8  |  +159.8 |  -0.5

Two clusters, 5× apart, and trbdf2 on the same grids splits the same way:
the swing is the GRID.  Gear is a steady 6.5–9× trbdf2 on any given grid
(BDF2's constant against TR-BDF2's on coarse edges), second order (4.4× per
halving), and there is no sign change here.  The clusters' folds are the
same grid to the point — 205 points, the same minimum step, the same two
edge groups of 80/81 points, the same seed state to 1e-3 — except that
cluster B's second edge group starts 0.020 T later than A's and its first
0.007 T later: the signature of a RESCALE.  `_fold_periods` walked past the
period by up to one coarse step and rescaled every fraction to sum 1, which
shifts every phase by up to that step times its phase; the orbit's edges
then fell on the boundary of the fine groups instead of inside them, and
the coarse steps at the edge's onset cost 5× for every second-order method.

Fixed: the walk ends exactly at the period; the remainder is spread over the
trailing steps, each grown to at most 2× the one before (the tail is the
seam, the coarsest phase), so there is no sliver and no ratio beyond the
controller's own.  The same six folds:

    start   N    gear 1x   2x (before the tail spread)  | trbdf2
    +2.0   204    +26.0   +14.6                        |  +11.8
    +1.0   205    +17.5   +12.4                        |  +10.4
    -1.5   203    +24.2   +15.1                        |  +12.6
    +0.5   205    +16.3   +12.1                        |  +10.2
    +2.5   192    -44.0    -2.1                        |   +4.3
    -0.3   204    +30.1   +15.7                        |  +12.4
    (every grid's maximum consecutive ratio exactly 2.00; the first cut of the
    fix appended the remainder as its own step and left 3.1-3.65x shrinks at
    the seam, harmless for stability but a sliver -- spread since)

One cluster; gear 57× better on the misaligned folds and 12× on the others;
gear's remaining tens of ppm sign-change between grids (−44 at 192 points
against +16..+29 at 205) is the ordinary cancellation regime of a small
constant, the same shape the −22 ppm window showed.  The record's "gear
swings, use trbdf2 or radau on a relaxation oscillator's adaptive grid" is
withdrawn as a statement about gear: every method paid the alignment, gear
merely paid it 8× louder.  The method-table guidance is updated to what the
matrix shows — gear ~8× trbdf2 on the same grid, both second order.

### 2026-09-21 — Item 4: gear's covariance floor — measured, the consistent stochastic-BDF2 term tried, NOT built (Andreas: "then 4")

Gear's covariance in a tracking phase sits at an O(h/τ) floor (the kT/C
sampler: 0.598 / 0.740 / 0.847 / 0.916 × kT/C at 100 / 200 / 400 / 800
uniform points, ratios 1.7 → 1.9, first order) where radau and trbdf2 read
1.00000 at 200: the stage methods inject the exact per-stage covariance
(`_stage_injection`, Van Loan fallback), gear's kind injects the frozen
rectangle `Jf⁻¹ (CY/2h) Jf⁻ᵀ` — one noise sample per step at the step's end.

Two candidates measured in scratch (monkeypatched, no source change):

1. **The Van Loan injection with gear's pair map**: held 2.45 / 2.40 / 2.34
   / 2.30 × kT/C.  Wrong by construction: the exact continuous increment
   assumes the physical propagator's gain h/C for the noise, while the BDF2
   pair map's gain is Jf⁻¹ = 2h/(3C) at the leading order and the pair
   recursion's own amplification makes up the difference — the rectangle
   is self-consistent because the same multistep formula discretises drift
   and diffusion; mixing an exact increment into it is not.

2. **The consistent stochastic-BDF2 noise term.**  For the noise path W the
   exact solution satisfies α₀x_{n+1} + α₁x_n + α₂x_{n−1} = σ(α₀ΔW_{n+1} −
   α₂ΔW_n) (α₀+α₁+α₂ = 0), two increments correlated across steps, against
   the rectangle's single ΔW_{n+1}/h.  Augmented state [x_n, x_{n−1}, ξ_n]:
   tracking 0.899 / 0.963 / 0.988 / 0.9966 (errors 0.10 / 0.037 / 0.012 /
   0.0034, ratios 2.7 → 3.5 — the second-order fix for the tracking phase)
   — and the HELD variance breaks: 0.569 / 0.751 / 0.868 / 0.932 against
   the rectangle's 0.963 / 0.992 / 0.9987 / 0.9998.  The −α₂ξ_n correction
   for the previous increment is applied through the NEW step's Jf⁻¹; at
   the switch-off the new Jf⁻¹ is 1/(α₀C) where the increment was injected
   through 1/g_on, six decades smaller — the constant-coefficient identity
   behind the two-increment term does not survive a conductance that
   changes by 10⁶ between steps.  A multistep stochastic injection for
   time-varying stiffness needs the residual of the exact solution with the
   propagator Φ inside both integrals, which is not the pure-noise identity;
   I do not have that derivation verified, and a guessed transition rule is
   exactly the kind of fix this campaign has learned to refuse.

Recorded, not built.  The advice stands and is now measured rather than
inferred: for noise on a coarse or folded grid use trbdf2 or radau (exact
per-stage injection); gear's covariance is first order in h/τ wherever the
grid runs at h ~ τ.  The open derivation is on the HANDOVER list.

### 2026-09-21 — Item 5: `refine_grid` DELETED (Andreas: "Start the refine_grid", then "Do the deletion")

The measurement that decided it, vdP μ = 10, reltol 1e-5, one repair pass
from a CONVERGED solve, against `lte_grid`'s fold at the same tolerance:

    start                     radau                                  gear
    lte_grid fold             114 pts  -0.3 ppm  ratio 2.00          204 pts  +26.0 ppm  ratio 2.00
    uniform 100 -> refine     +145 -> 1100 pts  -0.0  ratio 6.0      coarse solve does not converge
    uniform 200 -> refine     +4.8 -> 1120 pts  -0.0  ratio 4.0      -6264 -> 4617 pts  -0.9  ratio 2.56
      second pass             1125 pts  -0.0  ratio 4.0              4621 pts  -0.3  ratio 5.0
    decimated fold -> refine  57 (-10.7) -> 1077 pts  -0.0  ratio 5.3   102 (+1434) -> 4507 pts  +1.8  ratio 2.65

It reaches the accuracy and always lands at ~1100 (radau) / ~4600 (gear)
points — ten to twenty times the fold — because it subdivides every cell
uniformly at the finest step the transient wanted anywhere inside that cell
and can neither move nor remove a point (the roadmap's "structural gap",
now with numbers), and its output ratios of 4–6 are beyond the two-step
bound, so gear's replays refuse the grids it makes.  Its one argument was
"no 200-period stabilisation run" — and the fold from a converged state does
not need one either: `lte_grid(T, x0=x_converged, tstab=25 T)` from the same
converged uniform-200 solves gives radau 114 points −0.3 ppm in 7 s (11 s at
40 T, −0.2) and gear 204 points +27.8 ppm in 3 s (205, +15.0 at 40 T) — the
same grids the 200-period run makes.  No niche left.

Deleted: `PSS.refine_grid`, its test
(`test_refine_grid_repairs_an_under_resolved_grid_and_reaches_a_fixed_point`),
and every mention in the code that read as current; the repair idiom and the
table are in `lte_grid`'s docstring.  B7c's roadmap entry is annotated, not
rewritten: the separation-constant lesson it records (a constant transplanted
between two rules is a silent no-op) stands on its own and is referenced from
the `event_grid` test.

### 2026-09-21 — Events as a Newton unknown, phase A (Andreas: "Do the events as a Newton unknown")

**The defect, measured before anything was built.**  A voltage-mode PWM
loop — a 4 V sawtooth against the output filtered by an RC of 10 T, a
`VSwitch` (20 mV `tanh` window) feeding an LC filter — switches once per
period at a phase that depends on the state (0.7081 at the solution; the
ramp's own reset at 0.98/0.99 is a source event `break_events` already
lands).  Uniform grids, max |error| of the output against a radau
uniform-6400 reference and the time-averaged error, 100 / 200 / 400 / 800
points:

    radau   6.8e-3 / 1.8e-3 / 7.0e-4 / 7.3e-4    mean 2.9e-2 / 1.0e-2 / 5.2e-3 / 3.3e-3
    trbdf2  3.6e-3 / 4.2e-3 / 1.8e-3 / 1.8e-4    mean 2.3e-2 / 8.1e-3 / 7.1e-3 / 2.8e-3
    gear    1.2e-2 / 8.0e-3 / 3.4e-3 / 9.1e-4    mean 2.1e-2 / 8.5e-3 / 7.5e-3 / 3.1e-3

First order in the mean for every method, radau included: the crossing
sits inside a step and no step size makes that step's error small.  (A
first fixture without the filter CHATTERED — eight crossings per period, a
comparator-driven switch with no latch — and is not the class; recorded so
it is not built again.)  Landing a fine cluster on the reference's
crossings by hand vs the same cluster 0.03 T away: radau 9.8e-5 vs 3.7e-4
— where the fine steps sit is what matters.

**The build.**  `Circuit.state_events()` — each element's switching
conditions as `(row, threshold)` over its state, `row . x = threshold`,
linear by contract; `SubCircuit` maps them through `elementnodemap`;
`VSwitch` declares `v(cp) - v(cm) = (Von + Voff)/2`.  `PSS.solve(...,
state_events=True)` on a DRIVEN circuit under radau or trbdf2 runs a second
stage after the ordinary solve: the crossings of the stage-1 orbit become
unknowns `theta_k` (fractions of the period) with `x_0`; the base grid gets
the crossings landed (`_land_fractions`, `event_grid`'s snap rule); between
consecutive anchors every step scales with its segment (`_event_remap`,
with `hsens[j, k] = d h_j / d theta_k` analytic and the grid's topology
frozen, which keeps the Newton exact); the traversals propagate one column
per event by the period column's stage algebra with the per-step weight
from `hsens`, and capture the state and sensitivities at the event nodes;
the bordered residual adds `row_k . x(theta_k T) - threshold_k = 0`.  The
solved fractions become `_grid_fracs`, the event fractions join
`event_times` (breaks for the period quadrature), so every consumer
replays on the landed grid.

**Two things the period column never needed, found by the FD check
(the record's rule: the dT column was got wrong twice; check before use).**
First pass: the state block and the event row's `x`-part agreed to 1e-8,
the θ column was 77 % off and the event row's θ-derivative had the WRONG
SIGN (−3.92 vs FD +4.24).  (1) A driven source moves with the grid: shifting
a step shifts the times the stages are evaluated at, `f = -(i + u(t))`
changes by `-u_dot dt_stage`, `dt_stage = tau_n + c_i dh` with `tau_n` the
accumulated shift of the step's start — the term `-h sum_j A_ij u_dot_j
dt_j` on every stage.  (2) `_k_at`, the stage derivative, took `u` at
t = 0 ("only built for autonomous circuits, where it is constant") — on
the ramp's row that is 4 V wrong at most stages; it takes the stage time
now.  After both: θ column 1.2e-8 (radau) / 8.8e-9 (trbdf2) against FD,
event row 4.2404 vs 4.2404.

**The midpoint was not enough — the record's tr = 0 lesson, again.**  With
`VSwitch` declaring the window's MIDPOINT as the event, the staged radau
solve read 6.3e-4 of the swing at 50, 100, 200 and 400 points alike against
a staged 1600-point reference (and against an unstaged 6400-point one,
whose own error is first order at the crossing, ~4e-4 — an instrument that
does not land the event cannot judge a solve that does), and trbdf2 staged
was WORSE than unstaged (8.5e-3 vs 5.6e-3 at 50; its θ 0.7101 against
0.7082).  The `tanh` window is 20 mV wide — 0.005 T on the 4 V/T ramp, and
5e-5 T on the 400 V/T reset — and landing its centre leaves half the
S-curve in each neighbouring step, integrated by collocation points that
straddle a kink.  Same as the pulse whose 1e-18 ramp had to keep BOTH ends
as nodes: `VSwitch.state_events()` now declares both edges (`Von`, `Voff`),
and `_land_fractions` cuts the segment between two landed events that is
narrower than its neighbours into `EVENT_WINDOW_STEPS` = 8 steps, which the
remap scales with the window.

**Measured, on the loop with a 20 Ω off-phase path** (the first had a
10 kΩ "keeps the node defined" resistor and the interrupted inductor
current made a 220–650 V kick with τ = 2e-5 T that no grid resolved; its
energy shifted every node's operating point by 4e-4 V and the staged ladder
sat on that floor — the fixture, not the machinery; the 100- and 400-point
staged solves agreed with each other to six digits in θ while the reference
differed).  Against a STAGED radau-1600 reference (an unstaged one has its
own first-order error at the crossing, ~4e-4, and cannot judge a solve that
lands it), max |error| of the output over the swing, 50 / 100 / 200 / 400
points:

    radau   unstaged  4.7e-3 / 3.2e-3 / 3.8e-3 / 1.6e-3
            staged    5.2e-4 / 3.2e-4 / 1.8e-4 / 1.8e-4    theta within 3e-4 T of the reference's
    trbdf2  unstaged  4.3e-3 / 2.3e-3 / 1.8e-3 / 1.4e-3
            staged    1.1e-2 / 8.9e-3 / 2.4e-3 / 2.3e-3    theta 2e-3 .. 5e-4 T late

Radau: 9–21× at 50–200 points, the crossing landed, the staged error
falling until the reference's own ~1.5e-4 floor stops the ladder (a
Richardson pair is the next instrument, not more points).  ⚠ trbdf2 gains
NOTHING here and reads worse than unstaged at coarse counts: its staged
error is a smooth curve over the off phase (+8 mV at 0.69, −29 mV at 0.99
on the output at 100 points; fb's DC +8 mV, hence its crossing 2e-3 T late)
— its own second-order error on the switch-off decay (τ = 0.8 steps at
100 points), which the unstaged solve had partly CANCELLED against the
crossing's error; landing the crossing exposes it.  The shape rule again: a
better-looking number from a cancellation.  The stage stays on for trbdf2
(its Jacobian is verified to 1e-8 and the landed crossing is its own
discrete solution's) and the method table says to use radau on such
circuits.  The window sub-grid (`EVENT_WINDOW_STEPS` = 8 between a switch's
two edges; the first rule compared the gap with a sliver the insertion
itself had made and never fired on the on-window — it compares with the
base step now) changes radau's number by 2e-7: the window's resolution was
never the limit; the stiff decay after the switch-off is, and that is the
fold's business (phase B: the stage on an `lte_grid` fold).

**Not built (phase B):** gear (a two-step companion's step derivative needs
the previous step's partial, the 3/2 of `residual_dT`); autonomous circuits
with state events (period and events as unknowns together); the TOTAL
monodromy through a moving event — `M + P_theta (dtheta/dx_0)` by the
bordered Schur complement, the saltation matrix derived rather than
guessed — which PAC and noise on such circuits need; the stage on a folded
grid.

### 2026-09-22 — Events as a Newton unknown, phase B, first two results (Andreas: "Start phase B")

**(1) The total monodromy through a moving event — BUILT, exact.**  After
the staged Newton converges, `_state_event_stage` evaluates the bordered
pieces once more and stores `_monodromy = Mx + P_theta (dtheta/dx_0)` with
`dtheta/dx_0 = -Gt^{-1} G` from the event rows — the Schur complement of the
bordered system, i.e. the saltation matrix derived rather than guessed
(`_event_sensitivity` keeps `dtheta/dx_0`).  Measured on the PWM loop at
100 points against the finite difference of the staged period map (inner
Newton on θ per evaluation): the fixed-grid `Mx` **109 %** off, the total
**3.3e-8**; dominant multiplier 0.691 where `Mx` reads 0.632.  Pinned at
60 points (two columns to 1e-5, the fixed grid ≥ 30 % off).

⚠ **A LOCAL saltation is not the way to the consumers.**  The classic form
— a rank-1 correction on the two steps around each event from the
one-step length column, `S = I − p wᵀ/(wᵀp)` — assembled on the factored
period's own per-step maps (products agree with the traversal to 2e-13)
reads 0.656 for the dominant multiplier: 28 % of the correction short of the
exact total.  Which steps absorb the event's motion is not a small choice
at this resolution (the steps around the crossing are coarse against the
stiff decay), so the derivative of the discrete map depends on the
convention, and only the one the solve actually uses — the
segment-proportional remap — gives the derivative of the solution the solve
returns.  Consequence for PAC and noise on such circuits: not a per-step
patch in the replays but the BORDERED linear system — for a sideband
response a bordered solve per frequency (`[I − M, −P_θ; G, Gt]`), for the
covariance a bordered Lyapunov closure.  Not built; recorded as the design.

**(4) The stage on an `lte_grid` fold — MEASURED, and the fold wins on a
driven circuit.**  Radau on the loop's own fold (112 points at lte reltol
1e-5, 164 at 1e-6): 5.2e-5 / 5.3e-5 of the swing unstaged, 5.2e-5 / 5.3e-5
staged (θ 0.6914 / 0.6960 / 0.9929 / 0.9929, the reference's) — the fold
alone is 6× better than the staged uniform-100 solve and 60× better than the
unstaged one, and the stage on top changes it by 0.4 %.  A driven circuit's
crossing sits at the same drive phase every period, so the fold's transient
already puts its finest steps on it and on the decay after it (min step
4.5e-5 T).  So on driven circuits the stage's value is (a) a coarse or
caller's grid, where it buys 9–21×, and (b) the DERIVATIVE, which the fold
does not fix — its monodromy is the fixed-grid one, 109 % off.

**Remaining in phase B:** gear's columns (its previous-step partial is
assemblable from the two partials that exist, `(residual_dT·T −
residual_dh·h_n)/h_{n−1}`, plus the BDF source term at `t_{n+1}`);
autonomous circuits with events (period and events as unknowns together —
the hsens column algebra takes the period as one more column); the bordered
sideband response and Lyapunov closure above.

### 2026-09-22 — Phase B, the bordered sideband response: designed, drafted, NOT verified — reverted (Andreas: "Start phase B")

The design (the consequence of the local-saltation finding above): on a
staged solve `PAC.solve` borders its per-frequency system with the event
rows — `y_end = M y_0 + w + P_θ dθ`, `w_k · (P_node y_0 + f_node + Pk_node
dθ) = 0` — by block elimination (the m × m solve for the source and for each
event column, a K × K Schur complement for `dθ`, and `Pk_node dθ` added to
every node's response).  Drafted as `events_pac_patch.py` (the stage keeps
`_event_columns`: the event columns at every node and at the period, the
homogeneous maps to the nodes, the event rows' derivatives) and applied for
measurement, then REVERTED: the instrument that was to verify it never
passed its own control.  Three attempts — PAC's reported coefficients
against the DC shift of re-solved orbits under a small cosine source at f₀
and 2f₀ (sign flip at f₀ with the magnitude to 0.1 %, 6× at 2f₀), the
reconstruction Re Σ Vₖ e^{j2πfₖt} of `PAC.result` (√2 off on a LINEAR
circuit: the result is the RMS, energy-folded one-sided spectrum the record
warns about), and the envelope Re(y e^{jωt}) from PAC's own helpers (3× off
on the linear circuit) — and a bordering cannot be judged by an instrument
that misreads a linear circuit.  PAC is pinned in the suite against
analytic transfer functions ("the reported coefficients equal H_l · u_ac to
1e-15"); the next attempt starts from THAT fixture and convention, builds
the switched case on it, and only then reads the bordering.  The draft and
the three instruments are in the session's scratchpad; nothing of this is
in the tree.

### 2026-09-22 — Phase B continued: the bordered sideband response, this time with the conventions pinned first (Andreas: "Continue with the open items in phase B")

**Why the three instruments failed, found.**  `vac` DEFAULTS TO 1 on every
voltage source (`VS`, `VSin`, `VPulse`): in the AC and PAC source vector
every source in the fixture was an AC source, while the finite difference
perturbed one.  With `vac=0` on the others, PAC's coefficient equals the AC
analysis's phasor to 1e-12 on the linear driven circuit at 0.3 f₀ and at
f₀ (the second of the two twins at |f| = f₀ is the response; the first is
the folded zero), and the finite difference of periodic orbits under a
cosine source of amplitude ε equals that phasor divided by e^{jφ}, φ the
source's AC phase — `VSin`'s `phase` parameter serves both the transient
and the AC analysis, so a transient cosine (`phase=90`) is an AC source of
phase 90°: FD 1.9563 − 2.3377j against (2.3389 + 1.9573j)/j.  A trap for
any instrument that perturbs one source and reads PAC: set `vac=0` on the
rest, and divide by the perturbed source's AC phase.

### 2026-09-22 — Phase B: the bordered sideband response is EXACT; the "reverted" entry above was the instrument's verdict, not the bordering's (Andreas: "Continue with the open items in phase B")

Three things were wrong with the instruments, none with the design:

1. `vac` DEFAULTS TO 1 on every voltage source (`VS`, `VSin`, `VPulse`).
   Every source in the fixture was an AC source in PAC while the finite
   difference perturbed one — the 3× and the "√2".  With `vac=0` on the
   others, PAC's coefficient equals the AC analysis's phasor to 1e-12 on the
   linear driven circuit (peak convention, not RMS — that note is for
   `fpss`), and the finite difference of orbits under a cosine source
   equals it divided by e^{jφ}, φ the source's AC phase — `VSin.phase`
   serves the transient AND the AC analysis, so a transient cosine
   (`phase=90`) is an AC source of phase 90°.
2. PAC's `y` IS the complex response; its periodic envelope is `y e^{−jωt}`
   (what it DFTs).  My reconstruction multiplied by `e^{jωt}` a second
   time.  Fixed, the linear circuit reads 8e-12 and the switched loop
   UNSTAGED 1.6e-8 against the finite difference: PAC on a fixed grid is the
   exact linearisation of the fixed-grid solve.
3. Re-solved orbits at ±ε are the wrong finite difference for a STAGED
   solve: each one re-lands its base grid from its own first-stage crossing,
   so its interior grid moves with the anchor as well as with θ, and at this
   resolution (the steps around the crossing are coarse against the stiff
   decay) that convention differs from the fixed-base one by 9 % in δθ.
   Shown directly: along that FD direction the bordered linear prediction
   equals the directional FD of the periodicity residual EXACTLY (0.1087 =
   0.1087) and both Jacobian blocks match to 1e-7 — the bordered system is
   the exact linearisation of the map the solve returns; the re-solved FD is
   the derivative of a different discrete map.  The valid instrument keeps
   the base: an inner Newton on `(x_0, θ)` with the ±ε circuits on the
   solve's own grid (what the monodromy check already did).

With that instrument, PWM loop, 100 points, f₀ and 2f₀: **bordered PAC
`out` 4.1e-10 / 4.7e-10, `fb` 1.4e-10 / 7.0e-10, `sw` 4.4e-8 / 3.4e-7**, the
crossings' shifts identical to six digits (0.012620 / 0.012616 / −0.000121
/ −0.000121 per volt at f₀); **unbordered `out` 0.61 / 0.70, `fb` 0.18 /
0.17, `sw` 2.9 / 11.5.**  The design stands as drafted: after the staged
Newton the stage stores `_event_columns` (the event columns at every node
and at the period, the homogeneous maps to the nodes, the event rows'
derivatives — on the grid `_period_grid` makes of the solved fractions,
which is now `_grid_fracs` itself, so every consumer and the stage agree
node for node); `PAC.solve` solves the m × m system for the source and for
each event column, closes the K × K Schur complement for `δθ`, corrects
`y_0` and adds `Pk_node δθ` to every node's response; `PAC.event_shifts`
carries `δθ` per frequency and `PAC.time_response` the complex time-domain
response `(times, y)` per frequency — the sideband coefficients are
quadrature integrals of the envelope, and on a grid with eight 6e-4 T steps
inside the switch's window summing them back at the nodes is not exact
(1.2 of the swing on `out`), so a time-domain reading comes from there.
Test pinned at 60 points on `PAC.time_response` and `PAC.event_shifts`.

The "designed, drafted, not verified, reverted" entry above stands as
history; this supersedes its verdict.

### 2026-09-22 — Phase B: the bordered ADJOINT sideband row (Andreas: "Continue with the open items in phase B")

The transpose of the bordered forward solve, so `pnoise`, `mixer_response`
and everything built on `adjoint_sideband_row` read the moving crossings.
With `B = [[I − αM, −αP_θ], [Gn, Gt]]`, the output's costate `g` (the
existing reverse pass) and its θ-sensitivity `g_θ = Σₙ cₙ d·Pkₙ` (the event
columns at the nodes, weighted by the output's sideband phase and the period
quadrature), `Bᵀ [z; ζ] = [g; g_θ]` by block elimination — `Z_G = (I −
αM)⁻ᵀ Gnᵀ` (K transposed solves), `ζ = (Gtᵀ + αP_θᵀ Z_G)⁻¹ (g_θ + αP_θᵀ
z_g)`, `z = z_g − Z_G ζ` — and the row is the direct forced part, plus
`α Wᵀ z` (the transposed forced replay), plus the event rows' term: a second
reverse pass with the raw costate injection `−ζ_k w_k` at event node k
(`_sideband_forced_full/_dirk(extra=)`), which is the source coupling of the
node response the event row reads.  Verified as the suite verifies PAC
everywhere — dual consistency: the bordered forward solve's reported
coefficients equal `H_l · u_ac` (PWM loop, 60 points, f = 0.3 f₀, sidebands
0, ±1) to 1e-10, and the unbordered row differs from them by more than 1 %.

### 2026-09-22 — Phase B: gear's event columns and stage; the AUTONOMOUS stage (Andreas: "Continue with the open items in phase B")

**Gear.**  A two-step companion's residual depends on its own step and the
previous one, so each event column carries `dr/dh_n hsens[j] + dr/dh_{n−1}
hsens[j−1]`.  The previous step's partial is assembled from the two that
exist: `residual_dT` is `Σ_j h_j ∂r/∂h_j` (Euler's theorem on the
homogeneous coefficients, no source, no extra factor of T — my first cut
multiplied by T), `residual_dh` is the coefficients' partial PLUS `du/dt`
(the residual sits at `t_n + h_n`), so `dr/dh_{n−1} = (residual_dT −
(residual_dh − u̇) h_n)/h_{n−1}`; the source's motion enters once —
`residual_dh`'s own `u̇ h_n`-part through this step's weight and `u̇ τ_n` for
the shift of the step's start.  ⚠ Counting `u̇ (τ + w)` on top of
`residual_dh` — twice — read the node after a landed event 153 % off; the
step-length partials alone were exact on an autonomous circuit (1.7e-7),
which located it.  After the fix the columns match central differences to
3.6e-8 at the period and 1e-7 at the event nodes on the PWM loop with the
window sub-grid (Euler backstop steps included — `residual_dh/dT` take the
ACTIVE integrator's coefficients).  The stage runs on gear's pair of
unknowns `(x_0, x_{−1}, θ)`.  Measured: gear staged 5.9e-3 / 4.0e-3 of the
swing at 100 / 200 points against 1.1e-2 / 4.4e-3 unstaged, its crossing
within 2e-4 T of radau's at 200 — modest, as trbdf2's: the second-order
off-phase error dominates on this fixture.  Radau is the method for these
circuits; gear no longer warns.

**Autonomous circuits.**  `z = [x_0, θ, T]` (the free-period solver reads
the period last), the period one more column of the event algebra (`d h_j /
d T = fraction_j` at fixed θ, the same stage algebra with the `u̇` term
zero), the phase row closing the system, `_free_period_solve` naming the
degenerate root as for the plain free-period solve; after convergence the
monodromy at fixed period and `_event_columns` as for the driven stage.
Two bookkeeping defects found by the FD check: the autonomous rebuild hands
N times for N fractions (the driven one N + 1), so the stages count steps as
`len(times) − 1` and take their base grid from `diff(times)`.  ⚠ FD
instrument: a θ step of 1e-6 across a 0.04 ns window is 3.5 % of it and
reads the first-edge columns 1e-4 off by truncation; at 1e-8 every column
is below 1e-7 (T 5.4e-8).

The fixture: a comparator relaxation oscillator — RC from a rail, a
`VSwitch` across C driven by the capacitor voltage through TWO RC lags
against a reference (one lag settles into a sliding equilibrium at the
threshold; two oscillate at 1.39 µs).  ⚠ With the 20 mV window the UNSTAGED
radau period was already fifth order (3.6e-6 → 3.2e-11 over 100 → 800
points): a 4 ns ramp is a third of a step and three collocation points
resolve it — the fixture had no defect to fix.  With a 0.2 mV window (0.04
ns on the slope), against a STAGED radau-1600 reference (the unstaged
3200-point solve is 12 ppm from it — an unstaged reference cannot judge
this):

    points    unstaged      staged
    100       +4.4e-3       -6.4e-4
    200       +2.0e-4       -1.9e-4
    400       -1.8e-3       -6.8e-5
    800       -7.8e-4       +8.1e-7       (relative period error)

The unstaged error is set by where the crossing falls in its step and does
not converge; the staged one lands all four edges at the reference's and
reaches 8e-7 at 800 points, a thousandfold.  What holds it at 100–400 is the
10 ns discharge after the switch closes, one step wide there — the same
lesson as the driven loop: the stage lands the crossing, the grid after it
is the fold's business.

**Not built:** the bordered Lyapunov closure (`covariance` on a staged
solve); the bordered PAC on an AUTONOMOUS staged solve (the deflated solve
would have to border the pole and the events together — `PAC.solve` keeps
the deflated, unbordered route there and says nothing yet).

### 2026-09-22 — Phase B: the bordered PPV (exact-verified), the bordered Lyapunov closure (analytic gate), the autonomous stage's `event_times` (Andreas: "Continue with the open items when the gate is finished")

**Bordered PPV.**  On a staged solve `ppv()` takes the left null vector of the
TOTAL monodromy `M + P_θ dθ/dx₀` (`_MtT/_Mt` in its bordered null solves) and
its samples carry the crossings' motion as costate injections `−ζ_k W_k` at
the event nodes, `ζ = Gt⁻ᵀ P_θᵀ v`, through `_ppv_propagate(inject=)` →
`matvec_transposed(inject=)` (`inject[j]` lands on the adjoint at node j, the
adjoint's `extra` convention).  Four candidate sample formulas were computed
from the stored columns (`events_ppv_candidates.py`): the plain reverse pass,
the constant-ζ injection, the TAIL-restricted Newton (events after node n
re-solved, node n's time moving with them) and the tail form corrected for
the node's own motion `+ τ_nᵀ dθ/dδ`.  The constant-ζ pass and the corrected
tail form agree to 1e-4 at every node — one reverse pass IS the phase
gradient at fixed time — and both satisfy `v_n·ẋ_n = 1` to 4e-4 along the
orbit.

**The reference is EXACT.**  The comparator relaxation oscillator is
piecewise linear with an ideal comparator: linear flows in each switch state
(`c` shorted through `Ron`, or `Roff`), states `(c, fb0, fb1)`, joined by
saltation matrices `I + (f⁺ − f⁻) hᵀ/(h·f⁻)` at the crossings of `fb1 − 2.5`
(`events_ppv_exact.py`; `_exact_relaxation_oscillator_ppv` in the tests).
Period 1.3918374 µs (staged radau-400 −9.6e-5, the lte_grid seed +2.6e-5),
multipliers 1, 0.0200, 4.4e-10.  Bordered samples against it: 0.05–0.4 % at
400 points, 0.08–0.33 % at 200, at every phase — before the first crossing,
just after a window, well after both.  The fixed-grid PPV of the SAME staged
solve: 140–230 % off on `fb1` before the first crossing, its `Mᵀv − v`
residual 1.5 (the fixed-grid map has no unit multiplier: |λ−1| = 0.99).
Inside a 0.04 ns window the discrete sample interpolates the jump and a
nearest-state comparison reads 146 % — meaningless there, excluded.

⚠ **The transient instrument and its lesson.**  The transient FD of the
phase response (perturb a node's state, 20 periods, read the crossing-time
shift) validated on the SOFT-window oscillator (0.1–1 % against the ordinary
`ppv()`), then read `c` at node 10 as +4.4e-8 s/V where the bordered PPV
said −7.4e-9 — and converged in its own step (T/1000 → T/16000: 4.34 →
4.43 → 4.43e-8), resolved the window with 24–99 steps, read the period to
4e-6 and a perturbation ALONG the flow to 1e-4.  Every consistency check
passed; only the exact reference exposed which side was wrong.  Its phase
was read at the `c` waveform's crossing of `(max + min)/2` of the perturbed
record — the perturbation moved the level.  Read at the comparator's own
crossing (`fb1 − ref`) the same transient agrees with the exact value to
0.45 % (the per-period crossing shifts −7.60e-8, −8.72e-9, −7.38e-9,
−7.35e-9 … converge with the 0.02 multiplier).  An instrument's reference
level must not come from the record it measures; and "converged in its own
step" plus "right along one direction" is not validation across the others.
Also: at ε = 1e-4 V the reading was pure noise (the crossing localisation
floor ~0.05 ns over 2ε) — an ε ladder (3e-3, 1e-2, 3e-2) is the floor test.

**Bordered Lyapunov closure (`PAC.covariance`).**  `_event_closure`: the
noise `w_j` moves the crossings, `dθ = −Gt⁻¹(G dx₀ + Σ_j d_j w_j)` with
`d_j[k] = W_k P_{nd_k←j+1}` (K dense reverse products of the per-step maps);
the closure is `K₀ = M_tot K₀ M_totᵀ + Q_tot`, `Q_tot = [I, −P_θ Gt⁻¹]
Cov([u; v]) [·]ᵀ` from `K₁` (the plain forward recursion), `E = Z_N`
(`Z_{j+1} = A_j Z_j + Q_j d_jᵀ`) and `D = Σ d_j Q_j d_jᵀ`.  Samples are at
FIXED times: the nodes move with the events (segment-proportional remap), so
`Pk_j^fixed = Pk_j − ẋ_j τ_jᵀ` with `τ_j = Σ_{i<j} ∂h_i/∂θ` and `ẋ_j` from
`_orbit_rate` (a three-node parabola, one-sided at and after a landed
event); `Cov_j = R_j K₀ R_jᵀ + K_j^fwd − Z_j Gt⁻ᵀ Pkᵀ − Pk Gt⁻¹ Z_jᵀ + Pk Gt⁻¹
D Gt⁻ᵀ Pkᵀ`, `R_j = P_{j←0} + Pk_j^fixed dθ/dx₀`; `j = 0` gives `K₀`, `j = N`
closes on it (6e-20).  `dθ` depends on the noise of every step, the future
ones included — the sample recursion is not causal step by step.

**Its analytic gate — the comparator-jitter sampler (`_jitter_sampler`).**  A
sawtooth (slope s₁ = 5 V/0.9 µs) crosses a NOISY threshold node (`Vref`
through 10 kΩ into 1 pF: kT/C = 4.14e-9 V²); the crossing opens a VSwitch
that disconnects a 10 pF hold capacitor from a SECOND ramp (s₂ = 2 s₁).  The
held value is `ramp2(t_c)`, `t_c` jitters by `−δn/s₁`, so `Var(hold) =
(s₂/s₁)² kT/C_n = 1.66e-8` — reachable only through the crossing's motion
(the switch has no noise model, the sources none).  Measured (radau, staged,
`Var(n)` reads 1.0005 kT/C):

| points | bordered | unbordered | no node-rate correction, `Var(saw)/kT/C_n` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 100 | 0.9991 | 4.08 | 0.225 |
| 200 | 0.9992 | 3.79 | 0.225 |
| 400 | 0.9992 | 3.29 | 0.225 |

The unbordered closure on the same staged solve is wrong by O(1): the landed
window step's own linearisation carries the threshold noise into the hold
with a gain the collocation points invent (3.9 for a physical 2).  The
bordered residual 8e-4 is flat in the count — the fixture's 100 ps tracking
lag, not the grid.  The node-rate correction is real: without it the
NOISELESS sawtooth source reads 0.225 kT/C_n at t = 0.7 T — exactly its
node's share of the moving segment, `((0.925 − 0.7)/(0.925 − 0.45))²`.
Built for the hosts whose per-step maps are the state maps (radau); trbdf2
borrows its gear twin, which carries no columns — warned, unbordered.

**Autonomous `event_times`.**  The autonomous stage stored its columns but
never added the landed crossings to `event_times` (the driven stage does);
now it does, and the exact-PPV test pins four fractions.

Tests: `test_covariance_on_a_staged_solve_borders_its_lyapunov_closure_with_the_moving_events`
(held variance within 5e-3, source silent, unbordered > 2.5, the rate
correction's 0.225 share), `test_the_ppv_on_a_staged_autonomous_solve_is_bordered_and_matches_the_exact_saltation_ppv`
(within 2e-2 of the exact at six nodes, fixed-grid > 30 % off, four
`event_times`).  Remaining in phase B: bordered PAC on an AUTONOMOUS staged
solve (deflation and events together).

### 2026-09-22 — Phase B, last item: the sideband response and adjoint row on a STAGED OSCILLATOR (bordered + deflated), responses at FIXED time, the deflation's conditioning, and what the driven numbers really were

**The autonomous bordered PAC.**  On a staged oscillator the bordered
system collapses onto the total map: with `dθ = (dθ/dx₀) y₀ + dθ_f`,
`dθ_f = −Gt⁻¹ W f_node` the source's own motion of the crossings,
`(I − α M_tot) y₀ = α (w + P_θ dθ_f)` — the deflated solve with the TOTAL
operator (`_deflated_solve` adds `P_θ dθ/dx₀` to its matvec, transposed
too) and that source; the adjoint row is the transpose: the costate of `y₀`
is `g + (dθ/dx₀)ᵀ g_θ`, and `ζ = Gt⁻ᵀ(g_θ + α P_θᵀ z)` is injected in a
second reverse pass as on the driven solve.  The plain deflated solve on
such a solve bordered the fixed-grid `M` (no unit multiplier: |λ−1| = 0.99)
with the total map's null vectors and read the period node 0.3–1.8× and
interior nodes up to 400× off the exact response.

**The reference is EXACT again** (`_exact_relaxation_oscillator_forced`,
`events_pac_exact.py`): the variational system `y' = A_s y + B e^{jω(t−t₀)}`
in each switch state by augmented matrix exponentials, `y⁺ = S y⁻` at the
crossings, `y(T) = e^{jωT} y(0)`.  Control: on the SOFT-window oscillator,
unstaged, the established deflated PAC agrees with it to 0.2–1.2 % at 0.3 f0
and 1.7 f0 (PAC's `y` is the complex response itself, no carrier factor); at
1.001 f0 a uniform 3.5× — the soft window's own 2.5e-3 period offset scaling
the pole distance: a near-harmonic comparison must sit at the same offset
from EACH model's own f0.

**Responses are at FIXED time — and so were never the driven numbers.**  With
the autonomous PAC exact at the period node, the interior nodes of
`time_response` were still O(1) off (0.17× at one node): `y_j += Pk_j dθ`
is the response of "node j", whose TIME moves with the crossings — the same
node-motion artefact the Lyapunov samples needed removing.  With
`Pk_j^fixed = Pk_j − ẋ_j τ_jᵀ` (`_fixed_time_event_columns`, shared by the
covariance closure, `PAC.solve` and the adjoint's `g_θ`) every node matches
the exact response to 0.1–0.2 % at 0.3 f0 and 1.7 f0 (the collapsed `c` node
in the 10 ns ON phase 5 %: the three-node rate stencil on 7 ns steps).  ⚠
The DRIVEN bordered PAC's instrument (the fixed-base FD on the ±ε staged
orbits) read node j of solves whose node times differ — it shared the
artefact with the old `time_response`, which is why they agreed to 4e-10.
Re-measured at fixed time (the FD corrected by the node motion `ẋ·Δt_j/2ε`):
the bordered response is exact (3e-10 / 4e-10) and the UNBORDERED one is
3.1 % / 4.9 % off (out / fb) at 60 points — NOT the 55 % / 5.7 % recorded
with `48b1b1b`; the 55 % was the artefact.  The bordering's gain on the
driven loop is real but modest; the O(1) cases are the staged oscillator's
sideband response and the covariance closure.

**The deflation's conditioning.**  `_deflated_solve` bordered with the raw
tangent (V/s, ~1e6 here) and PPV (s/V, ~1e-6): the bordered matrix's
condition number was 1.8e9 where `I − αM_tot` alone was 2.4; the forward
solve returned with a 5.5e-4 residual, the transposed one did not converge.
Border vectors are now unit vectors (`s` absorbs the scale).  Then the
recovery `y = w + s u/(1−α)` assumes EXACT null vectors, and on a staged
solve they are not (below): the recovered `y` misses the true operator by
`|λ−1|/|1−α|` — 5.5e-4 at 0.3 f0, 0.14 at 1.001 f0 — and forward/adjoint
are not dual-consistent (4.5e-4).  The deflated answer now seeds a plain
correction on its own residual wherever `|1−α| ≥ 1e-8` (kept only if the
residual falls): residuals 5e-15 at 0.3 f0, 1e-12 at 1.001 f0, forward and
adjoint dual-consistent.  ⚠ This makes the answer the DISCRETE operator's
own: near a harmonic it carries the multiplier's displacement (15.7 % off
the exact at 1.001 f0, `|λ−1|/|1−α| = 0.13`), where the unrefined recovery,
carrying the pole analytically at α = 1, happened to read 0.2 % — but is not
dual-consistent and has its own O(h/|1−α|) split error.  Unstaged
oscillators: the residual is already at the tolerance, nothing changes.

**E8 — the staged map's unit multiplier is first order.**  Measured
(`staged_multiplier_ladder.py`, radau): unstaged SOFT oscillator `|λ₀−1|` =
4.7e-3 / 1.9e-6 / 5.7e-12 / 5.7e-15 at 100 / 200 / 400 / 800 points;
STAGED sharp: 2.3e-3 / 9.9e-4 / 4.2e-4 / 1.2e-4 — ratios 2.4 / 2.3 / 3.4.
And the staged period against the EXACT ideal-comparator one: −6.7e-4 /
−2.2e-4 / −9.6e-5 / −2.7e-5 — the earlier "+8.1e-7 at 800" was against a
staged 1600 reference that carries the same slow convergence.  ⚠ The
comparison is to an IDEAL comparator; the 0.2 mV window itself is O(3e-5)
of the period, so the tail of the period ladder may be the window, but the
multiplier's 1.2e-4 at 800 is not.  Candidates: the fixed 8-step window
sub-grid, the landing snap, the ramp.  First job: a windowed exact model
(the window as a third piece integrated at 1e-12) — roadmap E8.

Tests: `test_the_sideband_response_on_a_staged_oscillator_is_bordered_deflated_and_matches_the_exact_forced_response`
(0.3 / 1.7 f0 within 5e-3 at fixed time outside the ON phase, 1.001 f0 below
0.25 with its cause, unbordered > 0.2 or non-convergent, adjoint
dual-consistent to 1e-8); the driven PAC test re-pinned at fixed time
(unbordered 0.031 / 0.049).

**E8 explained the same afternoon — it is the switch, not the stage.**  The
window sub-grid count 8 → 64 moves nothing (`|λ₀−1|` 9.88e-4, period
−2.19e-4 at 200 points, identically).  A compactly supported C² smoothstep
inside `[Voff, Von]` (monkeypatched `i` and `G`, nothing else): `|λ₀−1|` =
3.1e-13 / 3.2e-14 / 1.1e-14 at 100 / 200 / 400 points, staged period
−8.8e-7 / −1.0e-7 / −8.1e-8 of the exact ideal-comparator period — fifth
order, at 100 points.  `VSwitch`'s `(tanh(2 x_norm) + 1)/2` has `x_norm =
±0.5` at the declared edges: 12 % of the swing on each side sits outside the
window, in the coarse steps between events, and that is the first-order
error in every consumer.  A modelling decision (compact transition; wider
declared events; both) — recorded in roadmap E8, not changed.

**The deflation test re-pinned.**  `test_the_deflated_solve_is_capped_by_the_TANGENT_not_by_the_PPV`
recorded that an error in the border column (the tangent) passes linearly
into the answer; with the plain-operator refinement it no longer does above
the gate: at 1e-6 f0 off the carrier both border vectors move the answer by
≤ 5e-10 (refined; the answer is the operator's own), at 1e-10 f0 (below the
gate) the old asymmetry stands (row 1e-14, column 2.1e-4 at ε = 1e-4).  The
test now pins both regimes.

### 2026-09-22 — `VSwitch`: the compact transition (Andreas: "Implement 1")

The tanh `(tanh(2 x_norm) + 1)/2` had `x_norm = ±0.5` at `Von`/`Voff`: the
switch was 12 % / 88 % of the way through its transition AT the parameters
described as the on and off states, with tails to ±2.5 windows — outside the
window a staged PSS lands and refines, in the coarse steps between events.
E8 measured the consequence (period and unit multiplier first order, the
window sub-grid irrelevant) and a monkeypatched compact transition removed
it (period within 8.8e-7 of the exact at 100 points, multiplier 1e-14).
Now the element: a C² smoothstep `t³(10 − 15t + 6t²)` on `[Voff, Von]`,
exactly `Goff` below and `Gon` above, clamped with `maximum` alone so the
autodiff toolkit differentiates the same expression; the numeric Jacobian
follows.  ⚠ Every `VSwitch` result moves: at the tail level away from the
edges, by 12 % of the swing AT an edge (the unit test's switch read at
`Von` is now `Ron` exactly, 1.0 Ω for the tanh's 1.135 Ω).  The unit multiplier of the FIXED-GRID map of a landed solve is now 1.1e-7 / 6.6e-10 / 6.7e-10 at 100 / 200 / 400 points (the total's 3e-12 .. 4e-15).

**⚠ What the compact transition did to the phase-B "unbordered is O(1)
wrong" numbers — all seven re-pinned.**  Every unbordered pin in the
staged-events tests failed the same way: the fixed-grid landed solve became
nearly right.  With the transition inside the landed window the fixed-grid
map through the resolved window already carries the switching's response; a
perturbation only pushes a sliver of the transition past the edges into a
coarse step, and that sliver — the whole tail with the tanh — is what the
bordering corrects.  Measured (compact transition; the tanh numbers in
brackets):

| consumer | bordered | unbordered |
|---|---|---|
| driven PWM loop, PAC at fixed time, 60 pts (out / fb) | 7e-10 / 1e-9 | 1.1 % / 2.5 % (3.1 % / 4.9 %; "55 %" was the artefact) |
| driven adjoint row | 1e-10 | 0.55 % (several %) |
| driven total monodromy, `fb` column | 1e-5 | `Mx` 8 % off (109 %) |
| gear's stage on the loop, 200 pts | 4.3e-3 | 3.7e-3 unstaged — the stage buys nothing (was 5.9e-3 vs 1.1e-2) |
| oscillator PPV vs exact, 200 pts | 4.7e-4 | 4.7e-4 (140 % off) |
| oscillator PAC vs exact, 0.3 / 1.7 / 1.001 f0 | 2.3e-3 / 7.4e-4 / 3.2e-3 | 8.7e-4 / 6.5e-4 / 1.3e-3 (0.3–400×) |
| jitter sampler covariance, 100 pts | 0.999 | 0.9996 (4.08×) |

The bordered machinery is the exact linearisation of the staged solve and
stays so; its numerical weight on these fixtures is now per-cent to
sub-per-mille, and the phase-B log entries above should be read with that
correction: the O(1) they measured was the tanh's tails, resolved by neither
the fixed grid nor the window.  Gear's and trbdf2's stages bring nothing on
the loop (their own off-phase error is what is left); radau is the method.

**E8's floor named (after `b5051d4`).**  The windowed exact period (the
smoothstep switch integrated at 1e-12, shooting on the crossing) is
−7.997e-8 off the ideal comparator's — the staged solve's −8.0e-8 was the
window.  Against the windowed exact: −7.3e-7 / −1.85e-8 / −9.0e-10 /
−2.5e-10 at 100 / 200 / 400 / 800 radau points.  `VSwitch` is the only
`state_events` declarer; the other `tanh`s in the package are limiters.

### 2026-09-22 — E7: `Transient` lands state events — built, and measured to buy jitter removal, not order

`_solve` cuts an accepted step to a declared crossing (secant on the
fraction, re-solved as a rejected step is, ≤ 4 cuts to 1e-3 of the step;
2.1 per landing) and restarts the history there; `state_events=True` by
default, `event_times`, two statistics.  ⚠ Against the windowed exact
period (gear, 25 periods) landing leaves the MEAN error where the
integrator's own order puts it — +5.4e-3 / 1.4e-3 / 3.7e-4 / 9.1e-5
unlanded vs +6.7e-3 / 1.8e-3 / 4.2e-4 / 9.6e-5 landed at reltol 1e-4 … 1e-7,
restart or not — and removes the period-to-period spread 10–50× (1.3e-4 →
1.5e-5 at 1e-6).  With the compact transition the LTE controller localises
the crossing to the tolerance on its own; the "first order at a straddled
crossing" the entry was written against was the tanh's.  Trap's −1.1e-5
mean hid in a 1.2e-3 spread (now +1.9e-4 ± 1.3e-5); radau (the RK loop,
not wired) is at −1.7e-8 unlanded.  Land-to-step tolerance: 1e-3 / 4 cuts
gives the same spread as 1e-6 / 6 at 2.1 cuts instead of 2.6; one cut
alone leaves the spread unchanged.

**E7 in the Runge-Kutta loop (Andreas: "wire _run_rk_adaptive …").**  The
same secant cut in `_run_rk_adaptive`, re-solved from the same state as the
loop's own rejections; nothing to restart.  Radau: spread 4.8e-6 → 8.2e-7
at reltol 1e-4 and 3.7e-8 → 1.9e-9 at 1e-6, mean unchanged (−1.0e-6 /
−1.7e-8 → −1.3e-6 / −9.1e-9), steps 1213 / 2681 → 2825 / 3797; trbdf2
mean +6.0e-4 / +3.3e-5 either way, spread 9.6e-5 / 8.9e-7 → 6.9e-5 /
1.5e-6.  `state_events=True` is no longer a silent no-op for the one-step
integrators; `_solve_coupled` remains unwired.

**E7 in the coupled-LTE loop (Andreas: "After gate fix `_solve_coupled` is
the one loop still unwired").**  The same cut before the commit, the coupled
`(x, h)` solve HOLDING the handed step (`hold_h`) so its own step unknown
cannot walk off the crossing; `_init_state_events(n)` for both entry points.
Here landing buys accuracy, not only consistency — the coupled controller's
step unknown walked through the crossing badly: gear, 14 periods, reltol
1e-4 / 1e-6, mean +2.1e-2 / +1.4e-3 unlanded → +3.6e-3 / +1.9e-4 landed,
spread 2.4e-2 / 2.7e-3 → 3.3e-4 / 2.7e-6, edges to 1–4 % of the window,
steps 999 / 3823 → 3646 / 6406.  Every stepping loop lands events now.

### 2026-09-22 — `_orbit_rate` is the DAE's derivative (Andreas: "Do the _orbit_rate three-node stencil")

`C(x) ẋ = −(i(x) + u(t))` on the differential rows, `G(x) ẋ = −u̇` on the
algebraic ones (zero rows of `C`), one solve per node.  Against the exact
piecewise-linear rate on the staged comparator oscillator (200 points):
1e-15 at every node outside the windows; the stencil 10.6 % at worst, 0.45 %
at the ON-phase node the staged-oscillator sideband test had excluded — now
included.  Driven sanity on the jitter sampler: the sawtooth's rate is its
slope to 7 digits, the held capacitor's its `Roff` leak.  The stencil stays
as the fallback for a singular split (index above one), warned.

### 2026-09-22 — the three items after E7 (Andreas: "Do 2 then 1 then 3, do not run the full gate before all three are done")

**Item 2 — the Runge-Kutta transient loop lands source corners.**
`_run_rk_adaptive` had no breakpoint handling: radau/trbdf2 in the transient
stepped over a `VPulse`'s corners where `_solve` cuts its step to

7.1e-5 / 5.1e-7 → 1.3e-8 / 8.1e-10 at reltol 1e-4 / 1e-6, with fewer steps
(109 → 79, 144 → 106); trbdf2 unchanged at its own second order (2.0e-4,
1.1e-5); gear's loop always landed (8.9e-3 / 5.9e-4 without).  ⚠ My first
instrument compared with an IDEAL step and read a flat 1.5e-4 for every
integrator at every tolerance — the ramp's own response, not the
integrators'; the shape (flat in reltol, common to all) named it.
Test: `test_the_runge_kutta_transient_loop_lands_source_corners`.

**Item 1 — the oscillator consumers read the total map on a staged solve.**
`floquet_modes` diagonalised the fixed-grid map and sampled its modes
without the event nodes' costate injections; `oscillator_covariance`
bordered `I − M⊗M` with the fixed-grid `M` while its `u`, `v` were the
total's.  Now `M + P_θ dθ/dx₀` in `floquet_modes`, the injections through
`_event_costate_injection` (factored out of `ppv`), `oscillator_covariance`

(staged comparator oscillator, compact switch, 200 points): multipliers
1.998277e-2 → 1.998273e-2 (exact 1.998264e-2); the bordered residual
1.06e-7 → 5.1e-15; the second mode's `Cᵀq` along the orbit matches the exact
saltation propagation's direction to 6 digits with or without the
injections — the fixed-grid map through the landed window carries the
switching, the injections are the per-mille sliver.  ⚠ The first comparison
read cos 0.97 / 0.96 at nodes before and after the crossings: `q` is the
EQUATION-ROW adjoint and the state-space direction is `Cᵀq` (as `ppv()`'s
docstring says) — the transform, not the samples.  Test:
`test_the_oscillator_consumers_read_the_total_map_on_a_staged_solve`.

**Item 3 — gear's stage stores its event columns; the bordered PAC takes
gear's pair width.**  The gear stage solved its bordered Newton but stored
no columns, so every bordered consumer ran unbordered on a staged gear
solve.  Now one more traversal on the ramped grid captures every node:
`P_nodes[j]` (m × 2m: a node's state depends on `(x₀, x₋₁)`), `Pk_nodes`,
`P_end` (2m × K from `Pk_last`, `Pk_prev`), `G`, `Gt`, `dθ/dx₀`, and the
total monodromy as the pair map plus the saltation; `PAC.solve`'s bordered
