against radau's bordered response (exact against the fixed-time FD): gear
bordered 5.6e-4 / 2.5e-4 (out / fb), gear UNBORDERED 10.6 % — a two-step
map through the landed window does not carry the switching as radau's
one-step map does, so on gear the bordering is two orders of magnitude,
not a sliver; the pair-total multipliers 0.98106 / 0.68206 vs radau's
0.98105 / 0.68203.  The covariance closure (multivalue pieces) and the
adjoint row (kind gate) stay unbordered on gear — warned, plain.  Test:
`test_gears_stage_stores_its_event_columns_and_its_bordered_pac_matches_radaus`.

### 2026-09-23 — the fold owns its resolution at a source corner (found by the gate, caused by item 2)

The combined subset failed one test —
`test_the_period_quadrature_breaks_its_spline_at_landed_events…` — and the
bisect put it on item 2 alone.  Not the solver: the FOLD.  That test builds
its PSS grid from `lte_grid`, which folds the transient's own steps.  Before
item 2 radau stepped OVER each pulse corner and its rejections left a cluster
of tiny steps after every edge (0.0006, 0.0009, 0.0015 of the period) that
the fold inherited and that resolved the RC's decay there.  With the corners
landed, radau — L-stable — damps that decay with a quiet estimate and 0.03 T
steps, then doubles through the flat hold phase to 0.23 T.  Accurate for the
transient; useless as a period grid: the orbit read 4.3e-3 against 4.6e-4,
H1 4.5e-2 (pin 2e-3) and the periodic-spline weights reached −0.24 / +0.36
with H2 115 %.  **The accidental resolution was load-bearing.**

Fixed in `_fold_periods`: the step density is funnelled towards every source
corner (a step at phase distance `s` from a corner is at most `s`, never
below one bin) and capped at `FOLD_MAX_STEP = T/16`.  Measured on that grid:
the cap alone takes H2 1.15 → 3.4e-3, the funnel takes H1 4.5e-2 → 1.1e-3
(2.2e-4 from a sixteenth of the edge width).  ⚠ Two repairs that did NOT
work, recorded because each sounded right: a doubling ramp after each corner
on the fold's own points (H1 4.5e-2 → 4.0e-2 — it adds points where the
walk already had them) and a growth-ratio limiter around the corners (no
change at all — the fold's ratios were already ≤ 2; the defect was the
ABSOLUTE step, not the grading).

### 2026-09-23 — crossing jitter as a feature; the bordered consumers on gear; the sampled series verified

**`PAC.event_jitter`** (item 1 of "what next"): the crossings' noise-driven
motion was already inside the bordered Lyapunov closure — `Cov(dθ) = dθ/dx₀
K₀ dθ/dx₀ᵀ + Gt⁻¹ D Gt⁻ᵀ`, the stationary state plus this period's
injections — and is now returned as σ in seconds per crossing.  Gate:
`_jitter_sampler`'s turn-off crossing has the analytic σ = √(kT/C_n)/s₁ =
11.5844 ps; measured 11.5873 ps, 1.0002, FLAT at 100 / 200 / 400 points.
The reset edges read 0.6437 ps (the threshold's own faster slope).  An
oscillator is refused: its crossings diffuse with its phase.

**Gear's bordered consumers** (item 2): the adjoint row now does the same
Schur elimination on gear's PAIR map with the event rows' term as a second
injected reverse pass, and `_event_closure` builds its noise-sensitivity
rows at the MAP's width.  The adjoint row on the PWM loop is dual-consistent
with the bordered forward solve to 1.7e-14 (l = 0) and 7e-14 (l = 1) where
the unbordered row is 15 % / 10 % off.  The covariance closure in pair form:
gear's held variance tracks its OWN threshold variance to 3 % / 0.4 % / 0.2 %
at 100 / 400 / 800 points.  ⚠ The absolute levels there — 0.60 / 0.85 / 0.92
of the analytic — are gear's known covariance floor and not the bordering:
`Var(n)` is the threshold's own kT/C with no event in it and reads the same
whether staged or not (its RC is 10 ns against a 10 ns step at N = 100;
radau reads 1.0004 throughout).  ⚠ And on this fixture the hold is saved by
the LANDED GRID, not by the bordering: with the grid landed and the columns
withheld gear reads 0.607 against 0.6016 — 1 % — while an UNSTAGED gear
solve reads 537× the analytic.

**`sampled_noise` on staged solves** (item 3): it now uses the total map,
reads its sample at fixed time and carries the crossings' injections in a
third reverse pass (`_stage_pass` takes a dict of seeds).  The verification
is sharp because `_jitter_sampler`'s held node has no noise source of its
own: every part of its sample-series variance arrives through the crossing.
Measured over `[1e-3, 0.5] f0` at `t = 0.7 T`: 1.373e-8 / 1.500e-8 at 100 /
200 points against the analytic 1.657e-8 (0.83, 0.91 — converging with the
band).  The bordering itself is worth 1.15e-6 of that on radau and 8.2e-3 on
gear, whose two-step map does not carry the switching; measured both ways,
and the code stays because the consumers now agree on one linearisation.

**⚠ And the gate's second failure was the TEST'S INSTRUMENT.**  The
staged-oscillator sideband test compared each node's response with the exact
one as `|got − ex| / |ex|`, per node and per component.  It had passed at
7.4e-4 and now read 7.4e-3 — from changes that moved nothing else.  At
t/T = 0.35 the `fb0` component passes through a near-null (1.3e-3 against
its own orbit maximum of 0.24), so the ratio there measures where the null
sits, not the solve; its ABSOLUTE error was 9.8e-6, the same 1e-5 as at every
other node.  Re-pinned on each component's own orbit maximum: bordered
9.8e-5 / 2.5e-4 / 1.5e-4 at 0.3 / 1.7 / 1.001 f0, unbordered the same to four
digits.  The campaign's notes already carried this for the PPV ("a relative
error against a zero-crossing quantity is not agreement") and the test walked
into it anyway, so it is now recorded for response waveforms too.

### 2026-09-23 — E9: the refactor, gated by a bit-identity snapshot; and the gate made cheap

**The instrument.**  Not the tests' tolerances: a snapshot harness dumping 65
arrays of real consumer output (monodromies, PAC responses and event shifts,
adjoint rows, PPV samples, Floquet modes, oscillator covariance and spectrum,
covariance samples, event jitter σ, sampled variances, the transient's times
and event times under gear/radau/coupled, the fold) — run twice on HEAD first
(bit-identical, so its floor is 0) and after each step at zero tolerance.
Every source step: worst difference 0.0.

**What changed.**  `EventColumns` (a dict subclass, so every `ev['P_end']`
read and the tests' `_event_columns = None` controls keep working) carries
the derivations the consumers had re-typed: the total map (4 copies), the
costate injection (4), the forced shift, the collapsed ζ, the bordered
adjoint's Schur elimination; the adjoint row's three branches are one.  The
three state-event stages share crossing detection, the event rows and the
finish (the ramped-grid and total-monodromy notes moved with the code).  The
transient's three stepping loops share one state-event hook, one breakpoint
guard and one result builder.  The tests' exact oscillator model is one cached
builder (one shooting solve for every use).  `_orbit_rate_stencil` got a test
that fires it.

**Two defects found by the refactor, both mine:**  (1) the DAE orbit rate read
0 at node 0 for an exact 6283 V/s — `VSin` clamps `t − td` at zero (SPICE's
transient rule), so at t = td = 0 it returns the LEFT derivative where the
periodic steady state has the right one; node 0 now evaluates as node N.
Invisible to every consumer (each multiplies the rate at node 0 by a zero).
(2) E7's landing constants sat above `Transient`'s docstring, so
`Transient.__doc__` was `None`; a scan of the four core modules finds no other.

**The gate.**  Its own timing record: 2131 s wall for 8460 test-seconds on
`-n 4`, 85 % in `test_analysis_shooting.py`.  `pytest.ini` now defaults to
`-n 8`.  B16 was 923 s: a profile put 790 s in two twin re-solves from an
Euler orbit the test asserts are refused — the twin inherits the caller's 300
iterations and both now refuse by NOT converging, running the whole budget
times the retry ladder (971 + 968 traversals); at 20 the refusals take 57 s
and B16 75 s.  Six events tests each solved the same 72.6 s `lte_grid` for a
seed; the exact model gives it in 0.000 s (staged periods agree to 5e-9, the
tests pass unchanged, 21–32 s each instead of 96–114 s).  Left as decisions:
a product-side cap on the twin's budget; merging `_traverse_dirk` /
`_traverse_full` (they share scaffolding, not algebra — a stage-solver
abstraction on the hottest path, not a de-duplication).

### 2026-09-23 — after E9: the twin capped, the phase pin kept raw on measurement, the stage family merged (Andreas: "For 1 Cap iteration count to 40 by default and give a warning if it is hit. For 2 do it. … For 3 implement the fix")

**1. The twin's budget.**  `PSS.TWIN_MAXITER = 40`: a monodromy twin's
re-solve takes `min(caller's maxiterations, 40)` (never below 20) instead of
inheriting the caller's, and when that cap was binding and the twin still did
not converge it warns — naming the cap and the caller's budget — before the
refusal it always raised.  A twin is a polish from the converged orbit (4–13
iterations from a good seed on B16's van der Pol); the 300 it inherited is what
made B16's two refusals cost 790 s.  ⚠ The warning says "did not converge
within its capped budget", not "hit the cap": `fsolve` records no iteration
count, so an early failure under the cap cannot be told from a stall at it.
Test: `test_the_monodromy_twin_is_capped_and_warns_when_it_hits_the_cap`
(trap's twin converges with no warning; Euler's with the cap patched to 6
warns, then refuses).

**3. The frozen phase pin: the unit-mixing fix built, measured, and NOT
shipped (Andreas: "Keep raw rule").**  The pin picks
`argmax |x₂ − x₁|` over the first step, raw — volts and amps in one argmax
(the peer's observation).  Scaling each coordinate by its own orbit swing is
the unit-free rule, and it did what it promises on an LC tank (1 nF / 1 µH:
the bordered Jacobian's condition 120–160 raw, 3.75–9.3 scaled).  But on the
comparator relaxation oscillator, from ten seed phases on the exact orbit
(unstaged, 40 iterations), the raw rule converged 7/10 and the scaled one
4/10: raw pinned coordinate 1 at nine phases, scaled moved the pin to 2 or 3
at every phase, and the two fail at different phases (not diagnosed further —
the decision was taken on these counts).  Conditioning is not convergence; the
raw rule stays, the measurement is written at the rule, and `PSS.phase_k` now
records which coordinate was pinned.  Test:
`test_the_frozen_phase_pin_is_the_raw_rule_kept_on_measurement` (the LC
converges under gear/radau/trbdf2 with the pin on the capacitor's node).

**2. The stage family merged — and the E9 note that called it "scaffolding,
not algebra" was wrong.**  The coupled (Radau) and sequential (TR-BDF2,
ESDIRK) copies differed only in how ONE step's stage system is factored and
solved; everything else — the forcings, the reverse pass's use of the stage
costates, the source couplings, the injections — was the same algebra typed
twice.  One `_StageStep` per step now carries the factorisation (`lu` for the
coupled block, `Kf` per stage with `None` at the explicit first stage) and
`solve` / `adjoint` / `sources` / `source_adjoint` / `source_response`; every
replay exists once: `_traverse_stage` (the dense traversal and the Newton's
two closures), `_traverse_factored_stage` / `factored_period_stage`, the two
mat-vecs, the forced replay and its transpose, the sideband fold, the Lyapunov
pieces, `PAC._stage_pass`, the stage injection and the stage times.  Twenty
methods, four Newton closures and nine `fp.kind` dispatch pairs became one of
each; `shooting.py` is 472 lines shorter (items 1–3 together).  `kind` still
says `'full'`/`'dirk'` (which factorisation), so no caller changed meaning.

**The instrument** was the E9 snapshot, widened: the 65-array Radau/Gear
events set plus 42 arrays under TR-BDF2 and ESDIRK43 (PWM staged PAC, adjoint
rows, covariance samples, sampled variance; an unstaged RC's PAC and
covariance; van der Pol's PPV, Floquet modes, PAC and oscillator covariance),
both bit-identical HEAD-to-HEAD.  The dense traversal's merge alone: 107/107
arrays bit-identical.  The whole family: 45/65 and 38/42 bit-identical, the
rest moved — the PAC transfers, adjoint rows, jitter and covariances, up to
1.2e-11 relative (radau's PWM PAC transfer on its staged grid), 4.6e-16 on the
DIRK set.  To tell a rounding convention from a defect, the snapshot was
re-run with the three old conventions patched back in: **107/107
bit-identical.**  So the merge is exact up to exactly these three:

1. the coupled copy's source paths (forced replay, its transpose, the
   sideband fold, the stage pass, the stage-source response) used `h =
   t_{j+1} − t_j` where its factorisation used `h_j`; on a non-uniform grid
   (`times = cumsum(hs)`) they differ in the last bits.  Now every path uses
   the factorisation's own `h`, as the sequential copy always did; uniform
   grids are unchanged;
2. the sequential forced replay added the stage source after the stage
   coupling (`C y − h Σ A G d + src`); now before, as the traversal's
   forcings always were;
3. the sequential stage-source response subtracted the coupling term by term
   and solved column by column; now one sum and one block solve.

**Two latent inconsistencies closed on the way.**  The coupled replays read
the tableau of `par.method`, not of the factored period they replay — wrong
for a `factored_period_stage(method=…)` that differs, unreachable from the
public surfaces (they all build it with `par.method`); each step now carries
its own `A`, `b`, `c`.  And an explicit stage other than the first was refused
only by the forward mat-vec (the dense traversal carried it through
silently); it is refused where the step is built.

**Cost:** measured by alternating HEAD, merged and HEAD on the PWM loop.  The
staged solves are unchanged within the box's drift (radau 9.04 / 9.29 / 9.29
s, trbdf2 10.78 / 11.09 / 11.05, esdirk43 79.0 / 81.7 / 80.4).  The PAC +
adjoint row pays the step object's per-step Python dispatch: +2.0–3.2 %
against the adjacent HEAD run, BLAS single-threaded (radau 0.333 → 0.343 s,
trbdf2 0.625 → 0.645 s; HEAD against itself drifted 6 % between runs).  The
first version paid 5 %, from a function-local `import scipy.linalg` plus
`np.iscomplexobj` in the per-step solve wrapper (6 % of the row's profile) and
a source-coupling loop testing every tableau entry instead of stopping at the
diagonal.  Both are fixed, and the output is bit-identical to before the fix.
Targeted subset (61 tests: stage methods, factored periods, transposes,
twin, phase pin, orbit rate) passed.

### 2026-09-23 — `max_dv_step` / `max_di_step` bind under every integrator (Andreas: "Push, do the loop refactor after doing the max_dv_step fix")

Found while surveying the transient's three stepping loops for Andreas's
one-path question.  The excursion check (a veto on a step the error test
passed, with a proportional `0.9/ratio` retry) was typed out in the LMM loop
and again in the coupled loop, and `_run_rk_adaptive` never had it, so every
Runge-Kutta and GLM method ignored both knobs silently.  Measured on a pulsed
RC with `'auto'` (bound 2π/64 × 1 V = 0.098 V): gear and trap 70 → 86
steps, max|Δv| 0.097; radau 69 → 69 at 0.0996 (above the bound); trbdf2
71 → 71.  Now one `Transient._excursion_ratio` serves all three loops (the two
copies were identical: the LMM and coupled paths are unchanged), and the RK
loop runs it on a step its estimate accepted: radau 85 steps at 0.0968, trbdf2
86.  trbdf2's OUTPUT was already under the bound; the extra steps are the
source node's 1 V edge, which the node-row check covers under every method.

⚠ I first reported this as "the default method ignores it".  `Transient`'s
default integrator is Gear2 (radau is PSS's default), so a default transient
was never affected; an explicitly chosen RK or GLM method was.

Test: `test_max_dv_step_binds_under_every_integrator` — 8 integrators on the
resistive amplifier of the parity test, the blind premise asserted per method
(a knob that cannot bind measures nothing).  On the pre-fix code it fails
exactly the five RK/GLM cases and passes the three LMM ones.
